| DL-09 | REFRACTIVE_RADIANCE_SCALING.md §10.2 | No interior-segment basic-radiance factor: inside a medium whose `ior` painter varies with POSITION, `L / n^2` is conserved ALONG the ray as well as across interfaces, but RISE applies `(eta_before/eta_after)^2` only at scatter events — so a contribution gathered at an interior vertex carries the entry point's index instead of the gather point's. A second, independent inconsistency at the same DielectricSPF call site (the exit-hit Snell trace) widens the row's scope. | OPEN-confirmed; RE-OPENED 2026-09-14 after the round-1 "fix" (`ae475295`) was found to be physically wrong and REVERTED — re-scoped round 3, same day, after a further review found the round-2 recipe's literal reading is ALSO wrong (a mirror-image regression) and a second inconsistency at the same site — read the corrected analysis before re-attempting | **The round-1 diagnosis was wrong and its fix has been reverted.** It substituted the SPF's fresh EXIT-hit ior for `RadianceEtaScale`'s stale `before.top()` (via a new `ScatteredRay::etaBeforeOverride`). But `before.top()` being stale is what makes RISE's full-trip account CORRECT today: for camera(air) -> A(`n_A`) -> B(`n_B`) -> air the physics is `(1/n_A)^2 * (n_A/n_B)^2 * (n_B/1)^2 = 1`, the middle term is the interior-segment factor RISE never applies, and the stale `n_A` at the exit read is exactly that missing term folded into the true exit factor. Substituting `n_B` without adding the interior factor turns the net 1 into `(n_B/n_A)^2` — the round-1 test enshrined a **2.05x brightening of an emitter behind a passive, lossless slab** (it asserted 0.652746 where the physics is `L*T1*T2 = 0.289909`; the value it called "buggy", 0.290109, was the right answer). `tests/RadianceEtaScaleGradedIndexTest.cpp` was rewritten to assert the PHYSICS (10/0: PT and BDPT both 0.290109, ratio-to-physics 1.00069, ratio-to-the-2.25x-value 0.44475) and now pins the invariant against exactly that regression; `RefractiveRadianceScalingTest` re-verified 38/0, byte-identical to `ddf05c6c`. **The genuine residual** is the missing interior-segment factor itself, which only shows where the cancelling exit event never happens: a gather at an INTERIOR vertex C (an NEE connection or a bounce before the walk exits the graded object) carries `(1/n_A)^2` where physics wants `(1/n_C)^2` — an error of `(n_C/n_A)^2` — and likewise for a walk seeded INSIDE a graded medium (`IORStackSeeding::SeedFromPoint` does NOT record the index at the seed point itself; it fires a probe ray from the seed point and records the index at the probe's FIRST SURFACE HIT on the containing object — a point that can be arbitrarily far from the seed point on a graded `ior` object; the first segment out from the seed pays no interior factor either way). **Round 3 correction (same day): the round-2 recipe text above, "on the walk, not at the SPF exit read", is itself wrong if read as "leave the exit read untouched."** The interior factor and a switch of the EXIT crossing's own read (stale `before.top()` -> the SPF's freshly re-fetched exit value) must land TOGETHER: applying the interior factor `(n_A/n_B)^2` alone, with the exit read still stale, double-charges the segment and gives `(1/n_A)^2 * (n_A/n_B)^2 * (n_A/1)^2 = (n_A/n_B)^2` — 0.444 at this row's 1.2/1.8 fixture, the MIRROR IMAGE of the reverted round-1 bug (reciprocals of each other: `(n_B/n_A)^2` vs `(n_A/n_B)^2`) — and `RadianceEtaScaleGradedIndexTest`'s through-slab pin CANNOT tell a half-applied fix from a correct one, because both rows there are pure through-transmission and `(1/n_A)^2*(n_A/n_B)^2*(n_B/1)^2 = 1` regardless of which of the two changes landed, as long as BOTH did. Only an interior-vertex gather (which never reaches the exit read) can distinguish them. **Prerequisite the fix needs and does not have:** `n_C` — the painter's value AT an interior gather vertex — is UNDEFINED for most `IScalarPainter` forms; `GetValuesAt`/`GetValueAtNM` take a `RayIntersectionGeometric`, a SURFACE hit record (UV, geometric normal, etc.), and an interior point never generated one. Only a world-position-only form (`ExpressionPainter` reading `P`, no `u`/`v`) is queryable there; UV-driven forms (`TextureScalarPainter`, `Function2DScalarPainter`, a curve composed with a UV lookup) are not. **Second inconsistency, same site, widens the row (M -> L):** `DielectricSPF::GenerateScatteredRay`'s exit branch computes the exit ray's DIRECTION and TIR classification via `Optics::CalculateRefractedRay(-ri.onb.w(), rIndex=n_B (fresh at the exit hit), exitIOR, refracted)`, while the walk's own IOR-stack model still tracks `n_A` (the entry-time value) as the medium the ray has been travelling in, because nothing updates it along the walk — the identical gap, now visible in the Snell trace rather than in radiance accounting. At this row's 1.2 -> 1.8 fixture: the TRACED medium's (`n_A`=1.2) critical angle is 56.4 deg, but the exit test Snell-checks against `n_B`=1.8 (critical angle 33.7 deg), so an incidence angle between 33.7 deg and 56.4 deg is classified TIR by the SPF's own fresh-value test even though the walk's own tracked account never put the ray in a medium dense enough to trap it there. Fix direction (unchanged in substance, corrected in the pairing): an interior-segment factor `(n_prev/n_C)^2` applied ALONG the walk each time it queries the painter at a new interior point without a scatter event to hang it on, paired with switching `RadianceEtaScale`'s exit-crossing read to the fresh value, restricted (for now) to world-position-only `ior` painters, AND a matching fix to `DielectricSPF`'s exit-hit Snell trace so its direction/TIR classification agrees with whatever medium the walk's corrected model tracks at that point — exactly 1 (and geometrically unchanged) for every uniform-`ior` scene. Recipe: take `RadianceEtaScaleGradedIndexTest`'s graded slab, add an interior gather (a diffuse target inside it, or lower `scattering` so the walk scatters non-deltaically at an interior vertex), and compare against a piecewise-uniform reference — the same object as N concentric shells of CONSTANT `ior` stepping `n_A` -> `n_B`, where every shell boundary is a real interface and the per-crossing factor is correct by construction. The graded render must converge to the multi-shell one as N grows; today it will not. Implement the interior factor and the exit-read switch in the SAME change (a fix that lands only one, in either direction, must fail an interior-gather test even though it passes the existing through-slab one) and extend the same pass to the DielectricSPF exit-hit Snell trace. | L | physics-bias | internal (not user-visible today: no in-tree scene binds a position-dependent `ior` — `scenes/Tests/GUI/panel_stress_params.RISEscene:232`'s `ior panel_scalar_dispersion` is three per-channel CONSTANTS combined algebraically, with no `P` term) |

| ~~DL-29~~ | ~~WETNESS_COAT_DESIGN.md §12 item 10~~ | ~~A measured `tau` curve is grey under the default RGB rasterizer because `PiecewiseLinearScalarPainter` broadcasts one 555nm sample and reports no per-channel variation~~ | CLOSED 2026-09-14 | `27645c2a` (test) / `c4ead095` (round-1 fix, CMF integration) / `dfe4a537` (redesign, this slice). **Convention: `GetValuesAt` returns the curve evaluated at the three representative wavelengths `RISE::ScalarPainterRGB::kChannelNM` = {611, 549, 465} nm** (Interfaces/IScalarPainter.h, shared with `DielectricSPF`'s RGB dispersion loop, which used to carry a private `kARChannelNM` copy of the same array); `HasPerChannelVariation()` is true iff those three samples differ, EXACT comparison. The round-1 CMF-integration-under-D65 answer was replaced on review: a colorimetric RGB is correct only for a slot consumed LINEARLY as a transmittance (`tau`), and most slots this painter feeds are not — `ior`/`ext`/`film_ior`/`film_extinction` feed a per-channel Fresnel, `absorption`/`scattering`/`extinction` are rates that go through `exp()`, `roughness` parameterises a lobe — so `f(∫n) ≠ ∫f(n)` and the painter cannot know its slot; `ColorUtils::XYZtoRec709RGB` additionally gamut-PROJECTS an out-of-gamut curve before the matrix multiply (`Color.cpp` ~:214-219 calls `MoveXYZIntoRec709RGBGamut`, `ColorUtils.cpp` ~:351-363), so the round-1 triple was not even the integral it documented. Sampling also needs no snap-to-uniform tolerance (three evaluations of a flat curve are bit-identical) and never clamps. Red-proof (this slice's convention, against the pre-DL-29 555 nm broadcast restored in-tree): `IScalarPainterTest` 72 passed / **9 failed** -> 81/0; `PiecewiseLinearScalarPainterRGBTintTest` 8 passed / **10 failed** -> 18/0 — the `tau` row read R=G=B=0.0771581 (R/B exactly 1) where the per-channel closed form `L·(1-R0(1.5))²·tau(λ_c)²` is 0.128355/0.0725641/0.0221849, and now reads 0.128255/0.0724608/0.0221235 (R/B 5.797 vs 5.786). Two handled consequences. (a) **requireSingle slots keep loading**: a curve at a single-scalar slot (`coated_material` coat_*, `subsurfacescattering_material`/`randomwalk_sss_material`/`hair_material` `ior`, `weave_material` warp/weft_ior and siblings, `fabric_material` sheen_roughness/weave_rotation, `generichumantissue_material` g/sca, `arealight_shaderop` N, `hair_geometry` density/length_painter) would otherwise start hard-failing on `kScalarBoundToPerChannelFmt`; `Job.cpp`'s `ResolveOrDiagnoseScalar` now binds a single-scalar VIEW (`IScalarPainter::MakeSingleScalarSlotView`, new virtual, default nullptr) — the 549 nm sample under RGB, the UNCHANGED curve under the spectral rasterizers — and warns at parse time. An inline `r g b` triple in the same slot still hard-fails: the distinction is authoring intent, not arithmetic. `ScalarPainterParserTest::TestSpectralCurveInSingleScalarSlot` pins both branches (65/0 -> 69/0). (b) **a file `ior` now renders dispersively** under RGB (`disperse = pRIndex->HasPerChannelVariation()`, `DielectricSPF.cpp` ~:394, `PolishedSPF.cpp` ~:187, `PerfectRefractorSPF.cpp` ~:227): 3 refractions + 3 Fresnel reflections per hit, inside `ScatteredRayContainer::kCapacity` = 12 but ~3x the scattered-ray work of the achromatic path. That is intended for a measured dispersion curve; the dispersive path's OWN accuracy is a separate pre-existing defect, filed here as **DL-81**. `PiecewiseLinearScalarPainterRGBTintTest` row 2 renders a file-`ior` glass and pins it against the per-channel closed form (8% band, DL-81) plus an EXACT equivalence control against the same three iors authored inline. Scene check: `scenes/Tests/Materials/Enamel/silver_reference.RISEscene` (`ggx_material` conductor, `ior Ag_n`/`extinction Ag_k` measured curves) re-pointed at `pathtracing_pel_rasterizer`, 128x128, 64 spp, `oidn_denoise FALSE`, `pixel_filter box` — BEFORE (555 broadcast) full-image linear means R=G=B=0.380640, R/B exactly 1.00000; AFTER R=0.380852 G=0.380591 B=0.380238, R/B 1.00161 (centre crop 1.00179). Small because silver is nearly spectrally flat in the visible (R0 at 611/549/465 = 0.98700/0.98290/0.97905); the point is that it is no longer exactly achromatic and the level is unchanged (+0.06%), i.e. tint gained, no energy moved. Gate: `IScalarPainterTest` 81/0, `PiecewiseLinearScalarPainterRGBTintTest` 18/0, `ScalarPainterParserTest` 69/0, `ReferenceGraphTest` 364/0, `JHWhiteGuardSpectralTest` 30/0, `ThinFilmRGBSpectralTest` 7/0, `CstDeriveContractsTest`, `DielectricARTest`, `CoatedMaterialChunkTest`, `WeaveMaterialChunkTest`, `HairMaterialChunkTest`, `FabricMaterialChunkTest` all unchanged. Round 1's new `PiecewiseLinearScalarPainter.cpp` is DELETED (the convention needs no Color/CMF headers, so it is back to a header-only class) and removed from all five build projects. **precision-slice P2-6 addendum (2026-09-14): measured `scenes/FeatureBased/Materials/thinfilm_heattint_showcase.RISEscene`'s Ta sphere (`tf_ta_090`, `fresnel_mode thinfilm`) and found the checked-in scene is UNAFFECTED by this fix, for a reason worth recording.** Ta's bare-conductor F0 genuinely swings hard under this convention — recomputed directly from `colors/thinfilm/substrates/Ta.{n,k}`: 555 nm broadcast F0 = 0.3702 (achromatic) vs `{611,549,465}` nm per-channel F0 = (0.4582, 0.3675, 0.4148), a 24.7% channel spread — and a real render of a BARE Ta conductor (`fresnel_mode conductor`, no oxide layer, scratch scene modelled on `silver_reference.RISEscene`, `pathtracing_pel_rasterizer`, 400 spp, `oidn_denoise FALSE`, `pixel_filter box`, `ddf05c6c`'s `PiecewiseLinearScalarPainter.h` swapped in for BEFORE) confirms it end-to-end: BEFORE R=0.370185 G=0.370186 B=0.370183 (achromatic, matches the 555 nm F0 to 4 digits); AFTER R=0.458155 G=0.367474 B=0.414787 (matches the closed-form per-channel F0 to 3-4 digits). **But the checked-in showcase scene uses `fresnel_mode thinfilm`, not `conductor`, and that path is untouched by DL-29**: `GGXBRDF::Directional`'s thin-film branch (`GGXBRDF.cpp` ~:88-97, `ThinFilm::ReflectanceConductorRGBSpectral`'s `stackAt` callback) already sampled `ior`/`extinction` per-channel via `GetValueAtNM` at each wavelength before this fix — it never reads the (DL-29-affected) `GetValuesAt` triple at all; only the plain-conductor branch (~:107-110) does. Rendering the ACTUAL showcase material (`tf_ta_090`, thin film, same rasterizer swap) confirms this: BEFORE R=0.472613 G=0.378164 B=0.441452, AFTER R=0.472622 G=0.378172 B=0.441457 — a <0.01% change, indistinguishable from MC noise at this sample count. **Conclusion: DL-29 has a large, confirmed, render-verified effect on plain-conductor (`fresnel_mode conductor`) GGX materials bound to a measured curve (this row's `silver_reference.RISEscene` case, and the Ta conductor-only scratch scene above), but no effect on `fresnel_mode thinfilm` materials — including every sphere in `thinfilm_heattint_showcase.RISEscene` — because that Fresnel path was already per-channel-correct.** No production scene file was modified for this measurement (scratch scenes only, not committed). | S | precision | user-visible (measured spectral tint vanishes under RGB pathtracing) |

| ~~DL-14~~ | ~~SIGNALS_UNDER_BIDIRECTIONAL_TRANSPORT.md §10~~ | ~~Ray differentials (`fw`/`fwo`) are 0 under BDPT/VCM/MLT — the FIELD exists and is carried, but no differentials are ever computed for those walks, so it stays zero; a PT-vs-bidirectional difference on filtered-noise / footprint-driven bands~~ | CLOSED 2026-09-17 (re-verification found the row's premise false, not a fix — no production code changed) | **Re-verified on this HEAD and found the defect does not exist.** `ICamera::GenerateRay` (`PinholeCamera.cpp:203-207`, `ThinLensCamera.cpp:450`, `OrthographicCamera.cpp:170`, `FisheyeCamera.cpp:202`) unconditionally stamps Igehy ray differentials onto the camera ray it returns, with no gate on which rasterizer is asking. `BDPTPelRasterizer.cpp:95`, `VCMPelRasterizer.cpp:299` and `MLTRasterizer.cpp:198` (MLT drives a `BDPTIntegrator` instance directly — `MLTRasterizer.cpp:166`,`376`) all obtain their eye subpath's starting ray through that SAME interface call and hand it, unmodified, into `GenerateEyeSubpathImpl` as `currentRay` (`BDPTIntegrator.cpp:1652`). `Object::IntersectRay` (`Object.cpp:1013-1022`) computes `ri.geometric.txFootprint` whenever `ray.hasDifferentials` is true — true for that first `currentRay`, false for every ray after any scattering bounce, IN EVERY INTEGRATOR INCLUDING PT (`Object.cpp`'s own comment: "every ray after the first scattering bounce" gets no footprint) — so the depth-0 eye vertex gets the IDENTICAL non-zero footprint PT's own primary ray gets, and every deeper vertex gets zero on both, which is not a PT-vs-bidirectional difference at all. The 2026-09-11 S1 slice (`925c2720`) already added the two copy sites this requires (`v.txFootprint = ri.geometric.txFootprint;` at the eye- and light-subpath vertex population blocks in `BDPTIntegrator.cpp`, and `PathVertexEval::PopulateRIGFromVertex`'s `ri.txFootprint = vertex.txFootprint;`) believing them inert ("all-zero... they emit no ray differentials") — they were not inert; the belief that the camera ray itself carried no differentials for these rasterizers was never checked against `ICamera::GenerateRay`'s own unconditional behaviour. Direct render measurement (`tests/BidirectionalTextureFootprintParityTest.cpp`, an `fbm`-fade floor at grazing incidence, single point light — VCM has no directional/ambient-light NEE at all, an unrelated pre-existing gap confirmed during this slice's own scene-authoring, so a point light was used to stay on-topic): PT vs BDPT whole-image mean 0.506787 vs 0.506787 and whole-image horizontal-finite-difference "HF energy" (a footprint-fade-sensitive metric, `mean(\|lum(x+1,y)-lum(x,y)\|)`) 0.0077324 vs 0.0077322 (agree to ~1e-4 relative, both at 64 spp); VCM mean 0.507929 (0.22% off, ordinary NEE-strategy-mixture noise). **Red-proof**: temporarily forcing `currentRay.hasDifferentials = false` right after `Ray currentRay = cameraRay;` in `GenerateEyeSubpathImpl` (BDPTIntegrator.cpp) — the literal code shape this row's text described — and rebuilding turns the PT-vs-BDPT HF-energy check red (BDPT's HF energy jumps 0.0077->0.0235, PT unchanged; VCM's own HF, already 4x noisier than PT/BDPT at 64 spp per RISE's documented VCM-variance characteristic, moves 0.0318->0.0379, confirming the mechanism is shared but the noise floor masks it at a render-based metric — the mechanism-sharing argument, not a separate render-level VCM proof, is what the row's VCM closure rests on: `VCMIntegrator.cpp` never references `txFootprint` at all — grep the symbol — because VCM consumes the exact same `std::vector<BDPTVertex>` BDPT's own `GenerateEyeSubpath` call produces). Reverting the mutation restores the green state exactly (`git diff` empty on `BDPTIntegrator.cpp`). Stale claims corrected: `BDPTVertex.h:162`'s field comment, `docs/SIGNALS_UNDER_BIDIRECTIONAL_TRANSPORT.md` §2's "BDPT/VCM/MLT eye rays carry no ray differentials" bullet and §10's matching residual bullet, and `tests/SignalIntegratorConsistencyTest.cpp`'s file-header comment (its design choice to avoid `fw`/`fwo` in its own scenes stays correct, for the depth>=1 reason, not the depth-0 reason it originally gave). Full account, including the light-vertex-footprint design note (a light-subpath vertex gets zero footprint by construction, the same way any post-first-bounce vertex does under any integrator — PT has no analogue to compare against, so this is not a residual) and the cost measurement (no cost: no code changed), in [DL14_BIDIRECTIONAL_RAY_DIFFERENTIALS.md](DL14_BIDIRECTIONAL_RAY_DIFFERENTIALS.md). | M | precision | user-visible (footprint-aware texture filtering under BDPT/VCM/MLT) — ALREADY WORKING, not broken |

| ~~DL-80~~ | ~~Utilities/Color/ColorUtils.h:17 / Color.h:71 / SpectralPacket.h:20~~ | ~~`Color.h` and `ColorUtils.h` are mutually circular via `SpectralPacket.h`'s own `#include "ColorUtils.h"`, so whichever of the two a translation unit includes FIRST wins the include-guard race and the other's declarations (`ColorUtils::XYZFromNM`, transitively `IFunction1D`) are invisible to `SpectralPacket.h` when entered the losing way~~ Cycle broken at the root: `SpectralPacket.h`/`SpectralPacket_Template.h` no longer `#include "ColorUtils.h"` at all | CLOSED 2026-09-14 (debt-misc slice) | Row filed by the `precision` slice (`11393740`, uncommitted to master at the time this slice started); its own fix commit originally worked around the landmine at its two discovered call sites (`PiecewiseLinearScalarPainter.cpp`, `tests/IScalarPainterTest.cpp`) by including `Color.h` first, not at the root.  **Superseded**: per the `precision` slice's own re-verification, its DL-29 redesign subsequently removed the code paths that needed that workaround, so those two include-order workaround sites no longer exist in its branch either way.  This row's root fix (breaking the `Color.h`/`ColorUtils.h` cycle at `SpectralPacket.h`) supersedes any such workaround regardless of whether the sites survive — the include-order hazard it worked around is gone for every caller, not just those two.  The `precision` branch's own copy of this DL-80 row is reconciled against this one at merge time to avoid a double-filing under the same id. Re-derived independently on current master (`a3aa5b8d`) by reading the three cited lines directly: `ColorUtils.h:17` `#include "Color.h"`; `Color.h:71` `#include "SpectralPacket.h"`; `SpectralPacket.h:20` (pre-fix) `#include "ColorUtils.h"`. Red-proved with two new standalone tests exercising both entry orders directly against `git rev-parse a3aa5b8d`'s unfixed headers: `tests/ColorUtilsBeforeColorIncludeOrderTest.cpp` (includes `ColorUtils.h` then `Color.h`) reproduces the EXACT reported errors — `error: unknown type name 'IFunction1D'` and `error: no member named 'XYZFromNM' in namespace 'RISE::ColorUtils'` at `SpectralPacket.h:109,246` and `SpectralPacket_Template.h:222` — while its sibling `tests/ColorBeforeColorUtilsIncludeOrderTest.cpp` (the order every existing production file happened to use) compiled clean throughout, confirming the race is real and order-dependent, not a general breakage. **Fix**: `SpectralPacket.h` and `SpectralPacket_Template.h` no longer include `ColorUtils.h` — each forward-declares the single function it actually calls (`bool ColorUtils::XYZFromNM(XYZPel&, const Scalar)`) plus `struct XYZPel;`, and `SpectralPacket.h` gained a direct `#include "../../Interfaces/IFunction1D.h"` (previously reached only transitively through the now-removed `ColorUtils.h` edge) for the `IFunction1D*`-constructor overload it defines inline. `ColorUtils.h`'s own `#include "Color.h"` is UNCHANGED (many existing files rely on it transitively) — only the back-edge that closed the cycle was cut, so the fix needs no caller to change its own include order. **Second-order breakage found and fixed in the same pass**: cutting that back-edge also removed a HIDDEN transitive path 9 other files relied on to reach `ColorUtils.h`'s declarations without including it directly (`Color.h` used to reach `ColorUtils.h` via `SpectralPacket.h`, so anything that included `Color.h` got `ColorUtils.h` for free) — a full library rebuild after the header fix failed with `no member named 'SerializeRGBPel'/'DeserializeRGBPel' in namespace 'RISE::ColorUtils'` in `CausticPelPhotonMap.cpp`/`GlobalPelPhotonMap.cpp`; a symbol-level audit (grepping every real, non-comment `ColorUtils::<exact-symbol>` call site against every file that already includes `ColorUtils.h` directly) found 9 total: `DetectorSpheres/IsotropicRGBDetectorSphere.cpp`, `Job.cpp`, `PhotonMapping/{Caustic,Global}{Pel,Spectral}PhotonMap.cpp` (4 files), `PhotonMapping/TranslucentPelPhotonMap.cpp`, `RasterImages/PPMWriter.cpp`, `Shaders/VCMIntegrator.cpp` — each given its own direct `#include ".../Color/ColorUtils.h"` rather than relying on transitive luck (two further textual hits, `Rendering/FrameStoreColorSpace.h` and `Shaders/VCMIntegrator.h`, were confirmed to be COMMENT-only references and needed no fix; `FrameStoreColorSpace.h` got a documentation-anchoring include anyway, harmlessly). Full library rebuild clean (zero warnings) after both the header fix and the 9 call-site fixes. Gate: both include-order tests green; `ColorUtilsTest` (all passed), `FrameStoreColorMathTest` (324/0), `JakobHanikaRoundTripTest` (14/0), `RGBPainterSpectralRoundTripTest` (18/0), `TexCoord1PainterTest` (33/0), `ThinFilmAnodizeSwatchTest` (24/0), `TextureExpressionVMTest` (846/0), `ThinFilmRGBSpectralTest` (7/0), `AgentObjectMapTest` (254/0), `PainterVolumeAccessorTest` (all passed) — every test file matching `grep -l 'SpectralPacket\|ColorUtils' tests/*.cpp`. **Not fixed, and not a new debt**: `SpectralPacket.h`'s own `#if 0 ... #endif` dead-code block (a `GetRGB()` overload calling `ColorUtils::RGBFromNM`, a name that has never existed — the closest real function, `ArbritaryRGBFromNM`, is itself commented out in `ColorUtils.cpp`'s explicitly-labeled "DEAD CODE section") is pre-existing, already self-labeled dead code on both ends, not a hidden landmine, and out of scope. | S | API/bridge gap | internal (fixed before any in-tree file tripped the losing order in production; the `precision` slice's DL-29 fix, if merged as-is, keeps working — its own include-order workaround is now simply redundant, not broken) |
| ~~DL-18~~ | ~~CLOTH_FABRIC_DESIGN.md §15 item 13~~ | ~~The Blender bridge has no sheen, anisotropic, or velvet mapping at all — Principled's Sheen sockets have no `fabric_material` target~~ | **CLOSED 2026-09-17** (slice `debt-dl18`, branched `master` `be8904dc`; red-proof test commit `ea6cce49`, fix commit `099a6cb1`, exporter wiring `d1d28417`; `tests/BlenderBridgeFabricTest.cpp` 27/0 checks/failures; `test_hair_export.py`'s new `BridgeMaterialSheenMarshallingTest` + suite total 64/0, was 4 errors + 1 failure pre-fix). **P1 FOLLOW-UP (same slice, same day, post-closure external review): the landing above had its own bug** — `add_pbr_metallic_roughness_material` baked `emission_painter_name` into the PBR base UNCONDITIONALLY, then handed that base to `AddFabricMaterial` as the sheen substrate; `FabricMaterial::IsSupportedSubstrate` refuses any substrate with `GetEmitter() != 0`, so Emission Strength > 0 + Sheen Weight > 0 on ONE Principled node failed `add_material` outright, and `rise_blender_scene_to_job` aborts the whole job on one material's failure — a whole-render regression. Sibling audit (audit-by-bug-pattern) found the IDENTICAL bug in `GLTFSceneImporter.cpp`'s own `KHR_materials_sheen` handling (which this bridge's mapping already mirrors) **and** a second sibling in its `KHR_materials_clearcoat` handling (`coated_material`'s substrate check is the same refusal). Fixed all three the same way: build the PBR base WITHOUT emission whenever it is about to be wrapped, register the wrap under an intermediate name, then re-attach the emission at the OUTER layer via `AddLambertianLuminaireMaterial` (a fully generic wrapper — forwards `GetBSDF`/`GetSPF` from any `IMaterial`, builds its own `LambertianEmitter`, the identical class `GGXMaterial`'s own emissive constructor uses) — this KEEPS both the sheen/clearcoat layer and the emission rather than dropping either, since RISE already has a way to make a wrapped material emissive (no `DL-153` needed). Fix commits `e80f9f23` (Blender bridge) + `83d0181b` (glTF importer + Python marshalling test + docs). `tests/BlenderBridgeFabricTest.cpp`: 43/0 (was 40/6 red). `tests/GLTFSheenImportTest.cpp`: 35/0 (was 22/1 red). `tests/GLTFClearcoatImportTest.cpp`: 27/0 (was 14/1 red). `test_hair_export.py`: 65/0 (was 64/0; new marshalling-parity row, not itself a red-proof — the bug was native-side only). Full account: `docs/BLENDER_MATERIAL_TRANSLATION.md` "Sheen + Emission Strength on the SAME node", `docs/GLTF_IMPORT.md` §15. Considered and left open, not filed: whether `rise_blender_scene_to_job` aborting the whole job on one material's failure is itself a debt — it is the SAME uniform behaviour every `add_*` loop in that function and `GLTFSceneImporter`'s own material loop already has (any single bad painter/geometry/material name already kills a whole scene load, confirmed via `AgentChunkCrudTest`'s and this slice's own red-proof's "one or more entities failed to register" diagnostics), so it is a pre-existing, consistent, cross-cutting design choice, not a defect newly introduced or uniquely reachable through this bug. | ~~No `fabric_material`/`sheen` reference found in the Blender bridge sources this sweep (`grep -rl fabric_material` under the Blender add-on tree returns nothing)~~ Re-verified 2026-09-17: **the "anisotropic" third of this row's claim was already stale before this slice started** — Landing 8 (commit `25d271df`, unrelated to this debt) already wired Blender's `Anisotropic`/`Anisotropic Rotation` sockets through `AddPBRMetallicRoughnessMaterial`'s `anisotropy_factor`/`anisotropy_rotation`; only `src/Blender/README.md`'s "Current Limitations" list still (wrongly) claimed it was missing, corrected in this slice's docs commit `acf1bbb8`. **Sheen genuinely had no mapping** (confirmed: zero references anywhere in `bridge.py` / `rise_blender_bridge.{h,cpp}` / `exporter.py`'s Principled-reading code pre-fix). Fixed with ABI v12: `rise_blender_material` gained `sheen_color_painter_name` / `sheen_roughness` / `sheen_roughness_texture_painter_name` (appended after `emissive_scale`); `add_pbr_metallic_roughness_material` registers its PBR base under an intermediate `<name>::pbrbase` name and wraps it in a `fabric_material` sheen layer via `AddFabricMaterial` when sheen contributes — the same `pbrRegisterName` mechanism `GLTFSceneImporter.cpp`'s own `KHR_materials_sheen` handling uses, which this mapping mirrors exactly (`sheen_weight * sheen_tint` -> one `sheen_color` slot, `fabric "custom"`, `weave_rotation "0.0"`). A material with BOTH Sheen and Anisotropic set reaches fabric-over-anisotropic-GGX through `fabric_material`'s OWN `base` slot (already on its substrate allowlist via `pbr_metallic_roughness_material`) — no `coated_material` allowlist widening needed. Textured `sheen_roughness` (an `IScalarPainter` slot) wraps a colour-painter texture via `RISE_API_CreatePainterChannelScalarPainter`, mirroring hair's v10 `beta_m`/`beta_n`/`ior` texture exception. Velvet: Blender removed the standalone `Velvet BSDF` node in 4.0 (folded into Principled Sheen) per Blender's own release notes — not independently re-verified against a live `bpy.types` registry from this sandbox, documented with that hedge in `docs/BLENDER_MATERIAL_TRANSLATION.md`'s new "Anisotropy and sheen" section, which also carries the full mapping table. A default-white (or unlinked) tint still sends `None`, bit-identical to a pre-fix payload. Native-path confirmation (the bridge->Job forwarding was never broken, so this is a consistency check, not a red-proof): `tests/BlenderBridgeSpecularTintTest.cpp`, 12/0 -- a saturated-red `specular_color_painter_name` on a grey-base, low-roughness dielectric measures an R/G channel ratio of 57.4 at the mirror-reflection peak (F0=(0.04,0,0)) against 1.0 for an untinted control (F0=(0.04,0.04,0.04)) in the SAME job. Sibling audit of every other Principled socket the exporter reads or ignores (`Coat Weight` [warned, no ABI field], `Coat Tint`/`Coat Roughness`/`Coat IOR` [not read at all, no ABI field], `Sheen` [DL-18, done], `Emission Strength`/`Emission Color` [wired], `Subsurface Weight` [warned, no ABI field], `Subsurface Radius`/`Subsurface Scale` [not read at all, no ABI field], `Transmission Weight` [wired], `Alpha` [warned but ignored, no ABI field], `Normal` [wired via bump/normal-map modifier], `Coat Normal`/`Tangent` [not read at all, no ABI field]) found every remaining gap has NO existing `rise_blender_material` field to populate -- a genuinely different, larger class of debt (an ABI addition, not a one-function exporter fix) -- opened as **DL-186**, not fixed in this S-sized slice. `docs/BLENDER_MATERIAL_TRANSLATION.md` "Specular Tint" section, `src/Blender/README.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `tests/README.md` updated. **Review follow-up (2 P2s, no P1, same slice, 2026-09-18):** (1) documented a pre-existing, unchanged-by-this-fix limitation — `rs = lerp(base_color, F0_dielectric, metallic)` routes BOTH `specular_factor` and `specular_color` through F0_dielectric's dielectric-only branch, so Specular Tint (like the already-shipped Specular IOR Level) has zero effect at `metallic=1`; Blender 4.x Principled additionally tints the METALLIC edge (F82-style) with the same socket, which this bridge does not reproduce. Not a wrong formula introduced here — recorded as a documented limitation in `docs/BLENDER_MATERIAL_TRANSLATION.md`'s "Specular Tint" section, `src/Blender/README.md`'s "Current Limitations", and this row. (2) Added a bpy-free behavioural marshalling test, `BridgeMaterialSpecularColorMarshallingTest` in `test_hair_export.py` (mirrors `BridgeMaterialSheenMarshallingTest`'s pattern for its own already-correct v12 fields) — confirms `_marshal_material` forwards `specular_color_painter_name` unmodified (a set value travels through; the default/deleted-attribute case sends `None`, i.e. NULL at the ctypes layer, which `add_pbr_metallic_roughness_material` then treats as its own `"none"` sentinel). `test_hair_export.py`: 72/0 (was 69/0). | S | API/bridge gap | user-visible (Blender-authored scenes only; any material with a non-default Specular Tint) |
| ~~DL-186~~ | ~~(new; opened by the `debt-dl151` slice's sibling audit while confirming no OTHER Principled socket the exporter silently drops has an ALREADY-ABI'D field, no source-doc heading)~~ | ~~Six Principled BSDF inputs the exporter reads-and-warns or never reads at all have NO corresponding field anywhere in `rise_blender_material`/`rise_blender_scene`: `Coat Tint`, `Coat Roughness`, `Coat IOR` (no `coated_material`/clearcoat bridging at all — `Coat Weight` is only ever compared against its own default to decide whether to WARN, `_check_principled_feature(..., "Coat Weight", 0.0, "clearcoat")`), `Subsurface Radius`, `Subsurface Scale` (same story via `Subsurface Weight`'s warn-only check — no SSS material bridging exists), and `Alpha` (`_material_payload` reads the socket only to decide whether to print `"RISE currently ignores Principled alpha and alpha textures..."`; there is no alpha slot on any `rise_blender_material` variant to populate, and `docs/SCENE_CONVENTIONS.md` separately notes alpha-mask is only honored under `pixelpel_rasterizer` scene-side, so even a populated slot would need rasterizer-aware handling). Also unread and unwarned entirely (no `_check_principled_feature` call for either): `Coat Normal`, `Tangent`. Closing any of these needs a NEW ABI field (or several, for the clearcoat/SSS cases: colour + scalar(s), matching the sheen precedent's `sheen_color_painter_name`/`sheen_roughness`/`sheen_roughness_texture_painter_name` triple) plus a native-side `coated_material`/`sss` construction path in `add_material` — an ABI-version-bump change, not a same-slot population fix like DL-151/DL-18 — so it is properly a SEPARATE, larger debt rather than a DL-151 sibling fix.~~ | **CLOSED 2026-09-18** (slice `debt-dl186`, branched `master` `50dbc4d8`; ABI v12 -> v13, fix commits in `src/Blender/native/rise_blender_bridge.{h,cpp}`, `src/Blender/addons/rise_renderer/{exporter.py,bridge.py}`) | Closed three of the six gaps with real bridging, left Alpha warn-only with a documented reason (no dead ABI field), and filed the remaining two (Coat Normal, Tangent) as **DL-192**.  **Coat**: `rise_blender_material` gained `coat_weight`/`coat_weight_texture_painter_name` (numeric + the v12 texture-exception pattern), `coat_tint_painter_name`, `coat_roughness`/`coat_roughness_texture_painter_name`, `coat_ior` -- `add_pbr_metallic_roughness_material` wraps the PBR base in a `coated_material` layer exactly the way it already wraps sheen in `fabric_material` (same `<name>::pbrbase` intermediate-name mechanism, same emission-deferred-to-the-outer-layer fix DL-18's P1 established).  `coat_roughness` is Blender's PERCEPTUAL roughness and is SQUARED into a GGX alpha before the call, matching GLTFSceneImporter.cpp's own `clearcoat_roughness_factor^2` convention for `KHR_materials_clearcoat`.  **LAYERING DECISION**: a material with BOTH Coat Weight and Sheen contributing gets SHEEN ONLY -- `coated_material`'s substrate allowlist does not accept a `fabric_material` (the sheen result), so the coat cannot wrap on top of sheen; this is the SAME call GLTFSceneImporter.cpp's own KHR_materials_clearcoat + KHR_materials_sheen combination already makes, applied consistently to the Blender bridge, with a warning at both the native and exporter layers rather than a silent drop.  **Subsurface**: a SEPARATE model, `RISE_BLENDER_MATERIAL_RANDOMWALK_SSS` (not a PBR wrap -- Blender's own Principled blends diffuse/SSS by weight rather than layering SSS under a specular stack, mirrored here by making Subsurface mutually exclusive with the PBR/Coat/Sheen branch, matching this function's pre-existing PBR-vs-DIELECTRIC exclusivity for heavy Transmission).  New fields `subsurface_absorption`/`subsurface_scattering` (ALREADY-CONVERTED inline `r g b` literals), `subsurface_ior`, `subsurface_g`, `subsurface_roughness`.  **THE CONVERSION** (exporter.py, in Python, because that is where the raw untextured Base Color triple it needs is already in hand): a documented single-scattering-albedo approximation -- `mfp = radius*scale`, `sigma_t = 1/mfp`, `albedo = clamp(base_color, 0, 0.999)`, `sigma_s = albedo*sigma_t`, `sigma_a = (1-albedo)*sigma_t` -- NOT PBRT's/Blender's own photon-beam-diffusion (Christensen-Burley) inversion, which needs a lookup-table fit this bridge does not carry; documented as an approximation in docs/BLENDER_MATERIAL_TRANSLATION.md "Subsurface".  Transmission wins over Subsurface on conflict (warned); Subsurface skips Coat/Sheen with a warning (no substrate to wrap when the model itself is RANDOMWALK_SSS, not PBR).  **Alpha**: deliberately left warn-only, no ABI field added -- RISE DOES have a reachable construction-API alpha path (`IJob::AddAlphaTestShaderOp` / a `transparency_shaderop`, already used by GLTFSceneImporter.cpp's `WireAlphaShader`), but it is a per-material SHADER-OP CHAIN (`Job::AddAdvancedShader`) that REPLACES the bridge's always-on default shader -- a subsystem this Blender bridge has none of today -- so a `rise_blender_material` alpha field would be a genuinely dead ABI field until that subsystem exists; also `AlphaTestShaderOp.h`'s own caveat that BDPT/VCM/MLT/photon tracers ignore it regardless.  Filed as **DL-193** (a per-material shader-op-chain bridge -- materially bigger than an ABI field, not attempted here).  **Sibling audit**: `Coat Normal` and `Tangent` remain entirely unread with no reader anywhere in `exporter.py` (confirmed by this sweep) -- filed as **DL-192**.  Tests: `tests/BlenderBridgeCoatTest.cpp` 49/0 (compile-fails against pre-v13 header/.cpp -- the genuine red-proof for a brand-new ABI field/branch, same pattern DL-18's own red-proof history documents), `tests/BlenderBridgeSSSTest.cpp` 29/0 (same compile-fail red-proof; probes `IMaterial::GetRandomWalkSSSParams()` directly for exact per-channel sigma_a/sigma_s/ior/g/maxBounces parity, not just a BSDF-response comparison), `src/Blender/addons/rise_renderer/test_hair_export.py` 77/0 (was 72/0; +4 new coat/subsurface marshalling rows in the new `BridgeMaterialCoatSubsurfaceMarshallingTest` class, +1 simulated-stale-dylib ABI-version-mismatch row, `test_all_pointer_target_structs_match`'s pre-existing generic ctypes-vs-header layout check now also covers every new `_Material` field).  Sibling suites (`BlenderBridgeFabricTest.cpp` 43/0, `BlenderBridgeHairTest.cpp` 133/0, `BlenderBridgeSpecularTintTest.cpp` 12/0, `SourceHygieneTest` 167/0) unaffected -- no `src/Library/` file was touched by this slice, only `src/Blender/`.  Renders: a coated red plastic sphere (`coated_material` over `pbr_metallic_roughness_material`, coat_weight=1.0, coat_roughness=0.1 == 0.316228^2, coat_ior=1.5) against the same base with no coat -- sphere-region mean R channel (0-255, sRGB PNG) 110.8 (bare) -> 83.6 (coated), matching the documented "wet darkening" direction (coated_material.RISEscene's own comment); a Subsurface sphere using the conversion above (Base Color (0.9,0.85,0.7), Radius (1.0,0.4,0.2), Scale 0.05) renders a warm skin-tone bias (mean RGB 63.8/55.9/46.1) consistent with the per-channel sigma_a the formula assigns (R=2, lowest absorption -> deepest, warmest scattering; B=30, highest -> shallowest).  No Blender/bpy install is available in this sandbox, so the "Blender-authored" side of both renders is the exact arithmetic conversion documented above rather than a live bpy export; the underlying bridge functions are the same ones `tests/BlenderBridgeCoatTest.cpp` / `BlenderBridgeSSSTest.cpp` exercise directly.  **P1 review follow-up (same slice, same day): the coat_roughness perceptual->GGX-alpha squaring only applied on the NUMERIC path** -- `resolve_material_scalar_slot`'s texture branch bound the channel view UNSQUARED, so the SAME authored roughness (0.5) read `CoatedBRDF::GetCoatRoughness()` alpha 0.25 numerically but 0.5 (2x) through a texture.  Fixed by adding a `square` parameter to that shared helper: when true, the returned scalar painter is `RISE_API_CreateMultiplyScalarPainter(view, view)` (a self-product), cached under a further-derived `::sq` name so a texture shared UNSQUARED by `sheen_roughness_texture_painter_name` and SQUARED by `coat_roughness_texture_painter_name` gets two independent, correct results.  Sibling audit (same review): `sheen_roughness_texture_-painter_name` needs no squaring on either path (Charlie alpha is never perceptual-then-squared); hair's `beta_m`/`beta_n` scalar-texture slots need none either (the Chiang BCSDF consumes them directly, no squaring anywhere); `subsurface_roughness` has no texture-driven variant in this ABI at all, and even its numeric path is already squared UNIFORMLY inside `SubSurfaceScatteringBSDF`/`SubSurfaceScatteringSPF` themselves (`alpha = roughness^2`), so no bridge-side change belongs there regardless. A SECOND, independent red state was measured (not just the ABI compile-fail): isolating just the two `CoatLobeValue(...)` call sites in `CoatedBRDF.cpp` back to the substrate's unperturbed `onb`/`nv`/`nr` (leaving the rest of the DL-192 code, including `ResolveCoatFrame` itself, untouched) still compiles but drops the "peak moved" checks to red (2 failures) -- exactly the DL-100-style "ABI wired through but the frame silently not applied" bug this test is built to catch.  `CoatedSPF::Pdf` was independently confirmed to agree with `value()` on where the density lives (`pdfTiltedAtTilted > pdfFlatAtTilted`).  **Tangent: CLOSED for cases (i)/(ii), residual split to DL-213.**  (i) A `ShaderNodeTangent` in UV_MAP mode naming the active (or no) UV map needs NO bridging: `GGXBRDF::ResolveTangentONB` (GGXBRDF.cpp) returns the mesh's own `ri.onb` UNCHANGED whenever no rotation painter is bound, and `ri.onb.u()` is built from that SAME mesh UV tangent (`Object::IntersectRay`, `bShadingTangentFromGeometry` branch) -- so this case is already bridged BY CONSTRUCTION; `exporter.py` now documents this explicitly (a `_is_active_uv_tangent` predicate + a 30-line comment) instead of leaving the socket entirely unread.  (ii) A CONSTANT `ShaderNodeVectorRotate` (Z-axis) feeding a Tangent(UV_MAP) node composes, by simple addition, onto the SAME `anisotropy_rotation_*` slot Principled's own "Anisotropic Rotation" already binds (DL-16's `tangent_rotation_scalar`) -- both are angles around the mesh tangent frame's own axis.  (iii) Anything else (a second UV map's tangent, a fully procedural direction, a non-constant rotation angle) has no RISE mechanism -- RISE's anisotropy direction always derives from the mesh's own UV tangent basis, optionally rotated by one scalar, never an arbitrary per-texel/per-vertex override vector -- and is now WARNED AND DROPPED (previously: silently unread, no warning at all) rather than silently ignored, naming **DL-213** (the mesh-level tangent-override mechanism this would need) in both warning paths. Filed as the general architecture gap **DL-214** (this correction is a fact about the ENGINE, not the Blender bridge specifically) and `AlphaTestShaderOp.h`'s own comment + `rise_blender_bridge.h`'s `alpha_mode` field comment + `exporter.py`'s matching comment were all corrected in this same slice. | DL-213 | (new; the residual of DL-192's own Tangent socket closure, no source-doc heading) | Blender Principled BSDF's Tangent input, when it names anything other than case (i) (a `ShaderNodeTangent` in UV_MAP mode on the active UV map, already bridged by construction) or case (ii) (a CONSTANT Z-axis `ShaderNodeVectorRotate` composing onto that -- DL-16's `anisotropy_rotation`), has no RISE mechanism: a SECOND UV map's tangent basis, a fully procedural direction (e.g. built from `Geometry`/`Object Info`/noise nodes), or a non-constant (linked/textured) rotation angle on the Vector Rotate node all reach this row.  RISE's `ggx_material` anisotropy direction is derived ENTIRELY from the mesh's own UV tangent basis (`Object::IntersectRay`'s `bShadingTangentFromGeometry` construction) optionally rotated by ONE scalar (`tangent_rotation`/`tangent_rotation_scalar`, DL-16) -- there is no per-object, per-vertex, or per-texel tangent-BASIS override mechanism at all (as opposed to DL-17's per-texel ROTATION, which is a genuinely different, already-solved problem: a rotation of the existing basis, not a replacement of it).  Closing this needs a mesh-level tangent-override mechanism (e.g. importing a second UV map's tangent as an alternate `ITriangleMeshGeometry` attribute, or a per-vertex direction buffer) threaded through the SAME place `Object::IntersectRay` currently derives the coherent tangent from -- a materially larger, mesh-data-model change, not a material-slot or ABI-field fix. | OPEN-confirmed | `exporter.py`'s `_material_payload` (post-DL-192): the Tangent-handling block warns and drops for exactly this case, naming DL-213 in the warning text (`grep -n "DL-213" src/Blender/addons/rise_renderer/exporter.py`); `grep -rn "tangent.*override\|per-vertex tangent" src/Library/Objects/Object.cpp src/Library/Geometry/TriangleMeshGeometryIndexed*`: zero matches -- confirms no such mechanism exists anywhere in the geometry layer. | M | API/bridge gap | user-visible (Blender-authored scenes only; a second-UV-map or procedural anisotropy-tangent direction is warned and dropped rather than approximated) |

| DL-214 | (new; found by the `debt-dl192` slice while building `tests/BlenderBridgeAlphaTest.cpp`'s render-level red-proof for DL-193, no source-doc heading) | `AlphaTestShaderOp.h`'s own "integrator-compatibility caveat" comment claimed the alpha shader-op mechanism (`AddAlphaTestShaderOp`/`transparency_shaderop`, reached via `IShader::Shade()` through `RayCaster::SelectShader(ri)` / `ri.pShader`) is honoured by "the path tracer (PT)" as well as the legacy direct shaders, and only bypassed by BDPT/VCM/MLT/photon tracers.  DIRECT RENDER MEASUREMENT (identical scene, three rasterizer kinds) proved this WRONG: `PathTracingIntegrator.cpp` -- what `pathtracing_pel_rasterizer` / `RISE_API_CreatePathTracingPelRasterizer` actually run, and RISE's (and this Blender bridge's) DEFAULT integrator choice -- has NO reference to `SelectShader` or `ri.pShader` anywhere in it; it evaluates emission, BSDF sampling and NEE directly against `ri.pMaterial`, entirely bypassing the shader-op pipeline this mechanism lives in.  `pixelpel_rasterizer` (RISE's legacy, direct-lighting-only, no-GI rasterizer -- built via `Job::SetPixelBasedPelRasterizer`, which DOES dispatch every hit through `RayCaster::CastRay`/`SelectShader`) is therefore the ONLY rasterizer kind any consumer of this mechanism (the pre-existing glTF importer's `alphaMode` MASK/BLEND handling, AND this slice's own new Blender-bridge Alpha bridging) can ever reach.  This is a GENERAL ENGINE finding, not specific to the Blender bridge that found it: `GLTFSceneImporter.cpp`'s `WireAlphaShader`-driven alphaMode handling has been silently non-functional under the modern PT integrator (and BDPT/VCM/MLT) since whenever `PathTracingIntegrator.cpp` superseded the shader-op-based PT implementation the comment's "path tracer (PT)" language apparently once described -- an unknown but plausibly long-standing user-visible gap for any glTF asset with alphaMode=MASK/BLEND rendered under anything but `pixelpel_rasterizer`.  Not fixed in this slice (COMMON_RULES: a distinct defect, not the same bug pattern as DL-192/DL-193's own ABI-gap closures) -- `AlphaTestShaderOp.h`'s comment, `rise_blender_bridge.h`'s `alpha_mode` field comment, and `exporter.py`'s matching comment were all corrected to state the ACTUAL compatible set (this slice's only change here: documentation, plus this slice's OWN new alpha-wiring warns correctly for the full incompatible set including PT).  A principled fix (per `AlphaTestShaderOp.h`'s own pre-existing "Phase 4 candidate" note) would promote alpha masking to a hit-time geometry concern (a pre-commit hook on `IRayIntersectionModifier` or directly in the intersector) reachable by every integrator, not a shader op -- a materially larger change than either DL-192 or DL-193, out of scope here. | OPEN-confirmed | `grep -n "SelectShader\|ri\.pShader" src/Library/Shaders/PathTracingIntegrator.cpp src/Library/Shaders/BDPTIntegrator.cpp src/Library/Shaders/VCMIntegrator.cpp`: zero matches in all three.  `grep -n "SelectShader" src/Library/Rendering/RayCaster.cpp`: 3 matches (`CastRay`, `CastRayNM`, `CastRayHWSS`), each reached by the legacy per-pixel rasterizers.  Render-level: `tests/BlenderBridgeAlphaTest.cpp`'s `TestAlphaCutoutUnderPixelPel` (green: cutout works) vs `TestAlphaIgnoredUnderPTWithCaveat` (green: PT ignores it) on the IDENTICAL scene/materials, differing only in `rasterizer_kind`. | M | doc-rot / API-bridge gap (the underlying mechanism's scope, not a fixable bug in either consumer) | user-visible (any RISE user relying on glTF alphaMode or this slice's own Blender Alpha bridging under any rasterizer but the legacy `pixelpel_rasterizer`) |

| ~~DL-197~~ | ~~(the residual of DL-150, confirmed by its round-3 review, no source-doc heading -- now documented in CROSS_OBJECT_PROXIMITY_DESIGN.md §10)~~ | ~~`TriangleMeshGeometryIndexed::ComputeWatertightness`'s coincident-triangle discriminator (DL-150) cannot see two independently tessellated sheets closer than the weld eps whose quads were split on DIFFERENT diagonals: no two triangles share all three post-weld vertices, so the weld stitches them into a false 2-manifold and `SignedDistanceLower` returns a confidently wrong signed depth~~ | CLOSED 2026-09-18 | `dc23c61a` (fix; red-proof test `a4686afb`): a second, edge-based discriminator runs after the coincident-triangle one -- at every edge already known to be shared by exactly two triangles with distinct vertex triples, it refuses when the two triangles are geometrically COPLANAR (point-to-plane distance against the SAME `eps` the vertex weld used, threaded out of `WeldVertexPositions` via a new `outEps` param) AND oppositely wound (a cheap dot-product pre-filter); coplanarity, not the dot-product alone, is what separates a false stitch from a genuinely sharp convex crease (whose dot also approaches -1 as its apex shrinks, DL-150's own round-1 lesson). Red-proof: isolated pre-/post-fix A/B of `TriangleMeshGeometryIndexed.cpp` alone, `tests/MeshInteriorSignalTest.cpp` 100/1 (section (o) MONEY: `gap=0.001 < eps` false-certified `ok=true` pre-fix) -> 101/0 post-fix (101 = 100 + a new gap=0.01 control mirroring section (m)'s structure). `PoleWeldingWatertightnessTest` 57/0, `DisplacedGeometryTest` all passed, `ProximitySignalTest` 491/0, unchanged. No shipped glTF asset affected: all 21 `.glb` assets in `scenes/Tests/Geometry/assets` (111 primitives across every parsing `(meshIdx,primIdx)`) produce byte-for-byte identical `SignedDistanceLower` answers pre-/post-fix (isolated A/B, throwaway scratch tool, not committed). A first implementation (a second `vector`-valued per-edge map) cost +17%/+29% (sphere/Dragon `DoneIndexedTriangles`/`BuildGeometryFromPrimitive`, median of 15 `clock()` samples); folding the per-edge count and (up to two) triangle-index slots into ONE map with a small fixed-size struct value (no per-edge heap allocation) brought that to +1-4% (48401-vertex synthetic sphere ~44.3-44.4ms -> ~44.8-46.0ms; 91216-triangle/76809-vertex `DragonAttenuation` "Dragon" primitive ~57.9-58.0ms -> ~59.3-60.0ms). See `docs/CROSS_OBJECT_PROXIMITY_DESIGN.md` §10's own DL-197 bullet and `ComputeWatertightness`'s updated function comment in `TriangleMeshGeometryIndexed.cpp`. **Self-review disclosure**: this discriminator does NOT catch two independently-tessellated, coincident sheets that happen to face the SAME way (`normalDot >= 0` at every shared edge) -- not an oversight but a structural blind spot: a coplanar, same-winding edge pair is the exact local signature of an ordinary flat mesh region (two adjacent quads of one tessellated face, or a quad's own internal diagonal), so flagging it would misfire on essentially every flat-shaded surface in the corpus. | DL-216 | (new; found by review round 1 of the `debt-dl125` slice, 2026-09-18; source doc: [DL69_BDPT_LOBE_THROUGHPUT.md](DL69_BDPT_LOBE_THROUGHPUT.md) "DL-125 outcome" section 6.5) | Every `ISPF::EvaluateKrayNM` implementation returns `f_I(lambda_c) cos / p_I(lambda_c)`, but the direction was DRAWN from `p_I(lambda_h)`; the unbiased companion weight under RISE's HWSS (uniform wavelength pdf, no directional MIS across the bundle's wavelengths) is `f_I(lambda_c) cos / p_I(lambda_h)` | OPEN, deliberately unchanged in the slice that found it -- a REGIME SPLIT, not a uniform defect | The two expressions COINCIDE exactly wherever the lobe's DENSITY is wavelength-independent, which is every material whose only chromatic inputs are REFLECTANCE painters -- the overwhelmingly common case, everything DL-125 measured, and `HairSPF`'s own stated premise for its long-standing override.  They differ only under a chromatic SHAPE painter (`exponent`, `isotropy`, `alpha`, `roughness` bound to a wavelength-varying `IScalarPainter`).  Quantified at `IsotropicPhongSPF`, where the algebra is closed-form: the shipped weight is `Rs(lc) ((N_c+2)/(N_c+1)) cos_o`, the unbiased one is `Rs(lc) ((N_c+2)/(2 pi)) cos^{N_c}(alpha) cos_o / ((N_h+1)/(2 pi) cos^{N_h}(alpha))`, so the ratio shipped/unbiased is `cos^{N_h-N_c}(alpha) * (N_h+1)/(N_c+1)`.  At the `LambdaRampScalarPainter` configuration `HWSSCompanionKrayTest` section A2 already builds (`N = 40 + 0.15*(nm-550)`, so `N_h = 40` at 550 nm and `N_c = 55` at 650 nm) that is `(N_h+1)/(N_c+1) * cos^{N_h-N_c}(alpha) = 0.7321 * cos^{-15}(alpha)` -- **0.732x at the lobe peak, crossing unity at `alpha = 11.64 deg`, and 1.861x at `alpha = 20 deg`, where the hero lobe still carries `cos^40 = 0.083` of its peak density** (3.20x at 25 deg / 0.0195, 6.33x at 30 deg / 0.0032, i.e. it diverges into the tail where there is progressively less energy to misprice).  (Independently recomputed in round 4: `alpha = acos((41/56)^(1/15)) = 11.6415 deg`.)  That is a DIFFERENT wrong from the pre-DL-125 aggregate fallback, not obviously a smaller one, which is why this row exists rather than a claim that DL-125 closed the chromatic-shape case too.  **Recipe -- and the PREFERRED option is cheaper than the status quo, not more expensive** (review round 2, P3).  At the `RecomputeSubpathThroughputNM` site the quantity actually wanted is a RATIO, and the hero density cancels out of it: the companion's correct weight is `kray_I(lc) = f_I(lc) cos / p_I(lh)` against a hero already priced at `f_I(lh) cos / p_I(lh)`, so the ratio is simply `f_I(lc) / f_I(lh)` -- no density on either side.  A per-lobe **f-query** (`EvalFNM`, say: the selected lobe's own spectral BSDF value at a direction, with no division) would therefore both FIX this row and DROP the two `EvaluateKrayNM` calls' internal density evaluations, which are exactly what the +9.01% hair cost is made of.  The two alternatives are strictly worse: an extra `Scalar pdfHero` argument on `EvaluateKrayNM` keeps the division, and post-multiplying by `p_I(nm)/p_I(heroNM)` via `ISPF::PdfNM` needs no signature change but costs two more `PdfNM` calls AND is wrong for an SPF whose aggregate `PdfNM` is not the selected lobe's density (i.e. exactly this set).  NOTE that the f-ratio shortcut is specific to the RATIO site; PT's HWSS body and the two generator ladders consume an absolute weight and would still need the hero density.  Red-proof: extend `HWSSCompanionKrayTest` section A2 with a HERO-DENSITY variant that asserts `EvaluateKrayNM(lambda_c) * p_I(lambda_c) / p_I(lambda_h)` against a `ScatterNM`-at-hero replay; render-proof needs a scene binding a spectral `scalar_painter` to `exponent`/`isotropy`/`alpha` under `hwss TRUE`, of which the shipped corpus has none. | S | physics-bias | latent (needs a chromatic SHAPE painter -- `exponent` / `isotropy` / `alpha` / `roughness` -- under `hwss TRUE`; no shipped scene binds one) |

(a) grows `ScatteredRay`, which `ScatteredRayContainer` copies by `memcpy` into a fixed `kCapacity` array -- measure before choosing; (b) changes the `ISPF` signature for ~23 classes. Measured (user CPU, n=5): bypassing the memo for sampling programs instead costs 3.35x on a sampling scene (6.452 +/- 0.080 s vs 21.630 +/- 0.094 s), while the wider key is WITHIN NOISE on `plank_closeup` -- this slice measured 22.874 +/- 0.143 s against a pre-review 23.228 +/- 0.108 s, but that pre-review figure does NOT reproduce (the round-3 reviewer re-ran the same A/B at 22.885 +/- 0.10 vs 22.847 +/- 0.07, a -0.17% delta), so the honest claim is "no measurable cost", not "marginally faster". `add_wetness`'s clause 2 (`AgentSession.cpp`) now ACCEPTS a genuinely textured/procedural base (`ClassifyColorBinding_ == Varying`) instead of refusing it, emitting `def base_color sample(<name>)` + `pow(base_color.x/y/z, k)` in place of the literal `base_r/g/b` path. Red-proof: `tests/TextureExpressionVMTest.cpp` tests 75-77 (new) fail to COMPILE against the pre-fix library (`no member named 'UsesPainterSample'/'ColorPainterRefNames'/...`); Test 78 (added by the review) is red on 18 checks against the synthetic-record dispatch -- the png footprint row reads 0 where the direct read reads 0.5, `curv` 0 vs 2.5, `fw` 0 vs 0.05, the signal channel neutral, the vertex colour its fallback, the iridescent read its bias and `ptCoord1` falling back to UV0 -- and green at 940/0 (846/0 before the arc, 888/0 before the review). `tests/AgentAddWetnessTest.cpp` 234/0: the textured-substrate darkening case, plus the review's P1-2 rows (a textured base reports NO base colour and names the substrate painter in its message, instead of publishing a fabricated `baseColor: [0,0,0]` and offering to have darkened "the 0 0 0 that was there"). `tests/WetTextureDetailRenderTest.cpp` 23/0 is the row's RENDER-level gate, the one this row's own recipe asked for.  Its Test A is the RED-PROOF: an identity `sample(tex)` renders identically to the texture bound directly, against 19.8 % more contrast on the synthetic-record dispatch (deterministic at 1 spp).  Its Test B is a CONSISTENCY PIN -- green before the P1-1 fix too, since the emitted recipe is `mix(base, pow(base, k), damp)` whichever record the substrate was read at -- pinning that the darkening is PER TEXEL rather than by one representative colour (wet/dry 0.806 over the dry frame's darkest quartile against 0.948 over its brightest, mean 0.909). Lossless-weld argument for all three: at a pole/center row the position, normal, AND texcoord are already forced to a single value independent of column `i` (each's own `atPole`/`atCenter` ternary already canonicalizes `u`, and the position/normal formulas evaluate to a constant there), so nothing is lost welding them to one shared index. **Re-measured tallies (`tests/PoleWeldingWatertightnessTest.cpp`, the FINAL file including the Ellipsoid rows, 57 checks total) — the historical 29/3, 38/4, 42/0 figures below were found NOT to reproduce against this file and are superseded by these, each via `git checkout a4495f94 -- src/Library/Geometry/<File>.cpp && make -C build/make/rise -j8 all && make -C build/make/rise -j8 build-test/PoleWeldingWatertightnessTest && ./bin/tests/PoleWeldingWatertightnessTest`, then `git checkout <slice-HEAD> -- <File>` to restore**: post-fix (all three) 57/0; full revert (Sphere+CircularDisk+Ellipsoid all to `a4495f94`) 47/10; Sphere-only revert 54/3 (284 raw-index boundary edges at detail=71 reproduces exactly); CircularDisk-only revert 53/4 (8 degenerate triangles reproduces exactly); Ellipsoid-only revert 54/3 (same 284-boundary-edge shape as Sphere). The 47/10 full-revert figure is exactly additive (57 − 3 − 4 − 3 = 47), confirming the three fixes' red-proof checks are independent with no double-counting. The historical 29/3 and 38/4 figures were each measured against an EARLIER, smaller version of this test file (32 total checks before the CircularDisk section existed, 42 total before the Ellipsoid section existed) and so cannot be compared directly against the 57-total final file; a prior reviewer's own isolated re-measurement against the 42-total file (full revert 35/7, Sphere-only revert 39/3) is consistent with that reading (42−3−4=35) and is superseded here only by the larger final-file numbers above, not contradicted by them. Pole/center rows now collapse to ONE shared (position,normal,texcoord) index (no information lost -- texcoord was already canonicalized to `(0,v)` for every column there); the general u=0/u=1 seam is deliberately left duplicated (`DisplacedGeometry`'s per-vertex pipeline requires `iVertices[k]==iCoords[k]`) and its residual boundary-edge count is filed as **DL-136**. `tests/MeshInteriorSignalTest.cpp` sections (f)/(f2): a tessellated Sphere/Ellipsoid now ANSWERS `SignedDistanceLower` (genuinely watertight via DL-31/DL-143's own position-weld) where the pre-fix pole-cell degenerate triangles made it refuse; that section's earlier pin of "still refuses post-weld" was itself a stale fixture written against the unfixed `SphereGeometry` and has been corrected in the same commit. See [DL-20/DL-116 closure](DL20_DL116_PATCH_CURVATURE_AND_POLE_WELDING.md). | S | precision | user-visible (a scene author who runs `TessellateToMesh` output through `interior(r)` -- directly, or via any future consumer that needs a certified-closed mesh -- no longer silently loses the signed side on a tessellated sphere, disk, or ellipsoid) |

with a diagnostic naming the substrate and the reason ("is spectrally defined ... bind ... DIRECTLY to a colour slot instead"), following DL-32's hard-fail-the-chunk convention -- the SAME diagnostic channel (`GlobalLog`/`g_cstFinalizeDiagSink`) every other attach-time Finalize failure uses. | ~~DL-150~~ | ~~(new; found by round-2 review of the `debt-prox` slice, no source-doc heading)~~ | ~~`TriangleMeshGeometryIndexed::WeldVertexPositions` merges any two vertices within `eps = max(1e-9, 1e-6*bboxDiagonal)` before the watertightness edge tally, so two GENUINELY DISTINCT open sheets closer than eps ... can be stitched into one falsely-closed 2-manifold~~ | CLOSED 2026-09-18 (`debt-dlweld`, round 2) -- MITIGATED, not eliminated; see the residual below | Red-proof (before ANY DL-150 code, reproduced fresh against `master` `4b692be6`): two COINCIDENT triangles (no internal diagonal, so no confound with the pre-existing DL-31 edge count -- see the round-2 correction below) plus a remote vertex inflating the bbox diagonal to ~1732, built through the real `BeginIndexedTriangles`/`DoneIndexedTriangles`/`SignedDistanceLower` path (`tests/MeshInteriorSignalTest.cpp` sections (m)/(n), gap=0.001 < eps~1.7e-3): `SignedDistanceLower` returned `ok=true` (falsely certified watertight) for both an opposite-winding and a same-winding pair -- 2 of 100 checks failed against that commit. **Round 1 (2026-09-17, STRUCK, see below) shipped an orientation-based discriminator that was ITSELF WRONG** and was fully replaced, not patched, in round 2 (2026-09-18) after independent review: `ComputeWatertightness` now instead refuses certification when the position weld produces two geometrically COINCIDENT triangles -- two topologically DISTINCT triangles (by authoring origin) that resolve to the SAME three post-weld vertex ids (an unordered-set match for an opposite-winding false stitch, an exact ordered match when the windings also happen to agree) -- detected by sorting each triangle's own post-weld id triple and scanning the full sorted list for adjacent duplicates (`O(T log T)`, no per-vertex or per-face normal computation at all). A legitimate closed mesh never has two distinct faces occupying the same three vertices, at ANY dihedral angle from a hairline crease to a near-flat seam, which is exactly why this signal has no known false-positive case (unlike orientation, see below). Red-proof against round 1's own orientation code (`1c0bf3ae`): a closed, correctly-wound, REAL-per-face-normal triangular-prism wedge (`tests/MeshInteriorSignalTest.cpp` section (p)) at apex angles 30/50/58/60/70 degrees -- ALL FIVE angles were falsely refused by round 1's own fixture (worse than the reviewer's own report of "only <=58 degrees refuse", because this fixture's per-face-duplicated vertices additionally coincide at the shared apex edge, enlarging the weld group the orientation scan sees); all five certify correctly post-round-2, with the signed depth matching the closed-form `0.5*tan(apexDeg/2)/sqrt(tan(apexDeg/2)^2+1)` to 1e-9. Post-round-2: `MeshInteriorSignalTest`: 100/0 (was 78/0 after round 1; grew by sections (p) wedge, (q) flat-cube-with-real-normals, (r) thin-valid-solid). No shipped glTF asset or tessellator is affected: the same four DL-143 assets (Avocado, DragonAttenuation's two primitives, SheenChair's four primitives, NormalTangentTest) reproduce their exact boundary/non-manifold counts through the real `GLTFSceneImporter`, with no "COINCIDENT" diagnostic firing for any of them. Cost (CPU-time via `clock()`, isolated pre-/post-fix library A/B of `DoneIndexedTriangles` alone, median of 20-40 repeats -- wall-clock was tried first and rejected, it swung 2x run to run from unrelated system scheduling noise on this shared machine): round 2 is INDISTINGUISHABLE FROM the true `master` baseline (no DL-150 code at all) within measurement noise on both a 48401-vertex synthetic watertight sphere (~970-975 ns/vertex round 2 vs ~991-1011 ns/vertex baseline, i.e. round 2 reads slightly FASTER, within noise) and the 76809-vertex Dragon asset (~807-819 ns/vertex round 2 vs ~851-853 ns/vertex baseline) -- markedly cheaper than round 1's own measured +8.3%/+8.9% overhead, since sorting id triples needs no cross products or per-position vector accumulation at all. **Round-1 STRUCK, 2026-09-18 (independent review P1)**: round 1's cosine `< -0.5` (120 degree) threshold, justified at the time by "measured minimum cosine 0 at a flat-shaded cube corner" and "comfortably below a genuinely opposed pair (cosine -1)", is FALSE for a genuinely closed solid with a sharp CONVEX crease -- a symmetric wedge's two side-face outward normals have cosine EXACTLY `-cos(apexDeg)`, so a real, correctly-wound, closed prism with apex angle below 60 degrees reads a cosine below -0.5, IDENTICAL in sign and rough magnitude to the false-stitch signature the threshold was meant to catch; there is no fixed orientation threshold that works, since a genuine knife edge's cosine approaches -1 as apexDeg -> 0. The flat-shaded-cube control that seemed to validate round 1's threshold was itself a red herring: it went through `BuildMesh()`'s helper, which stamps a `(0,0,1)` PLACEHOLDER normal on every vertex regardless of face, so it never exercised a real per-face crease. **Documented, NOT fixed, residual** (unchanged in kind from round 1, re-verified against the new mechanism): two independently-tessellated sheets closer than `eps` that are NOT triangle-coincident after the weld (e.g. two quads split along DIFFERENT internal diagonals) still weld into an equally false 2-manifold this check cannot see -- every edge still reads count 2, and no two triangles share all three vertices (`tests/MeshInteriorSignalTest.cpp` section (o), a CONTROL, not a red-proof target, `ok=true` both before and after). A stronger guarantee needs an independent geometric test (self-intersection, or distinct connected-component volume enclosure), out of scope for this cheap, build-time check; see the updated `ComputeWatertightness`/`WeldVertexPositions` function comments in `TriangleMeshGeometryIndexed.cpp` and `docs/CROSS_OBJECT_PROXIMITY_DESIGN.md` §10. **Round-3 review note (2026-09-18):** a second, undocumented refusal mode -- a genuinely closed solid and a separate isolated double-sided decal merged into ONE TriangleMeshGeometryIndexed object (plausible for glTF exports that merge disjoint parts into one primitive) has its entire certification refused by the decal's coincident pair; not observed in the 21-asset corpus (0 coincident-triangle diagnostics over 120 weld operations), proximity() degrades to unsigned-only. The different-diagonal two-sheet residual is now DL-197. | S | correctness-hazard | user-visible only on multi-scale content whose distinct sheets sit closer than one part in 10^6 of the mesh bounding-box diagonal AND are triangle-coincident after the weld (the differently-tessellated case above remains an open, documented residual; no shipped asset affected either way; a genuine sharp convex crease of ANY angle is no longer at risk of false refusal, closing round 1's own P1) |
| DL-194 | (new; found by the DL-164 closure's own structural-guard requirement, no source-doc heading) | `ExpressionProgram::BindPainterRefs` takes a RAW addref on the `IPainter*`/`IScalarPainter*` a `sample()`/`sample_scalar()` call resolves to, ONCE, at attach time, with no re-resolution hook; that is safe today ONLY because `Cst.cpp`'s `DeriveToJobIncremental` refuses every `ChunkCategory::Painter` chunk outright (its `default:` arm), so the only way a program's bindings are ever rebuilt is a full `DeriveToJob`, which always reconstructs the `ExpressionProgram` (and re-runs `BindPainterRefs`) from scratch. If a future change ever admits Painter-category chunks to the incremental path, an in-place re-Finalize of a SAMPLED painter chunk would leave every OTHER program's already-resolved `sample()`/`sample_scalar()` pointer referencing the OLD (possibly freed) painter object -- silently, since a raw addref cannot detect its target being replaced. Not reachable today: `DeriveToJobIncremental`'s wholesale Painter refusal is a load-bearing accident, not a targeted guard, but it is unconditional and every code path that could trip this is dead. | OPEN 2026-09-18 (guarded, not fixed) | `Cst.cpp`'s incremental `switch(cat)` (~:3693-3697) lists only `Material, Geometry, Object, Light, Modifier` as handled categories; `Painter` falls to `default:` and refuses with "incremental cannot fully drop this category". `ExpressionEval.h`'s `BindPainterRefs` doc comment (added this slice) states the hazard and names the two remediation shapes a future incremental-Painter admission must pick between. `tests/CstIncrementalSafetyTest.cpp` gained two DL-164 consistency-pin cases (a `sample()`-using `expression_painter` closure and a `sample_scalar()`-using `scalar_painter` closure, each asserting `applied==0`, `diagCount>0`, dump unchanged) -- both green before AND after this slice's DL-164 fix (the incremental refusal logic itself is untouched), so they are consistency pins, not a red-proof of a live bug; they exist to fail loudly if a future change relaxes the refusal without addressing this row. | S | latent (architectural hazard; unreachable while the incremental refusal holds) | not user-visible today; would become a wrong-render bug (not a load failure) the day `DeriveToJobIncremental` admits Painter-category chunks -- fix = re-run `BindPainterRefs` on every program referencing a re-Finalized painter (needs a colour/scalar-painter reverse-dependency walk, e.g. via `kColorPainterSubCat`/`kScalarPainterSubCat`), or refuse the incremental path specifically for a chunk that is the target of any live `sample()`/`sample_scalar()` binding |
| DL-195 | (new; promoted from existing chunk-descriptor/CLAUDE.md prose to a tracked row per the DL-164/DL-165 closure's own recipe, no source-doc heading) | `sample_scalar(name)` reads ONLY `ScalarPainterRGB::kSingleSampleChannel` (549 nm) of the bound `IScalarPainter`'s RGB-wavelength triple, so a genuinely wavelength-VARYING scalar painter (a Sellmeier/piecewise-linear/polynomial dispersion curve bound to e.g. `ior`) reads ACHROMATIC through `sample_scalar()` even though the SAME painter bound directly to a scalar slot renders dispersively (per-channel, DL-82's RGB convention). This is a DELIBERATE, already-documented single-representative-channel choice (matching every other single-scalar consumer in the codebase, per `ScalarPainterRGB`'s own convention) rather than a newly-discovered defect, and DL-165 did NOT touch it -- `IScalarPainter` has no `GetColorNM`/spectrum concept to begin with, so there is no analogous "collapse a real SPD" failure mode here, only a coarser one (one channel of three, not one of a continuum). No code changed for this row this slice. | S | physics-bias (spectral fidelity, scalar pipe), documented | user-visible only to an author who binds a genuinely dispersive scalar_painter behind `sample_scalar()` and expects per-channel variation; fix (if ever pursued) = a per-wavelength or per-RGB-channel `sample_scalar()` return shape, which needs a vec3-of-scalars type the current VM does not have -- deliberately out of scope here, same reasoning DL-165's own row gave for not extending the vec3 VM to a per-wavelength opcode |
| ~~DL-154~~ | ~~(new; found by the debt-cleanup master gates, no source-doc heading)~~ | ~~`AutoRasterizerTest`'s Tier-2 render-time probe for `scenes/FeatureBased/Combined/glass_pavilion.RISEscene` (`p4_glass`, expected `AutoIntegratorChoice::VCM` by the 'median-lum ~2.3x AND reach ~20-32x' refractive-caustic gates) now resolves to PT on `master`~~ CLOSED 2026-09-17 (debt-dl154 slice) — the test's own expectation was stale, not the router. | CLOSED — `AutoRasterizerTest` now asserts `glass_pavilion` never routes VCM (`CheckProbeRouteNotVCM`, not a strict PT assertion -- the probe's sigma2T fallback occasionally resolves BDPT instead of PT, ~1-in-5 runs, the same coin flip `jewel_vault` documents) and is 153/0 (10/10 runs deterministic); see recipe entry | **CORRECTED 2026-09-17 (post-review):** an initial merge-only bisect (first-parent MERGES from `fc371041`) wrongly pinned the whole collapse on `14bc2cb6` ("Merge debt30-eta2"). Review caught that a merge-only bisect SKIPS every non-merge commit sequence between two merges, and `14bc2cb6`'s actual first parent is the non-merge commit `b6c12301` (end of the debt28-thinlens sequence), not a merge at all. Re-bisected over first-parent COMMITS (not merges) in a fresh scratch worktree, 128x128/64spp/box/no-OIDN/EXR-linear: `185b0d5f` (fisheye-differentials merge) reads VCM/PT ≈ 26.1-26.3x; `b6c12301` (debt28's own end point, still `14bc2cb6`'s parent) is ALREADY at ≈1.02-1.04x; `14bc2cb6` itself moves it to ≈0.97-1.10x (noise, not a further collapse). **The real proximate fix for `glass_pavilion` is `c3c37e08`** ("fix(bdpt,vcm): sample the camera APERTURE for t==1 -- thin-lens splats were 1/(cos*A_lens) too bright", debt 28): `glass_pavilion` uses `thinlens_camera fstop 1.4` at 85mm (`1/A_lens ≈ 345`), and the pre-fix VCM max-luminance outliers (2045-2524 at this probe config) are camera-splat fireflies from the un-normalized aperture term — `debt30-eta2` is unrelated to this scene's collapse and moves its own reach number by only ~1%. Root cause (glass_pavilion): the thin-lens t==1 camera splat lacked the `1/(cos*A_lens)` normalization its aperture integral requires, so every VCM light-subpath vertex connecting directly to the lens (any pixel with a visible light source through the glass) was inflated by `A_lens ≈ 1/345`, i.e. ~345x too bright on that splat family -- exactly what the raw max values show. **`diamond_teapot_pour` (a SEPARATE, independently-bisected mechanism -- the review's own hedge that this scene is "plausibly genuinely debt30-eta2's" was checked and REFUTED):** `diamond_teapot_pour` uses a PINHOLE camera (debt28/thin-lens literally cannot touch it), and re-measuring it at every point on the `glass_pavilion` bisect chain (`185b0d5f`, `b6c12301`, `14bc2cb6`) reads a STABLE ≈1.10-1.11x at ALL THREE -- already fully collapsed even at `185b0d5f`, before either debt28 or debt30-eta2 exist in its ancestry as far as this scene is concerned. A second, INDEPENDENT bisection (first-parent commits, `5b117557..185b0d5f`, 245 commits, binary search) pins its actual collapse to a SINGLE commit: **`7889fa29`** ("fix(integrators): BDPT/VCM vertex connectibility is a surface property, not a per-draw one; VCM delta-light NEE MIS partition closed (debts 23-24, 25-26 opened)") -- the commit immediately before it (`20dd58ad`) reads 1.84-1.92x, matching the original 1.88-1.95x calibration; `7889fa29` itself reads 1.09-1.10x. `diamond_teapot_pour` has 5 `spot_light`s (delta/positional lights); this commit's own message ("VCM delta-light NEE MIS partition closed") is exactly the mechanism -- VCM was over-counting delta-light NEE illumination before this fix, the same "VCM keeps turning out to over-count" pattern as debt28 and debt30-eta2, just a different specific bug, months earlier (dated within the `debt25-box-selfhit`/cloth-fabric arc per CLAUDE.md's cloth/fabric design entry, NOT the `debt-cleanup` campaign fc371041 onward). Ruling: **(a)** for `glass_pavilion` -- the fixture's expected value was calibrated to a pre-fix artifact (debt28's un-normalized aperture splat) and is re-derived from current measurement, not the router code. Corroborating evidence: `docs/UNIFIED_INTEGRATOR_BASELINES.md` §5's own RMSE-decisive table has ALWAYS listed `glass_pavilion`'s true winner as PT (unlike sibling `diamond_teapot`, VCM); production-spp render evidence (256x256, PT vs VCM, `oidn_denoise FALSE`, `pixel_filter box`, 64 and 256 spp) shows p99 luminance agreeing to <6% across all 3 channels, with PT's own channel-0 max a classic MC firefly (grows with spp, different pixel each render) rather than missed energy. Sibling audit: `diamond_teapot_pour` shows the IDENTICAL shape of collapse (a real caustic scene's VCM/PT ratio falling to ~1.1x) via a DIFFERENT, independently-pinned VCM over-count fix (`7889fa29`, not debt28 and not debt30-eta2) -- unlike `glass_pavilion`, `UNIFIED_INTEGRATOR_BASELINES.md` lists ITS true winner as VCM, so its now-PT/BDPT route is a genuine untested regression risk, not a fix; opened as **DL-167** (updated to record both mechanisms, since any discriminator redesign must account for both). Doc updates: `docs/AUTO_RASTERIZER_DESIGN.md` §5 accuracy table + prose, §6.2's Phase-4 sweep table, §6.2.1 (DL-154 status block corrected to name both proximate commits), §6.2.2, §6.2.3's firefly-robust table; `tests/AutoRasterizerTest.cpp` comment corrected. **Lesson:** bisect over first-parent COMMITS, not merges -- a merge-only bisect silently skips every non-merge sequence between two merges, and can land the blame on whichever merge happens to be adjacent to the real fix rather than the fix itself; when two scenes collapse together under one merge-level bisect point, re-check whether they actually share a mechanism before writing one root-cause paragraph for both. | M | correctness (auto-routing) | user-visible: `auto_rasterizer` now correctly avoids VCM for `glass_pavilion` (mostly PT, occasionally BDPT via the sigma2T fallback), matching the project's own independent RMSE ground truth; no renderer or router code changed |
