| ~~DL-176~~ | ~~(new; found by round-2 review of the `debt-phongpdf` slice, pre-existing, no source-doc heading)~~ | ~~`tests/SPFPdfConsistencyTest.cpp`'s `CookTorrance_BlackDiffuse @ 60deg` chi-squared gate reads 927.52 against the critical value 928.261 (dof 799; +3.22 sd) -- 99.92% of critical, the most fragile gate in a file the phongpdf slice just made more load-bearing (its Phong/Ashikmin chi2 rows sit at -1.3..+1.6 sd across seeds). The harness is deterministic in practice only because `RandomNumberGenerator rng;` seeds from an un-`srand`ed libc `rand()`; any change to rand() consumption upstream, or a seeded run, can flip it.~~ | CLOSED 2026-09-18 | Reviewer replicated the harness (500k draws, 20x40 bins, 4x4 sub-integration, merge-at-5) at three fresh seeds for the phongpdf rows (all within +/-1.6 sd) and read the CookTorrance row's 927.52 from the suite's own output. **CLOSED 2026-09-18 (`debt-dl176` slice, commit `fa0fae7e`).** Not a `CookTorranceSPF` defect and not seed luck: a 10-seed sweep on the UNFIXED harness puts that row's true mean at **938.26 (z = +11.02)**, i.e. ABOVE critical, with `_BlackSpecular @ 60deg` at +4.82 and `_BlackDiffuse @ 30deg` at +6.36 while `Lambertian @ 30deg` reads -0.36. The cause is in the harness: Part 3 built its expected counts as `(4x4 sub-integral of Pdf over bin i) * totalAccepted` and NEVER normalised them to the observed total, so any deviation of `int Pdf` from exactly 1 appears as a uniform relative excess in every group and injects `totalAccepted * eps^2/(1+eps)` of pure normalisation artifact -- about 100 chi2 on that row. `CookTorranceSPF::Pdf` is a CORRECT SUB-density there (`int Pdf = 0.98463` against a measured emission rate of `0.984664`, agreeing to 3e-5: the SPF genuinely emits nothing on 1.5% of calls at 60 deg, when the internally-selected lobe's `kray` is the authored literal black). Fixed at the formulation, three parts: (a) rescale so `sum expected == totalAccepted`, making Part 3 a pure SHAPE test -- the one degree of freedom this costs was ALREADY being subtracted ("Lose 1 DOF because total count is fixed"), so the old code paid for a constraint it never imposed, and total MASS stays gated by Part 2; (b) fold the trailing partial merge group (the grazing horizon bins) into the last completed group instead of discarding it; (c) every RNG takes an EXPLICIT seed and the statistic is evaluated at `NUM_CHI2_SEEDS = 8`, gated on the seed-MEAN via `z = (mean - dof)/sqrt(2 dof/N)` ~ N(0,1), band `|z| <= 4` (~6e-5 per row, ~0.16% over the 26 gated rows) -- which shrinks a systematic offset's error bar by sqrt(N) instead of hiding it inside a 3.23-sd single-draw threshold whose false-alarm rate is 0.1% per row per run. Post-fix every gated row is within `|z| <= 4`, worst +3.58, and the filed row reads **z = +0.54 with nothing in `CookTorranceSPF` changed**. Runtime 3.7s -> 18.1s. Each row now also prints its measured emission rate against `int Pdf` (the DL-98/DL-99 sub-density contract, made visible); that column identifies the one genuine residual, **opened as DL-211**. See [DL177_WARD_DENSITY_AND_KRAY.md](DL177_WARD_DENSITY_AND_KRAY.md) section 8. | S | test-fragility | not user-visible; fix = derive why the CookTorrance black-diffuse 60deg distribution sits 3.2 sd off (a real small density defect in CookTorranceSPF at grazing, or an under-resolved bin at the horizon) rather than widening alpha; add a per-row seed sweep to the harness |

Red-proof (`tests/OptimalMISTrainingSitesTest.cpp`, `RunNeeCastRRTrainingFold`): a receiver sphere under a uniform environment at `importance=0.005` (exact deterministic `pSurvive=0.5`/`rrCompensation=2.0`), matched per-sample against a baseline run at `importance=1.0` that pre-consumes the identical throwaway RNG draw `CastRay`'s own roulette would make, isolating the compensation factor exactly: pre-fix ratio `test/baseline = 1.0` (103/1 failing); post-fix ratio `= 4.0` exactly (matching `rrCompensation^2`), ties preserved (2044/2044 attempts both sides). `OptimalMISTrainingSitesTest`: 104/0 (was 97/0 before this row's test was added). | ~~DL-177~~ | ~~(new; DL-127's sibling audit, `debt-dl127` slice, 2026-09-18; source doc: [DL127_SCHLICK_KRAY_VS_BRDF.md](DL127_SCHLICK_KRAY_VS_BRDF.md) section 6)~~ | ~~`WardIsotropicGaussianSPF` and `WardAnisotropicEllipticalGaussianSPF` carry the DL-127 pattern: each lobe's `kray` is the reflectance painter's own direction-INDEPENDENT `GetColor(ri)`, not that lobe's `f_I cos / p_I`, so the sampled continuation and `Ward*BRDF::value` integrate different functions.~~ | CLOSED 2026-09-18 | `tests/SchlickKrayBRDFConsistencyTest.cpp` section 6 (non-gated, printed every run): with the diffuse painter BLACK so `value()` IS the specular term, `MaxValue(kray) * pdf / (MaxValue(value(wo)) * cos)` over 20000 real `Scatter()` calls reads, at 0/30/60/80 deg incidence, **1.100053 / 1.385029 / 2.950590 / 5.878197** (isotropic, alpha 0.3) and **1.056958 / 1.324292 / 2.968491 / 6.071721** (anisotropic, alphaX 0.3 alphaY 0.12), against the exact 1.000000 the fixed `SchlickSPF` control and both Phong SPFs read on the same probe; per-draw maxima reach 685x and 1085x at 80 deg. `SPFBSDFConsistencyTest` already carries a 25 % furnace tolerance for both, attributed there to Ward's own non-energy-conservation -- part of that gap is this. NOT fixed in the `debt-dl127` slice deliberately: closing it needs the same TWO-SIDED change DL-127 needed, and `Ward*::Pdf` is a raw-albedo-weighted mixture PRECISELY BECAUSE the krays are currently direction-independent (that is why DL-98/DL-99 recorded Ward as immune to the selection-weight pattern), so both classes would need the DL-67/DL-98/DL-99 aggregate-`Pdf` quadrature construction built for them; and the corrected weight carries Ward's unbounded `1/sqrt(nl nv)` tail, which needs an answer of its own before it goes in front of a path tracer. Recipe: build the aggregate `Pdf` first (gate it the DL-98/DL-99 way: full-sphere normalisation vs measured emission probability AND total variation vs a real-`Scatter`+`RandomlySelect` histogram), then set `kray_I = f_I cos / p_I` and re-run section 6 -- both rows must land on 1.000000 -- then re-derive `SPFBSDFConsistencyTest`'s 25 % Ward tolerances downward and measure the `scenes/` Ward renders. **Review correction (2026-09-18, Opus round-1 review of debt-dl127):** the headline 5.878/6.071 ratios were measured THROUGH a broken stored pdf: `WardIsotropicGaussianSPF`/`WardAnisotropicEllipticalGaussianSPF` sample the half-vector by `tan(theta) = alpha*sqrt(-ln xi)`, whose true solid-angle density is `exp(-tan^2/alpha^2)/(pi alpha^2 cos^3 theta_h)`, but `GenerateSpecularRay`/`WardIsotropicPdf` (and the anisotropic twin at ~:138/:318) store `cos(theta_h)*exp(...)/(pi alpha^2)` -- the stored per-lobe pdf equals the true density times `cos^4(theta_h)` (per-draw ratio to 7e-14 over 9 configs); `Pdf` with a black diffuse integrates to 0.9806/0.8563/0.6135 at alpha .1/.3/.6 against measured emission 1.000/1.000/0.938. The missing kray factor against the TRUE density is `(h.wo) cos^3(theta_h) sqrt(nl/nv)`; against the STORED one it is `(h.wo) sqrt(nl/nv)/cos(theta_h)`, which is what 5.878 measures (near-correct only at grazing where h ~ n). `SPFPdfConsistencyTest`'s Ward comments ("the Ward model is not energy-conserving... does not fully integrate to 1", 10% tolerance) are the same stale mis-attribution; they pass only because those rows run at alpha .2/.3 where cos^4 ~ 0.94. The recipe below (aggregate Pdf first, gated by full-sphere normalisation vs measured emission) catches this automatically -- fix the stored density FIRST, then the kray. **CLOSED 2026-09-18 (`debt-dl176` slice, commits `e567c9e8` red-proof / `ebf80e94` fix / `00a48fb3` tolerances).** All THREE defects the review correction names, fixed in dependency order; `tests/WardDensityKrayTest.cpp` **352 / 0**, red **138 / 214** (both of the CURRENT file against two libraries; an earlier revision of this row quoted 108/158 -> 332/0, which compared two DIFFERENT files -- the fix commit grew the test by 80 lines and the review round added more). (1) STORED DENSITY -- the true solid-angle density of the sampler's own half-vector is `exp(-tan^2/a^2)/(PI a^2 cos^3 theta_h)` (anisotropic: `a^2 -> ax ay` and `tan^2/a^2 -> tan^2 D(phi)`, whose `D` cancels against the warp's own `p_Phi = 1/(2 PI ax ay D)`), and the stored value was that times `cos^4(theta_h)` -- measured EXACTLY so per draw (`worst |ratio - cos^4| = 0.000000` at every alpha, incidence and model). (2) AGGREGATE Pdf -- rebuilt with the DL-67/DL-98/DL-99 construction (`C_D` from a deterministic 16x16 stratified replay of the specular sampler's own `(xi1,xi2)` square, dominated by its REJECTION rate; per-lane `q_S(wo)` modulated by Malley's exact clipped-cosine diffuse-accept fraction; the per-channel branch handled by closed-form inversion of the query direction back to the shared random pair). Ward needs NO second quadrature (its diffuse `kray` is the constant `Rd`, so `q_S` depends on the query direction alone) and escapes DL-99's reduction-order trap structurally (`MaxValue(Rs * R) == MaxValue(Rs) * R`, `R` being a nonnegative SCALAR). (3) KRAY -- `kray_S = Rs (h.wo) cos^3(theta_h) sqrt(cos_o/cos_i)`, the whole roughness dependence cancelling exactly as in DL-127. Full-sphere `int Pdf` vs measured emission probability: worst `|diff|` **0.420490 -> 0.004749** over 16 rows (chromatic, back-face and per-channel included); TVD vs a 600k-draw real `Scatter`+`RandomlySelect` histogram **0.050515 -> 0.008660**; `kray p / (f cos)` **exact 1.000000 on all 64 rows** (RGB, NM, per-channel, both models), against pre-fix means up to 8.68 and per-draw maxima to 631; and `E[sum kray]` now lands on an independent 400x800 quadrature of `int max(value()) cos dw` with `E/Q` in **[0.9925, 1.0066]** over 42 configurations. **THE TAIL: decision (a), leave the weight as `f cos/p`, on data.** The row's "unbounded `1/sqrt(nl nv)`" premise is half wrong -- the `nl` half cancels against the cosine and the density, so `kray <= Rs/sqrt(cos_i)`, a per-shading-point CONSTANT; measured per-draw maxima 0.486 / 0.602 / 1.198 / 3.687 at 80 / 85 / 89 / 89.9 deg against bounds 1.200 / 1.694 / 3.785 / 11.968, with the furnace estimator's relative s.e. `<= 0.0040` at every one of the 42 configurations and p99.9 sitting at the max rather than far above it. (The "685x / 1085x per draw" figures were measured THROUGH the broken pdf; through the fixed one the same probe reads 1.000000.) Options (b) and (c) declined with reasons: the Ward-Duer / Geisler-Moroder-Duer bounded weight would have to change `Ward*BRDF::value` in lockstep or it re-creates DL-127's defect, i.e. it is a MODEL SUBSTITUTION that changes the shipped look of `ward_*_material`; a clamp would lose energy for no measured benefit. Tolerances re-derived DOWNWARD rather than left slack: `SPFBSDFConsistencyTest` 0.25 -> 0.01 on both rows (relErr 8.35% / 19.33% / 10.05% / 20.26% -> 0.0050% / 0.0255% / 0.0278% / 0.0177%, with the quadrature column UNCHANGED -- which is what refutes the old "quadrature under-integrates" half of the note, while "MC pre" pinned at 0.79-0.80 on every row is the signature of `kray` literally being the constant 0.8 reflectance); `SPFPdfConsistencyTest` chi2 UN-SKIPPED on both rows and the anisotropic 10% integral band retired (`int Pdf` 0.973145 / 0.963752 / 0.959258 / 0.949217 -> 0.999611 / 0.999912 / 0.999500 / 0.999978, chi2 seed-mean z +25.36 / +35.87 / +59.01 / +107.81 -> +0.837 / +0.302 / -0.084 / +0.059; the aniso 60deg integral row FAILED even the 5% band pre-fix, so the "relaxed to 10%" note was covering this and not anisotropy, and the `cos^4 ~ 0.94` at these rows' alpha 0.2/0.3 is exactly why mass stayed inside its band while the SHAPE was off by 25-108 sd); `SchlickKrayBRDFConsistencyTest` section 6 promoted from printed-only to a real per-draw gate on every MULTI-EMIT row (537/0 -> 557/0; **549/8** against the pre-fix library). Residual **DL-212** opened, not fixed: the MODEL's own grazing energy gain now reaches indirect transport (the Ward analogue of DL-178) -- specular directional albedo up to 1.99x `Rs` at 89.9 deg. **Review round 1 (2026-09-18) corrected the render ATTRIBUTION, which was the slice's one P1.** The claim that `kaleidoscope_atrium`'s -0.803 % showed a `pixelpel_rasterizer` scene moving through NEE's `IMaterial::Pdf()` weight was WRONG: all six objects binding its single Ward chunk declare `shader glossy_shader`, which is `shaderop pt` ALONE (deliberately no `DefaultDirectLighting`), so `DirectLightingShaderOp` never runs on a Ward surface there and the row is the KRAY correction, same mechanism as the PT cornellbox row. The `Pdf`-through-NEE mechanism is real (`LightSampler::EvaluateDirectLighting` computes `p_bsdf = pMaterial->Pdf(vToLight, ri, misIOR)`) and is now MEASURED rather than asserted, on the sharpest available discriminator: `scenes/Tests/Materials/materials.RISEscene` -- the ONE genuinely direct-only Ward scene in the shipped set (a single `standard_shader global` carrying only `DefaultDirectLighting`) -- re-rendered at 256 spp, n=5 per build, reads a **measured NULL** on both Ward teapots (+0.0002 % / -0.0004 %, t = +0.3 / -0.5, sigma 5e-6 so a 0.1 % effect would read t ~ 130), and the reason is structural: its only light is a `directional_light`, a DELTA light with no MIS partner, so the delta arm never evaluates `Pdf()` at all. The SAME scene with ONLY the light swapped for a `clippedplane_geometry` + `lambertian_luminaire_material` area emitter (512 spp, n=5) moves the ward-isotropic teapot **-4.524 % (t = -422)** and the ward-anisotropic one **-5.535 % (t = -975)**, while its Lambertian control reads +0.0009 % (t = +0.3) and its Schlick control -0.041 % (t = -2.2, a 2-sigma fluctuation at n=5). So a direct-only Ward surface is immune under a delta light and moves 4.5-5.5 % under an area light. Round 1 also added: a Part 2b SUB-DENSITY gate to `SPFPdfConsistencyTest` (full-sphere `int Pdf` vs measured emission probability, AND the upper-hemisphere SHARE of each -- the second is what catches `Translucent`'s 1.80x DL-41 domain split, now pinned two-sided at 0.44469 in [0.40, 0.48] rather than tolerated; the review's own reading of that row as a MASS mismatch was comparing a hemisphere-restricted acceptance count against a hemisphere integral, and its full-sphere mass in fact agrees to 4.1e-5); a two-sided DL-211 pin on `CookTorrance_BlackSpecular`'s chi2 z ([3.0,4.0] at 60 deg, [1.5,2.7] at 30 deg); 12 TILTED rows covering the previously-untested `aD < 1` arm (worst |int Pdf - emission| 0.00797, worst TVD 0.00848); an explicit seed hook on `SPFBSDFConsistencyTest` with an 8-seed sweep (worst Ward relErr **0.0757 %** against the 1 % gate, 0 failures at any seed); and a non-gated tail-CONTEXT table putting Ward's max/mean of **7.11 / 6.62** between `GGXSPF`'s 1.59 and the post-DL-127 `SchlickSPF`'s **312.5** -- a tail that already ships. The scene's pinhole camera is at `(0, 0.3, 6.5)` and its ONLY light is the plane `z = 6.5` spanning `x, y in [-1.4, 1.4]` -- the camera is INSIDE the emitter's own surface. Every camera ray leaves that plane immediately, so the emitter's only root along one is the mathematically ZERO root at the ray's own origin, and a pinhole in an infinitely thin emitter sees it edge-on. Pre-`d01a320a` the acceptance test was the bare `hit.dRange > 0` and `computet` recovers that zero root as a last-bit rounding residue of `(srfpos.z - origin.z)/dir.z` whose SIGN is a coin flip: ~6.2% of camera samples registered a full-radiance emitter hit at `t ~ 1e-15` -- this branch's own pre-fix build renders the as-authored frame at 0.180096, from which the spurious fraction solves to f = 6.24%, 1.2% under the 0.182353 the scenario recorded at `c33734c7` (the difference being ~1066 commits of unrelated shading work plus the run-to-run variation of a last-bit coin flip). Decisive reference-free evidence (two renders, no bisect): nudging the camera 0.001 off the plane -- 1/2800 of the quad's own width -- collapses the pre-fix 0.180096 to 0.001549 on the SAME build, and OFF the plane the pre-fix and HEAD builds agree to four significant figures (0.001713 vs 0.001714 at z=6.2), so the floor costs this scene's NEE/BSDF transport nothing. Closed-form ceiling: even with perfectly white spheres this scene cannot exceed meanLuma ~0.02 by lighting alone, so 0.18 was never reachable without the emitter being in frame; and the luminaire itself measures `exitance*scale/pi` to 4e-6 relative. THE DISPOSAL RULING, which this row left open: a delta Snell lobe has no distribution to renormalize, so DL-68's clip does not apply -- of mirroring the Snell result across the geometric plane (not a refraction about any normal; destroys a dispersion fan's ordering), re-labelling it a reflection (moves flux between channels at a rate unrelated to Fresnel, and emits a SECOND near-parallel delta reflection alongside the one already there), dropping it (deterministic energy loss with nothing to renormalize into -- DL-112's defect on purpose), and RE-DERIVING the refraction about the true geometric normal, only the last preserves the energy exactly, keeps the event a genuine refraction at the same eta, and cannot fail the gate (a refraction about N always lands on the far side of N, so no re-check is needed). It is also exactly what these same functions already did for their own mandatory TIR reflections. Reciprocity is NOT preserved by any of the four -- the selecting predicate is evaluated on the forward direction alone and is not symmetric under path reversal -- so the choice is made on energy and on consistency with each file's own treatment; see [the closure doc](DL111_DL112_TRANSMISSION_PUSH_GATES.md) section 3. FOUR CORRECTIONS/ADDITIONS to this row's own account. (a) For the DELTA lobes there are TWO reachable families, and the review's P2-1 corrected this sub-item -- an earlier revision of it (and of the source comments, the test derivation and tests/README.md) claimed "entering glass from air is safe at every tilt", which is FALSE. (a-i) While the tilted shading normal still OPPOSES the incoming ray (`Dot(d,n_s) < 0`), refraction into a DENSER medium indeed cannot land wrong-side (the transmitted direction is angularly BETWEEN the incoming ray and the shading normal's far side, both inside the convex crossing half-space), so under that premise the defect needs refraction into a RARER medium (a glass->air exit, or entry into a BUBBLE -- an `ior 1.0` object inside a glass block) AND the tilt and the refracted deviation to ADD: a grazing, normal-perturbed silhouette. `MakeObliqueHit` with a POSITIVE tilt builds it; measured 256/256 wrong-side at (delta 85, tilt 60) on all three SPFs. (a-ii) That premise FAILS at a bump-mapped silhouette. `Optics::CalculateRefractedRay` flips the normal internally to restore its sign convention, so once the tilt carries `n_s` PAST the grazing incoming ray (`Dot(d,n_s) > 0` -- `ReliefModifier` is explicitly NOT a horizon clamp) the refraction is built about `-n_s`, whose far side is the side the ray CAME FROM, and an ordinary air->glass ENTRY lands wrong-side: closed form at geomN=+Z, delta 89, tilt -30, ior 1.5, `t = (-0.911, 0, +0.412)`. Measured against `a4495f94` over `{80,85,89} x {-30,-45,-60}` (sub-test 2c, added by the review): `PerfectRefractorSPF` 64/64 wrong-side at all nine cells, and `DielectricSPF` emitted NOTHING at all (the companion Fresnel reflection is wrong-side too, so both lobes were dropped) -- total emitted energy **0.0000** instead of 1 at all nine. Pre-fix severity is therefore materially larger than this row originally recorded: ordinary glass ENTRY at a bump-mapped silhouette was 100% broken. (b) `DielectricSPF`'s `scattering`/HG warp escapes that theorem entirely and IS DL-68's clip case -- the warp is azimuthally uniform about the Snell axis at fixed polar angle, so drawing the azimuth on the valid arc is exact and costs the same single canonical number (`GeometricUtilities::PerturbClipped`, extracted from `TranslucentSPFDetail::SampleClippedPhong`). At `scattering 0` -- CLAUDE.md's "maximally DIFFUSE transmission, not off" -- `alpha = acos(u)` is a UNIFORM HEMISPHERE about the Snell direction and part of it was being deleted, on a FLAT surface at ZERO tilt.  HOW MUCH (review P2-3; an earlier revision of this sub-item said "roughly HALF", which overstates it ~2x): the deleted set is one hemisphere (axis: the Snell direction) minus another (axis: `throughSurface`), and two hemispheres whose axes subtend `theta_t` intersect in a lune of dihedral `pi - theta_t`, so the SURVIVING fraction is exactly `1 - theta_t/pi` -- verified against the real `acos(u)` + uniform-azimuth construction at 400k draws (entry 1->1.5 measured 0.9450/0.8433/0.7672 at incidence 15/45/89 deg vs closed form 0.9448/0.8437/0.7678; exit 1.5->1 measured 0.7297/0.5569 at 30/41 deg vs 0.7301/0.5569).  That is 0% at normal incidence, at most `theta_c/180 = 23.2%` on entry, and exactly 25.00% on exit averaged over the cosine-weighted SUB-CRITICAL interior population (the other 55.56% TIRs and emits no transmission at all -- `1 - sin^2(theta_c)` at n=1.5, the source of this row's "~56%"); 50% is only the `theta_t -> 90 deg` limit.  The row-3 numbers themselves are a snapshot lock and are unaffected: `LayeredWhiteFurnaceTest` row 3 moved `{0.3339,0.3044,0.3128,0.5324}` -> `{0.4271,0.4284,0.4569,0.6367}`, bit-identical across runs, still below 1 and closer to it at every angle (re-locked; 0 of 57 fail). (c) The `nEff`-anchored geometric reference in `DielectricSPF`/`PerfectRefractorSPF`, which each file's own comment called "provably equivalent to the ray-anchor rule used elsewhere", is NOT equivalent on a double-sided mesh: the geometry flips both normals toward the ray, so at an EXIT hit `nEff = -onb.w()` lands OUTWARD and the gate inverts -- 0/2048 reflection lobes emitted at tilt 0, 15 and 30 (100% of double-sided glass's internal Fresnel reflection dropped, no bump map involved) and 2048/2048 emitted pointing OUT of the solid past 45 deg while still carrying the interior stack. Both files now ray-anchor, the convention `a4fb884d` set for `GGXSPF`. (d) The wrong-side delta REFLECTIONS in all three files were DROPPED unless mandatory, paying `ref` to nothing; they now take the same re-derivation (on a shipped SSS material, `bAbsorbBackFace=true`, that delta reflection is the ONLY ray the SPF emits). The ROUGH VNDF reflection keeps its drop -- its `Pdf()` carries the matching gate, so the drop is the microfacet shadowing term, not an unmatched loss. Render: `scenes/Tests/SMS/sms_veach_egg_bumpmap.RISEscene` (relief-modifier dielectric egg, 200x200, 64 spp, `oidn_denoise FALSE`, `pixel_filter box`, EXR `Rec709RGB_Linear`, 6 renders per side against an ISOLATED build of `a4495f94`): full-frame mean luminance `1.019336 +/- 0.000285` -> `1.020587 +/- 0.000287`, **+0.1227%**, t=3.09 -- energy recovery, the same direction as the furnace. | ~~Exactly DL-68's pattern in three other files: a lobe that pushes (or carries a popped) `IORStack` is built from the SHADING normal, which a bump/normal map or `GlintModifier` (up to 60 deg) moves off the surface, so the continuation can travel to the side OPPOSITE the one the membership change claims. In each case the COMPANION Fresnel/reflection lobe in the same function IS correctly gated against a ray-anchored `geomN`, which is what makes the omission legible. (1) `DielectricSPF.cpp:213` `dielectric.ior_stack->push(rIndex)`; the transmitted direction's only gate is `Dot(dielectric.ray.Dir(), ri.onb.w())` (the SHADING normal) at the end of `GenerateScatteredRays*`, while `fresnel` is gated on `geomN` -- and the lobe is not delta: the `scattering`/HG warp `Perturb`s it by `alpha` off the Snell direction, so a wide `scattering` widens the wrong-side set. (2) `PerfectRefractorSPF.cpp:115` (RGB) and `:289` (NM) `specular.ior_stack->push(newIOR)` -- the refracted direction has NO geometric gate at all; only the Fresnel lobe is gated. DL-51). The `kray` the ray carries (`pTrans->GetColor(ri)`, or `front.kray*scat` for the backscatter) is unchanged, so the lobe's share of the material's total scattered energy is unchanged too -- which is the RIGHT answer if one reads the clip as "this lobe always existed only on the valid side", and the WRONG one if one reads it as "the clipped-away half of the lobe was real energy that has been folded back in". DL-68's closure doc takes the first reading explicitly and bounds the resulting MIS-side movement at 2x (measured <0.03% render-level), but never states which reading the ENERGY side takes, and the two differ by exactly that factor at heavy tilt. Entangled with DL-41 (`Pdf()` does not cover these lobes at all, so there is no second opinion to check against) and with DL-103 (the selected lobe's density is what the un-guided escape-side MIS partner reads). **THIRD CALL SITE (review P3, 2026-09-17):** `DielectricSPF`'s `scattering`/HG transmission warp now uses the same arc construction via `GeometricUtilities::PerturbClipped`, and its `dielectric.kray` (`tau^distance * (1-ref)`) is likewise unchanged by the clip -- this is a three-site question, not a two-site one. **RULING (review P3):** LEAVE `kray` ALONE. The DL-112 fix in the same slice settled the identical question on the identical mechanism: the clipped lobe always existed only on the valid side, so its albedo is the painter's reflectance at every tilt, and the DENSITY and the BSDF VALUE are renormalized while the throughput multiplier is not. Scaling `kray` by `halfArc/PI` would delete that fraction of the lobe's energy -- which is EXACTLY DL-112's defect re-filed under a different name (measured there as emitted energy `0.3 * P(valid)` instead of `0.3`, closing to `0.3` at every tilt after the fix). What stays open is narrower than this row's wording: not "is `kray` wrong" but "is the lobe's SELECTION probability -- which `RandomlySelect` derives from `kray` -- still proportional to the right thing once ONE lobe's density has been renormalized and the others' have not", a `RandomlySelect`/MIS question bounded by the same `PI/halfArc <= 2` factor and unanswerable in isolation while DL-41 leaves `Pdf()` not covering these lobes. **RECIPE.** A white-furnace on `translucent_material` with `trans` 1, `ext` 0, `scattering` 0 and a shading-normal tilt sweep: total outgoing energy must be tilt-invariant. Read the result against DL-68's §3 "the clipped-away energy is RENORMALIZED into the valid region" and settle, in one sentence in that doc, whether `kray` is meant to scale by `halfArc/PI` alongside the density or deliberately not. | S | physics-bias | user-visible only under heavy shading-normal tilt on `translucent_material` (identity at zero tilt; bounded by 2x) |

Gate: `GGXHeightCorrelatedEnergyLUTTest`'s aniso rows (both node-exact and off-node groups), `GGXDiffuseTransmissionTest`'s `TestAnisotropicFurnaceDL77` rows, a new low-alpha-aniso furnace sweep (none exists yet).~~ **Fix: the DL-105 low-alpha sub-grid construction, baked directly (not derived) because the `alphaX->0` boundary genuinely depends on `(alphaY,phi,cosTheta)`, exactly as this row predicted.** Two new bake families in `tools/GenerateMicrofacetEnergyLUT.cpp`/`MicrofacetEnergyLUT.h`: `E_ss_TABLE_G2_ANISO_PHI_ALPHALOW_X[10][24][13][32]` (+ grazing-sub-grid twin `_X_SUB[10][24][13][12]`) resolves a low alphaX (the SAME 10 `AlphaLowNode`/`AlphaLowSlot` virtual nodes DL-105 already defined, re-anchored to the ANISO grid's own row1 `ANISO_ALPHA_LOW_A1=0.01+0.99/23=0.053043` instead of the isotropic axis's `0.041935`) against the FULL ordinary 24-node alphaY grid, at full phi/cosTheta resolution; `E_ss_TABLE_G2_ANISO_PHI_ALPHALOW_XY[10][10][13][32]` (+ `_XY_SUB`) covers the corner where BOTH axes are low, baked upper-triangle-plus-mirror exactly like the main DL-77 table (the X<->Y relabel symmetry, `Ess(a,b,phi)==Ess(b,a,90-phi)`, holds for the low-alpha nodes too). A "low alphaY, ordinary alphaX" query is NOT separately baked -- `LookupEssG2AnisoDirectional`'s new dispatch derives it from the SAME `ALPHALOW_X` table by swapping `(alphaX,alphaY,localX,localY)`, which is what the relabel symmetry means physically. The exact `alphaX->0` virtual node (idx 0) is never baked -- `AnisoAlphaLowIndex`'s dead-zone `idx<=0` folds to idx=1's own baked row, because (a) an anisotropic closed-form limit does not exist here (confirmed by this row's own diagnosis) and (b) it is provably unreachable from any production roughness: `GGXBRDF.cpp`/`GGXSPF.cpp` floor authored alpha at `1e-4`, comfortably inside the `[AnisoAlphaLowNode(1)=7.8e-5, AnisoAlphaLowNode(2)~1.6e-4)` octave -- documented explicitly as a scoped simplification, not a silent gap. **The H6 multiscatter-lobe SAMPLER (`E_ss_TABLE_G2_ANISO`/`_SUB`, `LookupEssG2Aniso`, `MSLobeZG2Aniso`, `SampleMSCosThetaG2Aniso`, `MSPdfG2Aniso`, `BuildSegmentsFromRowN`) is DELIBERATELY NOT extended** -- unchanged, still using the pre-existing `AnisoAlphaIndex` clamp/coarse-cell behavior below `ANISO_ALPHA_LOW_A1`, matching the DL-86 precedent that the sampler's own precision is an efficiency concern (`MSPdfG2Aniso` always reports the density of what `SampleMSCosThetaG2Aniso` actually draws, so the estimator stays unbiased regardless of proposal accuracy) not a correctness one; only the two energy-compensation call sites are fixed. **Bug found and fixed mid-slice (own red-proof, not a review round): the FIRST implementation's dispatch computed `xLow`/`yLow` from `aX=r_max(0.01,r_min(1.0,alphaX))` -- the SAME clamp the pre-existing ORDINARY path applies -- which silently rounded every alphaX/alphaY below 0.01 UP TO 0.01 before the low-alpha check ever saw it, so e.g. `alphaX=0.002` and `alphaX=0.005` both dispatched as `alphaX==0.01` and returned IDENTICAL results (`GGXHeightCorrelatedEnergyLUTTest`'s new DL-161 rows caught this directly: `aX=0.002` and `aX=0.005` both read `0.880751` at `aY=1.0,phi=0,cos=0.0005`).** Fixed by checking `xLow`/`yLow` against the RAW alpha (floored only at 0) BEFORE the `[0.01,1.0]` clamp, matching `LookupEssG2`'s own `if(alpha<ALPHA_LOW_A1)` -- BEFORE-any-clamping -- structure. A SECOND bug (also self-caught before any commit): the new grazing-sub-grid accessors `AnisoPhiSubCellMixedX`/`AnisoPhiSubCellBothLow` were missing the `k<=0`/`k>=ANISO_SUB_TOTAL` boundary dispatch the pre-existing `AnisoPhiSubNodeEssG2` has (`k<=0` is the exact cosTheta->0 boundary, exactly 1 for G2; `k>=ANISO_SUB_TOTAL` IS the cell's own ordinary bin 0, not a `_SUB`-table index) -- an out-of-bounds read at `k=ANISO_SUB_TOTAL=13` (reachable at `cosTheta` near `c0`, e.g. `0.0156`) corrupted several PRE-EXISTING DL-86/DL-77 gate rows (`node-exact aX=0.01 aY=1 phi=0 cos=0.0156` read `0.996626` against a `0.519115` quadrature) -- caught by a full-suite re-run before any fix was declared done, not by a downstream review. **Residual** (worst relative error vs an independent 20M-sample-per-point VNDF quadrature, `GGXHeightCorrelatedEnergyLUTTest`'s new DL-161 rows, `alphaX` in `{0.002,0.005,0.01,0.015}` x `alphaY` in `{0.05,0.2,0.5,1.0}` x `phi` in `{0,45,90}` x several `cosTheta` down to `1e-4`, plus both-low and swapped-role rows): pre-fix up to **25.3%** (`alphaX=0.002,alphaY=1.0,phi=0,cos=0.0005`), post-fix **<=0.95%** at every probed cell. **Through the production BRDF** (`GGXDiffuseTransmissionTest`, Schlick F0=1 spec-only, added to `TestGrazingFurnaceDL86`'s aniso rows, `theta=89.60`): `alphax=.005/alphay=.5` `0.97306+/-0.00110 -> 0.99853+/-0.00107`; the axis-swapped twin `alphax=.5/alphay=.005` `0.97104+/-0.00110 -> 0.99662+/-0.00107`. **Regenerating `tools/GenerateMicrofacetEnergyLUT.cpp` reproduces the header byte-for-byte** (0-line diff against the checked-in file); every pre-existing table verified byte-identical by MD5 (not just by diff, since a large repetitive table can hide a real change inside diff-alignment noise), and the runtime-code diff against the pre-fix header is pure line-insertion (zero changed/removed lines) confirming byte-identity at every alpha `>= ANISO_ALPHA_LOW_A1` (both axes `>= 0.053043`, a stronger and more precisely-scoped claim than this row's own "both axes >= 0.042" estimate, which used the isotropic threshold by mistake). **Render sanity**: a scratch copy of `scenes/Tests/Materials/ggx_anisotropy_sweep.RISEscene` with its `alphax`/`alphay=0.05` cells lowered to `0.005` (`oidn_denoise FALSE`, `pixel_filter box`, EXR `Rec709RGB_Linear`, 64spp, isolated pre-/post-fix header rebuild, same PT seed) moved by `+0.009%` to `-0.303%` per 3x3 grid region (small, as expected -- this is a full-GI conductor-reflection scene, not an isolated grazing furnace, so the correction is heavily diluted the same way DL-105's own render sanity check found; no NaN/Inf in either render). **Gate**: `GGXHeightCorrelatedEnergyLUTTest` 278/0 (was 261/0, +17 DL-161 rows), `GGXDiffuseTransmissionTest` 192/0 (was 190/0, +2 DL-161 rows), `GGXSampleEvaluationConsistencyTest` 48/0, `GGXWhiteFurnaceTest`/`GGXMetalRoughGridTest` pass, `LayeredWhiteFurnaceTest` 0/57 (1 pre-existing unrelated KNOWN-FAIL row), `CookTorranceMultiscatterTest` 17/0, `FabricRenderTest` 60/0, `CstDeriveGoldenTest` 452 MATCH/0 DRIFT, `SourceHygieneTest` 167/0; clean warning-free rebuild (incremental + full). only when `rs.chainHasNEEOp` is true (else 0, folding DL-209's own fix in for free -- see that row). `DirectLightingShaderOp` forwards `rs.chainHasBsdfContinuationOp` as a new trailing itself) -- chosen over the ruling's literal "pass a NULL pMaterial" wording because `EvaluateDirectLighting` also reads `pMaterial` for `ScattersFullSphere()` (`bFullSphere`, load-bearing for hair/fabric/weave's full-sphere NEE), which a null would have silently broken; the additive boolean achieves the same `w_nee=1` outcome without that regression. `DistributionTracingShaderOp`'s own pre-MIS suppression hack (`considerEmission=false` whenever the source material has a BSDF) is RETIRED as obsolete now that a real partner is stamped; `bForceCheckEmitters` stays wired but is a no-op for that removed clause (unaffected: the orthogonal caustic-map suppression it also controls). `ReflectionShaderOp`/`RefractionShaderOp` are unchanged from V1 (already correctly gated on regardless of any partner or flag). Census of every distinct shader-op chain shape across the 459-scene corpus (grep `shaderop` lines, resolve to chunk type): every shape resolves correctly with NO scene-language change (`[DirectLighting] alone` x136, `[DirectLighting,Reflection,Refraction]` with no `dt`, `[DirectLighting,fg,Emission]`, `[dt,DirectLighting]` canonical-paired, `[Refraction,dt]`/`[dist,Emission]` with no NEE sibling). See [DL170_DL171_HWSS_AND_LEGACY_MIS_PARTNERS.md](DL170_DL171_HWSS_AND_LEGACY_MIS_PARTNERS.md) "DL-171" (rewritten) SS2 for the full derivation, ruling text, and census table. | `DistributionTracingShaderOp.cpp`, `ReflectionShaderOp.cpp`, `RefractionShaderOp.cpp` and `FinalGatherShaderOp.cpp` each build an `IRayCaster::RAY_STATE rs2` and set `type`/`depth`/`considerEmission` but never `bsdfPdf` or `bsdfMisPdf` (grep: `rs2.bsdfPdf` has no match in any of the four), so both default to their "no partner" values and `rs.MisPartnerPdf()` returns 0 at the next vertex. `EmissionShaderOp.cpp:94,158` then skip the weight block entirely (`( rs.bsdfPdf > 0 || misPartnerPdf > 0 )` is false) and the emission is taken at weight 1. Meanwhile `DirectLightingShaderOp.cpp` passes `ri.pMaterial` into `LightSampler::EvaluateDirectLighting`, whose arms compute `p_bsdf = pMaterial->Pdf(...)` and apply `PowerHeuristic(p_light, p_bsdf) < 1`. Two sub-cases, opposite signs: `DistributionTracingShaderOp.cpp:130` sets `considerEmission = false` when the material HAS a BSDF, so the BSDF-sampled emitter strategy is suppressed and the total reads `w_nee * L < L` (UNDER); where emission is NOT suppressed (`bForceCheckEmitters`, or a material with no BSDF) both strategies fire at combined weight `1 + w_nee > 1` (OVER). | ~~DL-200~~ | ~~(new; filed by review round 4 of the debt-dl126 slice, no source-doc heading -- see docs/DL126_BDPT_NULL_BSDF_CONTINUATION.md §7.2)~~ | ~~`BDPTIntegrator.cpp`'s medium-vertex `isConnectible` derivation (~line 1835/6100) inherits connectibility from the IMMEDIATELY PRECEDING vertex on THAT subpath only: a `LIGHT`-rooted predecessor never fails the `SURFACE`/`MEDIUM` type check, so a light emitting directly into an enclosed medium leaves that medium vertex `isConnectible = true`, while an EYE-rooted walk crossing the SAME specular boundary from the other side to reach a geometrically coincident medium point correctly reads `isConnectible = false`. Since DL-126 made `MISWeight` skip a would-be connection strategy whenever `!isConnectible` at either endpoint (previously it only checked `isDelta`), two strategies that can both generate the SAME physical path through that enclosure -- one light-rooted, one eye-rooted -- could be treated asymmetrically by that skip rule, which is the shape of a partition-of-unity violation.~~ | CLOSED 2026-09-18 (debt-dl200 slice) -- CONFIRMED REACHABLE, then fixed; but the PREDICTED MECHANISM measured ZERO | `5640ab18`.  **REACHED on the first scene variant**, which is what both prior investigations failed at: a dielectric sphere shell (`scattering 1000000`, a true delta boundary) carrying a strongly scattering `interior_medium` with a small emissive sphere INSIDE it, viewed from outside.  Scratch instrumentation on both generators and on every `isConnectible` connection gate measured `LIGHT-rooted enclosed medium verts conn=4683400 nonconn=2132904` against `EYE-rooted conn=0 nonconn=2197533`, and connection attempts at a MEDIUM endpoint `light-side ok=27474287 skipped=12942973` vs `eye-side ok=0 skipped=32085651` -- the asymmetry exactly as described.  **But the partition-of-unity story the row tells is worth nothing:** the rule this row's own Recipe prescribes (derive the flag from the ENCLOSURE BOUNDARY's material, applied symmetrically -- false on both sides for a delta boundary) was implemented and measured, and leaves the rendered image BIT-IDENTICAL to the pre-fix baseline (0.033181734 RGB / 0.030185761 spectral on the same four seeds, eight significant figures), i.e. the light-side connections it removes contribute exactly nothing and the asymmetry costs nothing on its own.  Applied as prescribed it would have closed NONE of the gap.  **The real defect at the same site** is that `isConnectible` -- which describes the vertex's OWN scattering function, and a phase function is never a delta -- was carrying a VISIBILITY heuristic: visibility is a property of the PAIR of endpoints, so two points inside the SAME enclosure are connectible and only a connection LEAVING it is blocked, which is precisely the light-inside-the-shell case.  SURFACE vertices were never demoted this way (a diffuse surface inside the same shell reads `GetBSDF() != 0` = true), and PT's own volume NEE connects out of interior medium vertices through the boundary with the same transparent-shadow transmittance -- so only MEDIUM vertices disagreed with PT.  Fixed by `mv.isConnectible = true` at BOTH generator sites.  MEASURED (48x48, 32 spp, PT the reference, isolated A/B against `caa0432a`): BDPT/PT **0.90338 -> 1.00356** (RGB, n=4; 0.91476 -> 1.00827 at n=3) and **0.79556 -> 0.98217** (spectral, n=4; 0.78357 -> 0.98905 at n=3), with BDPT's own run-to-run sd falling **2.94% -> 0.37%** (the dropped eye-side NEE was a variance cost too).  Red-proof/regression `tests/MediumEnclosureConnectibilityTest.cpp` 12/0 (red 10/2).  COST, measured (isolated A/B, n=3): on this deliberately adversarial scene -- 100% affected vertices -- a 48x48/32-spp BDPT render goes 1.07 s -> 2.15 s user CPU (+101%), which is the NEE and connection work PT was already doing; the same change drops BDPT's run-to-run sd 2.94% -> 0.37% (~8x lower standard error, ~64x fewer samples for equal noise), so it is a large net efficiency WIN even here.  Every other scene is unaffected BY CONSTRUCTION: a global medium took the old derivation's own early-out and a medium behind a non-delta boundary already had `prev.isConnectible == true`.  Sibling audit: `isConnectible` is ASSIGNED at seven sites in `BDPTIntegrator.cpp` and only the two medium ones carried an enclosure rule; `VCMIntegrator.cpp` assigns it nowhere (its `Convert*Subpath` key on `v.type`/`v.isDelta`/the area-measure Jacobian), so VCM and MLT inherit the fix by construction -- the check the Recipe asked for after DL-126's second instance.  Opened **DL-218**.  PRIOR EVIDENCE (kept): `BDPTIntegrator.cpp`'s own round-2 dismissal argued there is "no shared vertex position whose two conflicting classifications would ever need reconciling" -- WRONG on reflection (review round 4): MIS partition-of-unity is a claim about STRATEGIES (samples), not about one already-generated vertex array; two DIFFERENT samples (light-rooted vs eye-rooted) can produce the SAME physical path through an enclosure and need to be treated consistently by the SAME skip predicate. The reviewer's own attempted A/B (a glass-shell interior-medium scene meant to force an eye-rooted walk to cross the shell and reach a `MEDIUM`/`!isConnectible` vertex) measured 0 connection attempts at such a vertex out of 0 opportunities (0/0) -- the scene never constructed the triggering configuration at all, so this is INCONCLUSIVE, not evidence of no defect. A separate A/B on a different scene (BDPT 0.01961 vs 0.01959, both ~1.6% under PT) is a pre-existing, unrelated gap, not evidence either way. **RECIPE for whoever picks this up**: build a scene that forces an EYE subpath specifically (not the light subpath) to cross a specular boundary into an enclosed medium and attempt a connection strategy there; instrument to CONFIRM the triggering configuration (`MEDIUM` type, `isConnectible == false`, at least one valid connection candidate reaching that vertex) is actually exercised before drawing any conclusion from an image-level BDPT-vs-PT ratio -- a scene that silently fails to reach the configuration (as both prior attempts did) proves nothing either way. If a partition violation is confirmed, the likely fix is deriving medium-vertex connectibility from a boundary-material property (already partially done -- see the surrounding comment's own "property of the boundary MATERIAL, not the lobe" reasoning) applied SYMMETRICALLY regardless of which subpath crosses it, rather than from `vertices.back()` alone. | S (if real) | physics-bias (if real) | not yet shown to be user-visible -- filed to record the state, not to assert a confirmed defect |
| ~~DL-201~~ | ~~(new; filed by review round 4 of the debt-dl126 slice, no source-doc heading -- see docs/DL126_BDPT_NULL_BSDF_CONTINUATION.md §7.7)~~ | ~~`BDPTSpectralRasterizer::GetSplatSampleScale()`/`VCMSpectralRasterizer`'s twin return the FIXED `nSpectralSamples * SampledWavelengths::N` whenever `bUseHWSS`, independent of how many companion wavelengths any given bundle's `swl.TerminateSecondary()` actually excluded (dispersive delta, pre-existing; a null-BSDF continuation vertex, DL-126). `SplatFilm::Resolve` divides every accumulated splat pixel by ONE global scalar `sampleCount` (`invSamples = 1/sampleCount`, applied uniformly image-wide -- confirmed by the function's own header comment: "`SplatFilm::Resolve` divides every pixel by a SCALAR global sampleCount -- it does not track a per-pixel weight sum like `FilteredFilm` does"). A terminated companion's own `EvaluateAllStrategiesNM` loop correctly SKIPS depositing its (would-be) splat contribution (`BDPTSpectralRasterizer.cpp`'s per-companion loop `continue`s on `swl.terminated[w]`), so the NUMERATOR at any pixel receiving light-tracing (`needsSplat`) energy is honest about which companions actually contributed -- but the DENOMINATOR is one global constant applied to every pixel regardless of how many companions terminated on the way there. A pixel whose light-tracing energy arrives predominantly through bundles with a HIGH termination rate (concentrated near a dispersive or null-BSDF object) is darkened relative to one reached mostly by low-termination bundles, exactly the same dilution mechanism P1-1 fixed for the per-pixel bundle-mean accumulation -- but at the whole-FILM level, where the fix is architecturally harder because the denominator is a single scalar, not a per-pixel accumulator.~~ | CLOSED 2026-09-18 (debt-dl200 slice) | `53333c2d`.  Each bundle's t==1 splat deposits are now scaled by `SampledWavelengths::N / swl.NumActive()` AT THE DEPOSIT SITE, which is identical to dividing that bundle's splat energy by the lanes it actually ran instead of by N -- "the denominator counts what was actually deposited" -- and is exactly 1.0 when nothing terminated, so non-terminating spectral renders, every non-HWSS render and every Pel render are bit-identical.  Both termination checks were hoisted above the hero block in each rasterizer so the scale is final before the hero's own splat (they read only the two vertex arrays and the bundle's wavelengths, which the hero block does not write).  **This row's own proposed per-pixel weight accumulator in `SplatFilm` was REJECTED on derivation, not on cost**: a splat estimator's denominator must count every lane that COULD have deposited at a pixel, not the ones that did (a light subpath may land anywhere on the film), so a weight summed at deposit time computes a mean over CONTRIBUTING samples and over-brightens sparse splat regions without bound -- which is exactly why `SplatPixel::weight`, which already counts deposits, is deliberately read only as a nonzero flag.  Correcting per BUNDLE at the source is both exact and strictly FINER-grained than any per-pixel scheme, because termination is a per-bundle property and the spatial correlation this row is about disappears once each bundle is renormalized before its energy is pooled.  MEASURED (32x32, hwss FALSE 512 spp vs hwss TRUE 128 spp, isolated A/B against `caa0432a`): the left/right BALANCE statistic -- `(hwTRUE_left/hwTRUE_right)/(hwFALSE_left/hwFALSE_right)` on a floor lit through TWO glass slabs, the left one dispersive and the right one the same glass at its own d-line index -- reads **0.2661 -> ~1.01** (BDPT pure-splat), **0.2984 -> ~1.01** (VCM pure-splat) and **0.6307 -> ~1.01** (VCM at an ORDINARY `max_eye_depth 5`), against 1.0038 / 1.0058 on the two both-slabs-flat CONTROL rows, which are green in BOTH builds.  0.2661 is `1/SampledWavelengths::N` to within noise, as predicted from the measured termination fraction (essentially 100% of the bundles depositing there).  MLT needed no change (`MLTSpectralRasterizer` already scales every strategy, splat and non-splat alike, by `1/activeWavelengthCount` since DL-126 P1-1, and never consults `GetSplatSampleScale()`).  Red-proof/regression `tests/SpectralSplatActiveCountTest.cpp` 30/0 (red 24/6).  The sibling audit at the same three deposit sites found and closed **DL-217**.  PRIOR EVIDENCE (kept): Sites: `src/Library/Rendering/BDPTSpectralRasterizer.h:69`, `VCMSpectralRasterizer.h:44` (`GetSplatSampleScale`); `src/Library/Rendering/SplatFilm.cpp:269` (`Resolve`, takes one `const Scalar sampleCount`, no per-pixel weight tracking -- its own header comment at `:150` says so explicitly, contrasting with `FilteredFilm`, which DOES track a per-pixel weight sum); `BDPTSpectralRasterizer.cpp`'s companion loop (~line 393-460) correctly `continue`s past a terminated companion's splat deposit. **Attempted render-level measurement, INCONCLUSIVE**: rendered `scenes/Tests/Spectral/hwss_prism_dispersion_bdpt.RISEscene` (dispersive glass sphere, pinhole camera, Cornell box) at 64 spp, `hwss FALSE` vs `TRUE`, `oidn_denoise` output discarded in favour of the raw (non-denoised) EXR. Whole-frame achromatic ratio TRUE/FALSE = 1.020 (+2.0%) -- dominated by the box's own diffuse NEE-lit walls, not diagnostic of a splat-specific effect. A 4x4 block-grid comparison found every block's ratio inside roughly 0.92-1.06 (noise-level spread at this spp), with no block showing a clearly isolated 2x-class deficit the way P1-1's own per-bundle mechanism would predict if a caustic region were cleanly isolated -- but this scene's light-tracing splat energy is a SMALL, spatially DIFFUSE fraction of a Cornell box's total illumination (most of the box is lit by ordinary NEE/BSDF-hit strategies, which this bug does not touch), so a null result here does not rule out the mechanism; it means this scene is the wrong instrument, not that the bug is absent. **RECIPE for whoever picks this up**: (1) build a scene where light-tracing (`needsSplat`, t=1 connect-to-camera) energy is the OVERWHELMINGLY DOMINANT contribution in a spatially isolated region -- e.g. a small, bright light source behind a dispersive or null-BSDF caster, aimed to cast a concentrated caustic/highlight onto an otherwise near-black receiver (minimal ambient/NEE fill), so that region's brightness is almost entirely splat energy and directly comparable hwss TRUE vs FALSE; (2) if confirmed, the fix needs `SplatFilm` to track a per-pixel (or per-splat, accumulated into a parallel per-pixel weight buffer) active-count denominator instead of one global scalar -- structurally similar to `FilteredFilm`'s existing per-pixel weight-sum design, which `SplatFilm` deliberately does not share (see its own header comment) -- NOT a one-line fix, matching this row's own P2-1 instruction to file rather than attempt a rushed film-architecture change. | S-M (confirmed mechanism; magnitude and reachability not yet render-measured) | physics-bias | user-visible IF confirmed: any HWSS spectral BDPT/VCM render whose light-tracing splats pass through a dispersive or null-BSDF object with a spatially uneven termination rate across the image (concentrated caustics/highlights through such an object would darken relative to the rest of the frame) |
| ~~DL-217~~ | ~~(new; opened AND closed by the debt-dl200 slice's own sibling audit of DL-201, at the same three deposit sites)~~ | ~~The spectral BDPT/VCM t==1 SPLAT deposit omitted `mYNormalization`, the `(lambda_end - lambda_begin) / k_y` INTEGRAL scale (~3.74 over the default [380, 780] nm) that every NON-splat spectral contribution in the same render carries.  `SplatFilm::Resolve`'s divisor is a SAMPLE COUNT and nothing more -- the splat analogue of the `/ nSpectralSamples` in the non-splat return, not of the integral scale -- so every spectral splat landed ~3.74x too dim RELATIVE TO the same render's own non-splat layer.~~ | CLOSED 2026-09-18 (debt-dl200 slice) | `dc6e1f0a`.  A DISTINCT defect from DL-201 (a missing multiplicative integral constant, not a wrong sample-count denominator) but the SAME bug pattern -- the spectral splat layer's normalisation drifting from the non-splat layer's -- so fixed in-slice per COMMON_RULES rather than filed. Three sites: `IntegratePixelNM`'s non-HWSS splat plus the HWSS hero and companion splats (BDPT), and the two `SplatLightSubpathToCameraNM` calls (VCM, folded into the `splatScale` argument DL-201 added). Red-proof/regression `tests/SpectralSplatIntegralNormalizationTest.cpp` 15/0 (red 12/3).  `EnvLightBalanceTest` stayed 123/0 -- no band needed re-deriving -- but its SPECTRAL VCM rows DID move, by up to +4.7% (topology means 0.579766 -> 0.606845, 0.601256 -> 0.619435, 0.615919 -> 0.626362; another row 0.605476 -> 0.627115), while its RGB rows are unchanged to 4-5 significant figures. | M | physics-bias | user-visible: every `vcm_spectral_rasterizer` render was 45-68% of its Pel twin; `bdpt_spectral_rasterizer` only where its t==1 share is non-negligible |
| DL-218 | (new; opened by the debt-dl200 slice while building DL-200's own reference rows) | Two measured bidirectional-vs-PT MEDIUM-transport disagreements that are NOT DL-200's mechanism and are unmoved by its fix.  (a) `vcm_pel_rasterizer` reads **6.0% of PT** on an emitter inside a fog-filled dielectric shell viewed from outside (0.060729 pre-fix, 0.060248 post-fix).  (b) `bdpt_pel_rasterizer` reads **1.73-1.82x of PT** on the SAME medium and emitter bound as the scene's GLOBAL medium with no shell at all (1.79333 pre-fix, 1.73429 / 1.81976 post-fix -- the spread is that row's own MC noise, the PT reference being the noisiest render in the file).  (b) is provably untouched by DL-200: `pMedObj == 0` took the pre-fix derivation's own "global medium: always connectable" early-out, so the fix is a literal no-op there. | OPEN-confirmed (both render-measured, both pinned) | Both are pinned at their measured values in `tests/MediumEnclosureConnectibilityTest.cpp` (`PIN(DL-218) VCM vs PT` band [0.04, 0.09]; `CONTROL/PIN(DL-218) BDPT vs PT, GLOBAL medium` band [1.50, 2.05]) so closing this row has to move them deliberately rather than silently.  **RECIPE**: they are two different causes and must be split before either is attacked.  **(a)'s root cause is already identified by inspection during the DL-200 slice, so do not re-instrument for it**: every VCM consumer gates on `v.type != BDPTVertex::SURFACE` BEFORE it looks at `isConnectible` -- `EvaluateNEEImpl` (`VCMIntegrator.cpp` ~:1246), `EvaluateInteriorConnectionsImpl` (~:1968 light side and ~:1985 eye side) and `EvaluateMergesImpl` (~:2333) -- and `ConvertLightSubpath`'s own `isMedium` branch `continue`s before the light-vertex-store append.  So VCM does NO NEE, NO interior connection and NO merge at a MEDIUM vertex AT ALL, by explicit type gate, which is CLAUDE.md's already-documented "VCM/BDPT take no NEE and no merges at MEDIUM vertices" note (docs/SUBMERGED_CAMERA_IOR_SEEDING.md) -- except that the BDPT half of that sentence is now stale, since BDPT's own `EvaluateNEE` has always had a real `eyeIsMedium_s1` branch and DL-200 is what finally let enclosed medium vertices reach it.  The work for (a) is therefore to give VCM the medium-vertex branches BDPT already has (NEE and interior connections at least; merging is surface-only by design and `vm_enabled` was FALSE in this measurement, so it is not the explanation), including the `sigma_t_scalar`-in-place-of-cosine area-measure Jacobian its own `Convert*Subpath` already implements.  For (b), BDPT is OVER PT, not under, so suspect the light-subpath side or the medium-vertex MIS densities rather than a dropped strategy; a good first discriminator is whether the excess survives `max_light_depth 1` (light-root-only) and whether PT's own camera-ray-first-medium-interaction walk (the DL-109 site) is the one that is short. | M | physics-bias | user-visible: any `vcm_*` render of an enclosed participating medium, and any `bdpt_*` render in a global medium |

independently re-verified the code exists as described rather than taking
the heading's word for it.

| DL-220 | (new; opened by the `debt-dl157` slice's review round 3, P3, no source-doc heading) | `BezierPatchGeometry` stamps DL-96's `ri.bOpenSheet = bDidFlip` on the same false premise round 3 removed the `bProvablyNoInterior` stamp for -- that a patch geometry cannot enclose a volume -- so a CLOSED Bezier solid's interior hits are reported as open-sheet hits, and `RayIntersectionGeometric::BSSRDFEntryFacing()` then admits them as subsurface ENTRIES | OPEN 2026-09-18 (mechanism derived and the geometry evidence confirmed; NOT measured at render level) | **MECHANISM.** `BezierPatchGeometry.cpp:113` sets `ri.bOpenSheet = bDidFlip` with the comment "a patch never encloses a volume, so a double-sided hit here is always an open sheet". That premise is false for the same reason round 3's P1 was: the class holds a `BezierPatchList` (a vector) behind a BSP/Octree, `AddPatch` appends, and `Job.cpp:6789`'s `.bezier` loader puts EVERY patch of a file into ONE geometry. `models/raw/teapot.bezier` declares **28** patches in its first line; `models/bezier/aphrodite.bezier` (2.3 MB) and `f16.bezier` are closed solids. The flip itself is a WINDING artifact, not a facing fact -- `RayElementIntersection`'s own comment says the teapot file traverses some patches CCW and others CW -- so it cannot carry a topological claim at all. **CONSUMER.** DL-96 introduced `bOpenSheet` for exactly one reader, `RayIntersectionGeometric::BSSRDFEntryFacing()`, which relaxes the DL-70 closed-solid front-face gate so BOTH faces of a genuinely open sheet (a leaf, a cloth card) can admit SSS entry. On a closed Bezier solid that relaxation admits a hit on the FAR INTERIOR WALL as an entry, so a subsurface walk is seeded from inside the object: the DL-96 fix applied to a body it was explicitly not for. **RECIPE.** Same shape as round 3's P1: either delete the setter outright (Bezier objects then fall back to the DL-70 closed-solid gate, which is the correct default for a body that MIGHT enclose), or give the class a real certification -- a genuine open/closed test over the assembled patch set is possible in principle (patch boundary curves shared exactly in pairs), but it is a build-time topology pass that does not exist and is strictly larger than this row. Note the two flags are NOT interchangeable: `bOpenSheet` must stay set for the genuinely-one-patch case if a certification is built, while `bProvablyNoInterior` is a strictly stronger claim. **GATE.** A closed Bezier solid (`aphrodite.bezier`, or a hand-built patch pair closing a box) bound to `subsurfacescattering_material` and viewed from outside, against a `TessellateToMesh`-derived indexed-mesh twin of the same object -- the mesh has no `bOpenSheet` stamp unless DL-143's weld fails, so it is the control. PT and BDPT, `oidn_denoise FALSE`, `pixel_filter box`, linear EXR. Expect the Bezier build to read BRIGHTER (extra entry events seeded at interior walls). | S | physics-bias | latent (no shipped scene binds `bezierpatch_geometry` to a subsurface material today; `scenes/Tests/SMS/sms_teapot_close_sms.RISEscene` does drive a Bezier teapot, but through `ManifoldSolver`, not the BSSRDF gate) |
| DL-223 | (new; opened by the `debt-dl157` slice while closing DL-38, no source-doc heading) | A BDPT/VCM CONNECTION at a `translucent_material` vertex -- and, in two narrower cases, a STACKLESS evaluation -- does not reproduce the transport weight the SPF's own walk charges, for FOUR independent reasons (see the evidence cell's (1)-(4)) | OPEN 2026-09-18 (both sub-cases measured) | **(1) The interior Beer factor is lost.** `TranslucentSPF`'s exit side charges `B = exp(-ext * |ri.ray.origin - ri.ptIntersection|)` on both its lobes, and since DL-157 `TranslucentBSDF::value` reads that same expression from `ri` -- which is correct at a REAL intersection record (PT's NEE) and identically `B == 1` at a connection, because `PathVertexEval::EvalBSDFAtVertex` builds `Ray(vertex.position, -wo)` and `PopulateRIGFromVertex` sets `ri.ptIntersection = vertex.position`, so the segment length is 0. Measured on the DL-157 money fixture (bump-mapped translucent sphere + overhead area light, 200x200 / 256 spp, `oidn_denoise FALSE`, `pixel_filter box`, EXR linear, central 80x80, n=4), at ZERO relief: PT/BDPT reads **0.912276** at `ext 0` and **0.522062** at `ext 0.35`, i.e. BDPT over-reads by **1.747x** purely from the missing extinction. Zero at `ext 0`, which is the descriptor default and what most shipped scenes use. **(2) The model is not reciprocal and BDPT evaluates it with the roles swapped.** `f(a->b)` for `a` outside and `b` inside is the ENTRY transmission lobe (`kray = tau`); `f(b->a)` is the EXIT lobe (`kray = B*(1-s)`) -- different weights for the same direction pair, which is a property of RISE's translucent MODEL, not of DL-157's fix. `GenerateEyeSubpathImpl` passes `EvalBSDFAtVertex(v, scatDir, -currentRay.Dir())` while `GenerateLightSubpathImpl` passes `(v, -currentRay.Dir(), scatDir)`, so the two sides evaluate at different directions. Measured at zero relief, `ext 0`: PT/BDPT **-8.772 %** at `N 10` and **+30.071 %** at `N 1` -- the `N 1` rig makes every lobe direction-CONSTANT, so what is left there is the side asymmetry alone. **RECIPE.** For (1): add the incoming segment length to `BDPTVertex` (it is known at generation time, `ri.geometric.range`) and let `PopulateRIGFromVertex` place `ri.ray.origin` at `vertex.position - ri.ray.Dir() * len`; note this gives the right MAGNITUDE only when the query direction is the generating one, so it needs an answer for the reverse query too. For (2): decide an ADJOINT-BSDF convention for a non-reciprocal stateful material and make both generators state which direction they mean, the way `IBSDF`'s own doc comment's reciprocity claim assumes. Gate with the DL-157 money fixture's zero-relief cell at both `ext 0` and `ext 0.35`, plus a Lambertian control at the same relief (DL-224) so the tilt artifact is not mistaken for progress. **(3) ADDED by DL-157's review round 1 (2026-09-18): the LIGHT-SUBPATH ENTRY FRAME.** `BuildLobeSet` takes the ENTRY side's lobe frame from `ri.ray.Dir()`, because that is literally what `Scatter` does and because a stack-derived frame is WRONG on a reachable record -- an open double-sided sheet struck BACK-FACE-FIRST, where the stack says not-inside and the ray says leaving and BOTH are true (`clippedplane_geometry`'s `doublesided` defaults to TRUE and translucent is what authors put on open sheets, DL-46 review round 3(c)).  Measured with a stack-derived frame on that record at tilt 0, `E[kray]/E[value*cos/pdf]`: front reflection **1.260222**, transmission **2.336741**, against **1.000000 / 1.000000** with the ray-anchored one and a front-face control reading 1.000000 in both builds.  **⚠ That pair was originally written against a gate that could not have produced it** -- round 2's gate 1 drove only the STACKLESS `value`, so a number measured by hand on the STACKED path had no gated home, which is review round 2's P2-1.  Both entry points are now gated on every row, and the single-change A/B reads **924 / 84 -> 1008 / 0** on the round-2 file (**1030 / 84 -> 1114 / 0** re-run on the round-3 one) with those same four ratios appearing on the `planeBack` and `meshBack` rows, stacked AND stackless, plus gate 3's `planeBack` TVD at 0.57972 against a 0.08999 measured floor.  At a LIGHT-subpath entry vertex the rebuilt record's ray is `-scatDir`, not the walk's own incoming segment, so that frame is inverted there.  It does NOT kill the walk (the inverted transmission lobe still has support at the queried direction, and `TranslucentIORStackTest` reads `reached=512` on every mode) and it is not new -- the pre-DL-157 `GetReflectedSide` was equally ray-anchored -- but it is the same caller-convention defect as (2) and closes with it.  The EXIT side is immune BY CONSTRUCTION and stays ray-independent: `Scatter`'s own `geomN` there is `-geomNRaw` at every record its exit branch can be reached with, since that branch requires `containsCurrent()` and a ray inside an object travels outward at its boundary hit.  **(4) The STACKLESS side inference.**  ⚠ Round 2 asked `ri.bOpenSheet` here on the reading "a sheet has no interior", and scoped the residual as "wrong only for a ray that has already transmitted through the sheet".  **Both are false, and review round 2 measured the cost**: for the two mesh classes `bOpenSheet` means UNCERTIFIED, not open -- `TriangleMeshGeometryIndexed` sets it whenever DL-143's weld could not certify watertightness (all four glTF assets DL-143 audited, every one a CLOSED solid) and the non-indexed twin sets it on every double-sided hit -- so at a genuine interior EXIT on such a mesh the stackless path priced the ENTRY lobes: `dsMeshExit` stackless gate-1 rows read **1.745299** (diffuse exit) and **0.647376** (backscatter) at tilt 0, 984/24 against 1008/0 on the round-2 file (1066/48 against 1114/0 re-run on the round-3 one).  Fixed with a real certification, a new `RayIntersectionGeometric::bProvablyNoInterior`.  **Round 2 set it from TWO geometries and review round 3's P1 cut that to ONE**: `ClippedPlaneGeometry` alone -- four corners spanning one bounded bilinear sheet, which cannot enclose a volume however it is transformed, so the claim needs no build-time check and cannot be wrong.  Two kinds of geometry can never make it: a MESH, because "not certified closed" is not "certified open"; and any geometry holding a COLLECTION of primitives, however interior-free each primitive is alone, because an interior is a property of the whole surface and N open sheets can bound a volume no single sheet can.  `BezierPatchGeometry` is the second kind and round 2 mistook it for a single patch: `patches` is a `BezierPatchList` (a vector) behind a BSP/Octree, `AddPatch` appends, and `Job.cpp:6789`'s `.bezier` loader puts EVERY patch of a file into ONE geometry -- `models/raw/teapot.bezier` declares 28, `models/bezier/aphrodite.bezier` and `f16.bezier` are closed solids.  At a genuine interior exit there `dotND > 0`, the patch normal flips, and the round-2 stamp asserted "no interior" on precisely the hit that disproves it (a second reason the flip cannot carry a topological claim: `RayElementIntersection`'s own comment says it exists because WINDING varies patch-to-patch inside one file).  The setter was removed and the new `bezierExit` gate row -- whose flags are stamped by a LIVE two-patch `BezierPatchGeometry` hit, so reverting that one file is a genuine single-change A/B -- measures what it cost: **1.745299** (diffuse exit) / **0.647376** (backscatter) at tilt 0 stackless, bit-for-bit the `dsMeshExit` pair, **1089 / 25** against **1114 / 0**.  LATENT (no shipped scene binds `bezierpatch_geometry` to `translucent_material`) but as authorable as the clipped-plane case, and `sms_teapot_close_sms.RISEscene` already drives a Bezier teapot through `ManifoldSolver`'s four stackless sites.  **What remains** is a stackless caller on a double-sided MESH open sheet struck back-face-first, which is indistinguishable from that mesh's own interior exit: gate 1's `meshBack` stackless rows read **1.680295** (front reflection) and **1.001460** (transmission) at tilt 0, carried as a printed KNOWN-RESIDUAL row.  **The affected set is 16 calls in eight files (round 5 corrected the count; round 3 expanded the earlier incomplete family list)**: `TranslucentPelPhotonMap.cpp:91` (its shader op HAS a stack; the `IPhotonMap` interface does not carry one); `GlobalPelPhotonMap.cpp:97`; `GlobalSpectralPhotonMap.cpp:82` and `:135`; `CausticSpectralPhotonMap.cpp:82` and `:137`; `PhotonMap.h:705` (the shared templated gather); `PointSetOctree.cpp:271`/`:287` (the SSS irradiance cache); `InteractivePelRasterizer.cpp:322`/`:323`/`:368` (the preview); and `ManifoldSolver.cpp:5684`/`:5798`/`:6624`/`:8085` (the four SMS sites).  Another site, `FinalGatherShaderOp.cpp:220`, was FIXED in round 3 rather than recorded -- the same file's primary gather arm at `:521` was converted by this slice while the gradient-estimator arm was left behind, with the live `ior_stack` already a parameter of that helper -- and because FINAL GATHER IS A PRODUCTION PATH, round 2's "none of them a production integrator path" was wrong as written; the remaining photon-gather and SMS calls still include production paths.  (`PathValueOps::EvalBSDF` has no production caller; its two callers are tests.  `ILightManager::ComputeDirectLighting`'s forwarding loop was on the round-2 list and is dropped from it -- it has no in-tree caller at all, as `LightManager.cpp`'s own comment says.) - CROSS_OBJECT_PROXIMITY_DESIGN.md §10 — SDF-neighbour distance is an upper bound by design (never over-reads contact), bounded by the last probe step; a candidate whose crossing is not found in budget reads far. Documented behavior, not a defect.
- CROSS_OBJECT_PROXIMITY_DESIGN.md §10 — the reported range is `[0, maxDist)`, not `(0, maxDist]` (zero attained at interpenetration, the radius itself excluded); a stated, continuous convention, not a bug.
- CROSS_OBJECT_PROXIMITY_DESIGN.md §10 — the eager snapshot build and per-ray `EnsureBoxSnapshot()` are gated on `ProximityDemand` rather than built unconditionally; a deliberate perf/correctness trade (an unregistered direct caller pays the build cost once under a lock instead of finding it pre-built), documented and accepted.
- CROSS_OBJECT_PROXIMITY_DESIGN.md §10 — `proximity(r)` cannot drive `ReliefModifier`'s relief: confirmed by code comment, `ReliefModifier.cpp:279-283` ("DELIBERATELY LEFT ALONE: vNormal, derivatives, signals... holds them invariant under shading-normal [perturbation]"). A deliberate architectural invariant (the same one that makes `curv` bump-invariant), not an oversight.
- CROSS_OBJECT_PROXIMITY_DESIGN.md §10 — per-sample motion-blur staleness of a proximity/interior memo entry (a moving neighbour under a non-keyframed painter, or the scalar pipe's `time=0` stamp relying on the jitter argument alone): disclosed, and the doc's own analysis is that sub-pixel jitter makes a stale hit essentially unreachable in practice; no scene in the design's own corpus exercises it.
- CROSS_OBJECT_PROXIMITY_DESIGN.md §10 — the TLAS-backed candidate-scan shape description (a leaf visits up to 4 occupants before pruning; a per-element box pre-test roughly halves that) is a documented perf characterization with a measured 1.094x wall-clock effect on five pairs, not a correctness bug.
- CROSS_OBJECT_PROXIMITY_DESIGN.md §10 — `proximity` is unsigned by design; `interior(r)` supplies the inside half for solid families and, since DL-31's 2026-09-14 closure, a watertight mesh too (documented scope, not a defect in itself — the residual for an open/non-manifold mesh is what DL-31's own now-closed row tracked, and it stays that way by design: a sheet has no inside to report).
- CROSS_OBJECT_PROXIMITY_DESIGN.md §10 — the query's read of other objects' live transforms joins the pre-existing per-sample `EvaluateAtTime` race under motion blur, "no wider than `Object::IntersectRay` already does" per the doc's own comparison; not a new or worse hazard.
- CROSS_OBJECT_PROXIMITY_DESIGN.md §10 — the memo-eligibility threshold moving with `kFields` (29 -> 35) is bit-identical and perf-only, disclosed as such.
- CROSS_OBJECT_PROXIMITY_DESIGN.md §10 — the agent-facing draft-preview descriptor strings claiming quality:"draft" ignores materials/lighting entirely were wrong (draft actually evaluates the material's full BSDF under a fixed studio rig, only real scene LIGHTING is skipped) — already corrected in the same session in `AgentMcpAdapter.cpp`/`AgentChatCodecs.cpp`/`AgentSession.{h,cpp}`/`AgentRpc.h`; a documentation-accuracy fix already landed, not an open code debt.
- SIGNALS_UNDER_BIDIRECTIONAL_TRANSPORT.md §10 — "No weight formula changes": MIS heuristics per integrator stay exactly as documented in MIS_HEURISTICS.md; a stated non-goal, not a debt.
- SIGNALS_UNDER_BIDIRECTIONAL_TRANSPORT.md §10 / CROSS_OBJECT_PROXIMITY_DESIGN.md §10 — GUI painter preview, realize-time displacement, and `HairGenerator` build their own hit records and read signals as neutral; doc's own words: "not transport; unchanged and already disclosed." These are non-transport, non-comparison paths (a single-sample preview, a one-shot bake, hair-strand generation), not part of the PT/BDPT/VCM consistency contract that DL-14/DL-19/DL-22/DL-30/DL-36 track.
- ~~DL-73~~ (DL72_RAYCASTER_BSDFTIMESCOS_TRAINING.md: volume-guiding bsdfPdf composition) — STRUCK 2026-09-13, ruled consistent by derivation (P2-C, debt-sssenv round-2): the row proposed replacing `RayCaster.cpp`'s `rs2.bsdfPdf = phasePdf` (raw, un-combined) with the guided-mixture `combinedPdf`, on the theory that it should match the main surface continuation's `effectiveBsdfPdf` convention. Derivation shows this is backwards: env-NEE at a volume vertex weights via `MediumScatterMaterial::Pdf` (`MediumTransport.cpp`'s `EvaluateInScattering` -> `LightSampler::EvaluateDirectLighting`'s env arm, `LightSampler.cpp` ~:2652), which returns the RAW, un-guided `m_pPhase->Pdf(...)` — the SAME raw `phasePdf` the escape side already uses. Both sides feed `PowerHeuristic` the identical `(phasePdf, envPdf)` pair (opposite argument order), which is `PowerHeuristic(a,b) + PowerHeuristic(b,a) == 1` by construction — UNBIASED as written. `guidingMISWeight = phasePdf / combinedPdf` (folded into `rs2.importance`) already applies the full guiding correction to the sample's contribution; substituting `combinedPdf` into the MIS weight too, as this row prescribed, would double-apply that correction and BREAK the partition. Not a debt; the real, opposite-signed asymmetry is on the surface path, filed separately as DL-74. See [DL72_RAYCASTER_BSDFTIMESCOS_TRAINING.md](DL72_RAYCASTER_BSDFTIMESCOS_TRAINING.md) "Residual: volume-guiding bsdfPdf composition — DL-73 RULED NOT A DEBT".
