## Counts

**2026-09-18 (debt-dl125 slice, branched from `master` `9fe42f52`):** DL-125 CLOSED; DL-221, DL-222, DL-216 (round 1) and DL-219 (round 2) opened.  **TWO review rounds, same day, same branch; each found the previous round's render-level headline FALSE, so the numbers below are round 2's and the history is kept deliberately.**  Round 1: one P1 (an arithmetically impossible red counter) and five P2s.  Round 2: two P1s -- (1) round 1's own "-0.906% -> -1.437%, Welch t = -4.43" achromatic systematic is a LOW-SPP ARTIFACT (hwssTRUE-only pre/post at `num_wavelengths 160`: post/pre 0.99484 at 256 spp, then 1.00077 / 0.99990 / 0.99979 at 512 / 1024 / 2048, t = +1.49 / -0.16 / -0.34 -- the two builds converge to the same mean), so a gate band had been DERIVED from an artifact; and (2) topology P's own assertion ("the HWSS bundle REPRODUCES the hero-only render's channel balance") is FALSE once `num_wavelengths` is matched -- it passed at the default 10 only because the discrete left-endpoint hwssFALSE grid's own error partially cancels a real, PRE-EXISTING defect, now filed as DL-219. **DL-125** -- the five SPFs that store a PER-LOBE conditional density on each emitted ray and did not override `ISPF::EvaluateKrayNM` (`SchlickSPF`, `WardIsotropicGaussianSPF`, `WardAnisotropicEllipticalGaussianSPF`, `IsotropicPhongSPF`, `AshikminShirleyAnisotropicPhongSPF`) now do, so the HWSS companion ladders price each companion wavelength with the SELECTED lobe's own `f_I cos / p_I` at THAT wavelength instead of the material's AGGREGATE `valueNM` over the hero lobe's density.  Three of the five turn out to be roughness/alpha-FREE in the transport weight and that is DERIVED, not assumed (DL-127's `Z(t)` and DL-177's `exp(-tan^2/alpha^2)` both cancel between `f_I cos` and `p_I`); Phong's exponent and Ashikmin's `NU`/`NV` genuinely survive and are read at `nm`.  The row's headline probe closed decisively: PT pel vs PT spectral `hwss TRUE` on `BDPTStrategyBalanceTest` topology L moved `1.60711 +/- 0.00433` -> `1.00093 +/- 0.00043` (historical isolated A/B, n = 3 per side) with the immune topology-M control unchanged, and its KNOWN-DEFECT band was retired for a real two-sided parity gate at 1.0.  **A THIRD ladder the row did not name was found and fixed in the same slice**: the two `hwssBetaNM` loops in `BDPTIntegrator.cpp`'s generators are read only for RR/guiding training (DL-126 review round 2), so the render-visible companion pricing for BDPT, VCM and MLT is `RecomputeSubpathThroughputNM`, which rescaled by the AGGREGATE ratio; it now asks the SPF for the selected lobe's kray ratio via a new `BDPTVertex::scatterType` (left `eRayUnknown` under a guiding-SUBSTITUTED direction so hero and companion stay on one estimator).  **That second half is a VARIANCE change, not a mean change** -- the conclusion of round 2, after round 1 reported the opposite.  The mean is unmoved once the sample count is adequate (see the spp sweep above); what moves is the TAIL, because `lobeRatio = krayComp/krayHero` is unbounded as `krayHero -> 0` while the aggregate ratio it replaced was a convex blend.  Measured tail, historical isolated A/B, n = 3 per side (BDPT `hwss TRUE`, topology P, nw 160, 256 spp): **p99 +33.4 / +13.2 / +24.9 % (R/G/B), max +19.4 / +15.0 / +81.5 %, means -0.8 / 0.0 / -0.8 %**.  The sampled synthetic-ri population does not support a near-zero clamp: of 1969 per-draw ratios above 4x on this material, ZERO have `krayHero < 1e-3` (worst 7.40x at `krayHero = 0.127`), on the synthetic ri at 0/35/70 deg (not a render-weighted tail distribution, nor a bound on extreme-grazing heroes), so these large ratios are legitimate spectral swings and the existing `krayHero > NEARZERO -> 0` guard remains unchanged; the synthetic sample is not a proof about every grazing event.  What survives from round 1: the `lobeRatio *= 4.0` sensitivity control (achromatic `0.986 -> 3.163`, B/R `-19.8 pp` -- the branch is far from inert), the CANCELLATION explanation for why the mean barely moves, and the topology-L attribution control (post/pre `0.998..1.002` per channel -- the change is confined to divergent lobe spectra).  The four historical B/R pre-vs-post point estimates span **-0.075 to +0.335 pp** and resolve neither a sign nor a hard bound; the original `0.2 pp` bound is refuted and superseded by DL69 section 6.3, and "directionally consistent" is withdrawn.  Topology P is now a FOUR-ESTIMATOR probe at `num_wavelengths 160`: the three agreeing estimators gated to parity at 1.5%, and BDPT `hwss TRUE`'s B/R outlier (matched-spp n/sd calibration at two spp levels in DL69 §6.3) pinned as DL-219.  The `CompositeSPF` residual is **DL-221** (ruled unclosable from the method's signature, and made AUDIBLE by a new `ISPF::PerLobeDensityFallbackName()` + a one-shot-per-class warning at all three ladders rather than left silent); a newly-found sixth per-lobe-density SPF, `TranslucentSPF`, is **DL-222** -- round 1 found that it had NOT been given the override, so it was as silent as before, and that DL-222's own recoverability claim was wrong: its interior Beer term reads `Magnitude(ri.ray.origin -> ri.ptIntersection)`, both fields of the handed-over `ri`, so it is exactly recoverable at the three LIVE call sites and lost only at DL-125's own new `RecomputeSubpathThroughputNM`, which REBUILDS a synthetic `ri` (fabricated origin, `glossyFilterWidth` 0) -- a contract widening now documented on `ISPF::EvaluateKrayNM` itself.  **DL-216** is the third row: every override divides by `p_I(lambda_c)` while the direction was drawn from `p_I(lambda_h)`; they coincide for reflectance-only chromatic materials (everything measured here) and differ under a chromatic SHAPE painter, quantified closed-form at `IsotropicPhongSPF` as `(N_h+1)/(N_c+1) * cos^(N_h-N_c)(alpha)` = 0.732x at the lobe peak rising through unity at 11.6415 deg.  The convention is deliberately UNCHANGED this round.  **Sibling audit REFUTED** the obvious third candidate: a material with NO `IBSDF` at all (`dielectric_material`, `perfectreflector_material`) never reaches PT's companion ladder, because `PathTracingIntegrator.cpp`'s `if( !pBRDFCur )` block delegates every live wavelength to `IntegrateFromHitNM` and breaks -- confirmed by render (a mirror scene's `hwss TRUE` reads RMSE `3.70e-3` against the pel reference, TIGHTER than hero-only's `1.67e-2`).  Red-proof `tests/HWSSCompanionKrayTest.cpp` (new): **91/0, red 46/45** (the round-1 P1: the originally recorded "43/44" is arithmetically impossible on this file and was a stale count from an earlier draft; corrected in all five places it appeared).  Cost (interleaved separately-built binaries): PT spectral `hwss TRUE` **+1.65%** (n = 3, t = 4.1) and BDPT spectral `hwss TRUE` **+3.02%** (n = 3, t = 3.3) on an all-`schlick_material` scene, and **+9.01%** (n = 5, t = 17.0) on `scenes/Tests/Hair/hair_styled.RISEscene` under `bdpt_spectral_rasterizer hwss TRUE` -- `HairSPF` already implemented `EvaluateKrayNM`, so `RecomputeSubpathThroughputNM`'s new branch now routes its four-lobe `EvalFsum` + `EvalPdf` twice per interior vertex per companion (round-1 P3). **2026-09-19 (debt-dl157 slice, round-5 response to review round 4):** CSG whole-record copies inherited a plane operand's `bProvablyNoInterior`; omitting the field in `AdoptCsgSurfacePayload` never cleared it. `CSGObject::IntersectRay` now clears it after all copies/adoption. Red **1196/26**; green **1222/0**, each after its own `build-test/TranslucentLobeConsistencyTest` relink. **2026-09-18 (debt-dl157 slice, REVIEW ROUND 3 on `1d4fad8b`):** one P1, four P2, three P3; all addressed. One NEW ledger row, **DL-220** (the reserved id assigned for it). **P1 -- `bProvablyNoInterior` is not a valid certification for `BezierPatchGeometry`, and the premise that licensed it was false.** Round 2 stamped the flag there on the reading "a single patch cannot enclose a volume". The class is not a single patch: `patches` is a `BezierPatchList` (a vector) behind a BSP/Octree, `AddPatch` appends, and `Job.cpp:6789`'s `.bezier` loader puts EVERY patch of a file into ONE geometry -- `models/raw/teapot.bezier` declares 28 in its first line, `models/bezier/aphrodite.bezier` and `f16.bezier` are closed solids, and `RayElementIntersection`'s own comment notes the winding varies patch-to-patch inside one file (so the back-face flip the stamp keyed on is a winding artifact, not a facing fact). At a genuine interior exit on such an object the flip fires and the stamp asserted "no interior" on exactly the hit that disproves it, after which `BuildLobeSet`'s stackless arm priced the ENTRY lobes at an exit. The setter was REMOVED -- `ClippedPlaneGeometry` is now the only certifier -- and the flag's own contract now states the general rule the round-2 wording missed: besides meshes, no geometry holding a COLLECTION of primitives can certify, however interior-free each primitive is alone, because an interior is a property of the whole surface. The suite gained the missing record kind, **`bezierExit`** -- a genuine interior exit on a closed multi-patch Bezier object with an INSIDE stack -- and its four flags are imported from a LIVE two-patch `BezierPatchGeometry` hit rather than hand-set, so reverting that one file is a real single-change A/B and a fixture that silently missed would be caught (four fixture-sanity checks assert the hit, the back-face flip, the canonical `-Z` stamped normal, and the absence of certification). That A/B: **1089 / 25** against **1114 / 0**, the 25 being one fixture check plus the 24 stackless `bezierExit` rows, reading **1.745299** (diffuse exit) / **0.647376** (backscatter) at tilt 0 RGB and 1.182365 / 1.035511 NM -- bit-for-bit the `dsMeshExit` pair, which is the point: the two records are physically identical. The other two A/Bs re-run against the enlarged suite read **1030 / 84** (frame split reverted; the same 84 rows, `planeBack`/`meshBack` at 1.260222 / 2.336741 RGB and 0.533741 / 2.206923 NM, on both entry points) and **1066 / 48** (`bOpenSheet` in place of the certification; now 48 rather than 24, because a Bezier hit carries `bOpenSheet` from production too -- which is DL-220). Latent, since no shipped scene binds `bezierpatch_geometry` to `translucent_material`. **P2-1/P2-2/P2-4 -- three stale transcriptions of numbers and rules this slice had already corrected elsewhere**: `TranslucentSPF.cpp`'s frame comment still carried round 1's superseded 0.840 / 1.402 (the measured pair is 1.260222 / 2.336741); the closure doc's canonical pseudocode still read `bOpenSheet` in the stackless clause, three lines above prose saying that flag is not the one; and the round-1 slice note still said the frame "is anchored to the SIDE", describing the rule round 1's own P1 replaced. All three now state the shipped split rule. **P2-3 -- the earlier stackless caller list omitted photon-map families; the current list is 16 calls in eight files.** The round-2 list stopped at the families this slice had converted or inspected and never swept the photon-map layer: `GlobalPelPhotonMap.cpp:97`, `GlobalSpectralPhotonMap.cpp:82`/`:135`, `CausticSpectralPhotonMap.cpp:82`/`:137` and `PhotonMap.h:705` all evaluate a BSDF with no stack. `FinalGatherShaderOp.cpp:220` was also omitted and is FIXED rather than recorded -- the same file's primary gather arm at `:521` was converted by this slice while the gradient-estimator arm was left behind, with the live `ior_stack` already a parameter of that helper -- so the round-2 claim "none of them a production integrator path" was wrong as written and now reads "the modern integrator paths". (`ILightManager::ComputeDirectLighting`'s forwarding loop is dropped from the list: it has no in-tree caller at all. `PathValueOps::EvalBSDF` has no production caller (two test callers).) **P3s**: `MakeDoubleSidedMeshExit` now DERIVES from `MakeMeshSheetBackFaceFirst` instead of rebuilding a byte-identical record, so the pair cannot drift into two spellings of one case (they are physically identical by design -- only the STACK separates them); the round-1 block's `534/0, red 498/30` carries the same "earlier file revision" label the 240 / 0 figures already carried; and DL-220 is filed for `BezierPatchGeometry.cpp:113`'s DL-96 `bOpenSheet = bDidFlip`, which rests on the identical false premise and whose consumer, `BSSRDFEntryFacing()`, will admit a closed Bezier solid's interior wall as a subsurface ENTRY.

**2026-09-18 (debt-dl157 slice, REVIEW ROUND 2 on `9cadc59a`):** two P1, two P2, three P3; all addressed, still no new ledger row (the findings widen DL-223 to four sub-cases). **P1-1 -- the tree did not COMPILE.** `tests/LightBVHTest.cpp`'s `MockSpotLight` is the only `ILightPriv` subclass outside `src/Library/Lights/`, it deliberately does not mark its overrides `override`, and round 1's `ComputeDirectLighting` parameter therefore left it abstract with the failure surfacing at three `new MockSpotLight` sites. **`make -C build/make/rise all` does not build tests, so a warning-free library build is not evidence that the tree compiles** -- the mock's own comment says it is the site to update when that virtual changes, and the slice walked past it. Fixed; `LightBVHTest` 20/0 and added to the gate; a grep of `tests/` confirms it is the only such implementer (the four test-side `IBSDF` subclasses are unaffected because `valueStateful{,NM}` are DEFAULTED). **P1-2 -- round 2's `ri.bOpenSheet ||` clause in the STACKLESS side rule was a measured REGRESSION.** That flag does not mean "no interior": for the two mesh classes it means UNCERTIFIED (`TriangleMeshGeometryIndexed` sets it whenever DL-143's weld could not certify watertightness -- all four glTF assets DL-143 audited, every one a closed solid -- and the non-indexed twin sets it on every double-sided hit), so at a genuine interior EXIT on such a mesh the stackless path priced the ENTRY lobes: `dsMeshExit` stackless gate-1 rows **1.745299** (diffuse exit) / **0.647376** (backscatter) at tilt 0, **984/24** against **1008/0** on the round-2 file (**1066/48** against **1114/0** re-run on the round-3 one, which adds a `bezierExit` record carrying the identical ratios). Rather than drop the clause, the review's preferred option was taken: a REAL certification, a new `RayIntersectionGeometric::bProvablyNoInterior` set only by `ClippedPlaneGeometry` and `BezierPatchGeometry` -- **the Bezier half of which review round 3 removed as unsound (P1), leaving `ClippedPlaneGeometry` the only certifier**. Both records now gate at 1.000 (the clipped-plane back face AND the uncertified mesh's interior exit); the mesh OPEN-SHEET back face stays a printed KNOWN-RESIDUAL at **1.680295 / 1.001460**, with its then-incomplete five-family list subsequently expanded on DL-223(4). Round 5 explicitly clears the flag in `CSGObject::IntersectRay` after whole-record copies and payload adoption; omitting it from `AdoptCsgSurfacePayload` alone had left it inherited: `bOpenSheet` is a property of the SURFACE and survives compositing, this is a property of the OBJECT, and a CSG tree built from planes can have an interior. **P2-1 -- round 2's red-proof was MIS-ATTRIBUTED, and the cause was a hole in the suite.** Gate 1 drove only the STACKLESS `value` while gates 2/3/4 drive the STACKED `Pdf`, so `valueStateful(wo, ri, &stack)` -- the entry point PT NEE, `EvalBSDFAtVertex` and BDPT's zero-exitance row all use -- had NO gate, and numbers measured on it by hand were written down against a gate that could not produce them. **P2-2** closed that hole (every gate-1 row now runs BOTH entry points; gate 4 pairs the stacked `Pdf` with the stacked `value` instead of crossing entry points), and with it the frame split's evidence lands where it belongs: single-change A/B **924 / 84 -> 1008 / 0** on the round-2 file (**1030 / 84 -> 1114 / 0** re-run on the round-3 one), the 84 being gate-1 rows on `planeBack` and `meshBack` reading **1.260222** (front reflection) and **2.336741** (transmission) at tilt 0 on BOTH entry points, with gate 3's `planeBack` TVD at **0.57972** against a measured floor of 0.08999 and the `sheetFront` control at 1.000000 throughout. Every figure in the doc, the ledger and CLAUDE.md is re-measured and re-attributed. **P3s**: the stale `240 / 0` counters are replaced by **1008 / 0** everywhere, with the round-1 file's own `240 / 0` / red `67 / 173` kept and labelled as such (the current file cannot be built against `bb2ccd80` -- it references `valueStateful`, `EvaluateKrayNM`, `bProvablyNoInterior` and the five-argument `TranslucentBSDF` constructor); DL-223's description and CLAUDE.md now both say FOUR sub-cases, matching its evidence cell; and the hair (`HasTrueGeomSide()` false) and stale-"inside"-stack cases are stated as MISPRICINGS (the other side's lobes are priced) rather than no-ops. Two new records in the suite -- `dsMeshExit` and `otherEnclosure` (an entry made while the walk is inside a DIFFERENT object, which separates "the stack is non-empty" from "the stack contains US") -- both read 1.000000 on every gate.

**2026-09-18 (debt-dl157 slice, REVIEW ROUND 1 on `ccea1a0a`):** one P1, three P2 and five P3 from an external review; all addressed, no new ledger row needed (the findings widen DL-223). **P1 -- the round-1 lobe frame was derived from the SIDE and that is wrong on a REACHABLE record.** A camera ray striking the BACK FACE FIRST of an open double-sided sheet has a not-inside stack (true: it never entered) and a leaving ray (true: it hit the back face); `clippedplane_geometry`'s `doublesided` defaults to TRUE and translucent is what authors put on open sheets. Round 1 asserted that pair away in three places -- the closure doc's section 3.1, `BuildLobeSet`'s own comment, and the JUSTIFICATION for deleting `PTGuidingMISPartitionTest`'s (b) probe -- and the assertion was false. The rule now SPLITS by side, each half derived from what `Scatter` literally does: the ENTRY side uses `Scatter`'s own ray-anchored expression (so the back-face record matches), and the EXIT side uses `-geomNRaw`, which is not an approximation but the same value Scatter computes there (its exit branch requires `containsCurrent()`, and a ray inside an object travels outward at its boundary hit) and which is ray-INDEPENDENT, so it survives `EvalBSDFAtVertex`'s rebuild -- the property that keeps the BDPT light subpath alive (`reached=512`, not 0). Red-proof, new row [E] in `tests/TranslucentLobeConsistencyTest.cpp`: with the round-1 frame the back-face-first record reads **1.260222** (front reflection) and **2.336741** (transmission) at tilt 0 while the front-face CONTROL reads 1.000000; with the split rule both read **1.000000** at every tilt on both pipes. 534/0, red 498/30 (earlier test-file revision). The deleted (b) probe is restored on that record (the sentinel and the live stack there evaluate two different lobe SHAPES: 0.00702687 vs 0.109153); `PTGuidingMISPartitionTest` **101/0**. **P2-1** -- `CompositeMaterial` did not forward `ScattersFullSphere()` while `GetBSDF()` hands NEE the top layer's BSDF verbatim, so `composite { top = translucent }` (`mat_wax_gold` in `scenes/Tests/Materials/composite_material.RISEscene`) presented a transmitting BSDF to a `bFullSphere == false` NEE. Forwarded from WHICHEVER layer's BSDF the composite presents, not an OR -- a Lambertian top over a translucent bottom presents the non-transmitting BSDF and must stay false, which is a new gated control. Rendered, isolating that one file: PT **+0.2352 %** (1.041349 -> 1.043799, sd 1.6e-5 / 6.0e-6, n=3), BDPT **-0.0015 %** (unchanged, as predicted -- it never had the gate), PT/BDPT **-1.699 % -> -1.467 %**. `SourceHygieneTest`'s closed claimer list grows to SIX and learns the cached-member form, because a claimer its matcher cannot see is worse than one it lists. **P2-2** -- every rig built `N` from a `UniformScalarPainter`, so `Scatter`'s PER-CHANNEL-exponent branch (three Phong rays, single-channel `kray`, four lobes in the set) was untested; rig [D] adds it, and it immediately found that the TEST's own `MaxValue` reduction was the DL-69 aggregate-over-per-lobe pairing in miniature (0.664694 / 0.736752). Reducing by the lobe's OWN channel fixes it and is numerically identical on every other rig. **P2-3** -- `ILight::ComputeDirectLighting{,NM}` carried no stack, so LightSampler's Step-1 zero-exitance sweep and BDPT's mirroring s==1 row inferred the side geometrically while the other three NEE arms got the live stack; a trailing defaulted `const IORStack*` now threads it through all four light types and both call sites, matching the parameter's own established pattern on that virtual. Also threaded: PT's three guiding `PTEvalBSDFAtSurface` sites (which already handed the same `iorStack` to `PTEvalPdfAtSurface` one line away). **P3s**: `EvaluateKrayNM` implemented on `TranslucentSPF` (**DL-222, opened on the concurrent `debt-dl125` branch, can be STRUCK at merge**) and gated at 1e-12 against `ScatterNM`'s own `krayNM` over then-four record kinds x six tilts x three wavelengths (nine record kinds in the current suite); the "cannot drift" claim in `TranslucentSPF.h` corrected (Scatter/ScatterNM do NOT call `BuildLobeSet` -- they are kept in step by gates 1/3/5, not by construction); `EvalLobe`'s comment corrected about which gate catches a normalisation error and which a shape error; DL-127 cited as the precedent on DL-223(2).

**2026-09-18 (debt-dl157 slice, branched from `master` `bb2ccd80`):** DL-157, DL-41 and DL-38 all CLOSED; DL-223 and DL-224 opened (the two reserved ids, both used). The three closed rows are one defect seen from three sides -- `TranslucentSPF::Scatter`/`ScatterNM` (the sampler), `TranslucentSPF::Pdf`/`PdfNM` (the MIS-partner density) and `TranslucentBSDF::value`/`valueNM` (what NEE and every BDPT/VCM connection pay) were three independent transcriptions of a four-lobe model, and only one cell of one of them described a lobe the sampler draws. They are now three readings of ONE per-hit lobe set, `TranslucentSPFDetail::BuildLobeSet`. The mixture weights are EXACT in closed form rather than replay-quadrature estimates (contrast DL-67/DL-98/DL-99) for two structural reasons: every lobe's `kray` here is direction-independent, so `RandomlySelect`'s realized probability IS the raw weight ratio; and on each side the two lobes occupy complementary half-spaces. Recipe option **(a)** was taken for the entry-vs-exit bit -- a DEFAULTED `IBSDF::valueStateful{,NM}` pair that forwards to `value` for all 18 other implementers (none changed).  **The lobe FRAME SPLITS BY SIDE** (this sentence described only the round-1 rule until review round 3's P2-4): the ENTRY side uses literally `Scatter`'s own RAY-ANCHORED expression, `Dot(geomNRaw, rayDir) < 0 ? geomNRaw : -geomNRaw`, because a side-derived frame is wrong on a reachable record (an open double-sided sheet struck back-face-first -- 1.260222 / 2.336741 at tilt 0, round 1's P1); the EXIT side uses `-geomNRaw`, which is not an approximation but the same value `Scatter` computes there and is ray-INDEPENDENT, which is what testing proved load-bearing: `EvalBSDFAtVertex` rebuilds the record as `Ray(vertex.position, -wo)` and the two BDPT generators pass `(wi, wo)` in OPPOSITE roles, so a ray-anchored EXIT frame inverted the lobe frame on every light-subpath vertex and killed the walk (`reached=512 -> 0`). `TranslucentMaterial::ScattersFullSphere()` is now true; DL-157 and DL-41 together are that capability's own safety condition. Red-proof `tests/TranslucentLobeConsistencyTest.cpp` **1222 / 0** (round-5 CSG red: **1196 / 26**; round-3/4 file: 1114 / 0; the ROUND-1 file revision read 240 / 0 against its own red of **67 / 173** on `bb2ccd80` and the ROUND-2 revision 1008 / 0; the current file cannot be built against `bb2ccd80` at all, so later figures are SINGLE-CHANGE A/Bs at their stated historical file revisions). Four gate suites that PINNED the defective behaviour were re-gated, none by loosening a tolerance: `TranslucentSpectralParityTest` (1918/0), `TranslucentIORStackTest` (ALL PASSED, was 20 CHECK(S) FAILED -- its rig had `scattering 0`, so `value`'s phantom case-1 lobe was what let 371/512 inward guided candidates through), `SPFPdfConsistencyTest` (the `Translucent` Part 2b pin 0.44469 in [0.40,0.48] retired for the ordinary gate; the two shares now agree to 2.3e-4), and `TranslucentDoubleSidedTest` sub-test 4 (same lune fractions, new mechanism, plus a value-vs-Pdf support check reading 0/40000). `PTGuidingMISPartitionTest`'s DL-74 premise probe was retargeted for the second time in two rounds and is 99/0. **DL-224 is the more surprising of the two new rows**: a plain `lambertian_material` sphere under the same `relief_modifier` reads PT/BDPT +63.5 % at |scale| 0.20 and -0.004 % at 0, which refutes `DL111_DL112_TRANSMISSION_PUSH_GATES.md` §14.3's attribution of that tilt-driven gap to DL-157. Full account, every number, and the sibling audit: [DL157_TRANSLUCENT_ONE_FUNCTION.md](DL157_TRANSLUCENT_ONE_FUNCTION.md). **Ledger hygiene**: `master` carried TWO `| DL-157 |` rows, the first of them glued to DL-131's row with a missing newline; the newline is restored and both are struck. This slice's branch does not update the "Authoritative totals" block.

**2026-09-18 (debt-dl207 slice, branched from `master` `fd92c780`):** DL-207 and DL-196 both CLOSED; no new rows opened (reserved ids DL-219/DL-220 were not needed). **DL-207** — `BDPTIntegrator.cpp`'s deterministic zero-exitance-light sweep (`EvaluateAllStrategiesImpl`) now detects `eyeEnd.isBSSRDFEntry` before its general `isConnectible`/`GetBSDF()` gates and prices such a vertex through the SAME stack-local `BSSRDFEntryBSDF`/`RandomWalkEntryBSDF` adapters (`BSSRDFEntryAdapters.h`) `PathTracingIntegrator.cpp`'s own BSSRDF-entry NEE already uses, instead of the material's raw aggregate front-reflection `IBSDF` — the entry vertex's position/normal are the diffusion-profile ENTRY point, not the camera-visible exit point that reflection lobe is defined at, so the pre-fix call priced the wrong physical quantity at the wrong location. The `isConnectible` gate is deliberately bypassed for this branch only (this sweep's MIS weight is unconditionally 1.0 for every zero-exitance light, so the general-connection-strategy pdf-consistency reason for that gate does not apply). VCM untouched (no directional-light path at all, a pre-existing separate gap). **DL-196** — `PathTracingIntegrator::IntegrateFromHitHWSS` gains a trailing `castRRCompensation_` parameter, forwarded to its `firstHit`/`startDepth` NM-delegation fallbacks and to its own "PART 2: NEE (HWSS)" call gated `depth == startDepth`, mirroring DL-185's RGB/NM twins exactly; its three mid-path `IntegrateFromHitNM` delegations correctly keep the default (each is a fresh vertex, not a `RayCaster::CastRay` re-entry). `PathTracingShaderOp::PerformOperationHWSS` forwards `rs.castRRCompensation`. `tests/OptimalMISTrainingSitesTest.cpp`'s new `RunNeeCastRRTrainingFoldHWSS` (mirroring DL-185's own recipe via `CastRayHWSS`): pre-fix trained-moment ratio reads exactly 1.0 (compensation dropped), post-fix exactly 4.0 (`= rrCompensation^2`), with the returned-radiance ratio unchanged at 2.0 both before and after (render-neutral). Confirmed inert in every shipped render today (the only production `CastRayHWSS` caller passes a default-constructed `RAY_STATE`). **2026-09-18 (debt-dl176 slice, branched from `master` `a9b1d3d8`):** DL-177 and DL-176 both CLOSED; DL-211 and DL-212 opened. **DL-177** turned out to be THREE defects, not the one the row names, and they only make sense in dependency order: `Ward*SPF::GenerateSpecularRay` stored the TRUE solid-angle density of its own half-vector times `cos^4(theta_h)` (measured exactly that way per draw, `worst |ratio - cos^4| = 0.000000` at every alpha/incidence/model); `Ward*SPF::Pdf`/`PdfNM` were raw-albedo mixtures, which is exact ONLY while the krays are direction-independent -- precisely the premise the kray fix removes, and precisely why DL-98/DL-99 had recorded Ward as immune; and the kray itself was the reflectance painter's colour rather than `Rs (h.wo) cos^3(theta_h) sqrt(cos_o/cos_i)`. The aggregate `Pdf` is the DL-67/DL-98/DL-99 construction with two Ward-specific rulings recorded rather than assumed: NO second quadrature is needed (Ward's diffuse kray is the constant `Rd`, unlike Ashikmin's), and DL-99's `MaxValue` reduction-order trap cannot bite (`MaxValue(Rs * R) == MaxValue(Rs) * R` exactly, `R` being a nonnegative scalar). `tests/WardDensityKrayTest.cpp` 332/0, red 108/158 -- and the file proves its OWN reference densities first (integrate to 1.0000005..1.0000051; TVD 0.0029/0.0040/0.0062 against a 400k-draw histogram of real half-vectors, with the sampler's accept test applied to the reference, without which a correct build reads 0.1256 at alpha 0.6). **The tail decision the row was held open on is (a) leave it, on data:** the "unbounded `1/sqrt(nl nv)`" premise is half wrong, since the `nl` half cancels against the cosine and the density, leaving `kray <= Rs/sqrt(cos_i)` -- a per-shading-point CONSTANT (measured per-draw maxima 0.486 / 0.602 / 1.198 / 3.687 at 80/85/89/89.9 deg against bounds 1.200 / 1.694 / 3.785 / 11.968, estimator relative s.e. `<= 0.0040` everywhere, p99.9 at the max not above it); the 685x/1085x figures in the row were measured THROUGH the broken pdf and read 1.000000 through the fixed one; and the Ward-Duer / Geisler-Moroder-Duer bounded variant would have to change `value()` in lockstep or re-create DL-127, i.e. it is a model substitution that changes the shipped look, not a bug fix. **Tolerances were re-derived DOWNWARD** in three suites rather than left slack (`SPFBSDFConsistencyTest` 0.25 -> 0.01, `SPFPdfConsistencyTest` chi2 un-skipped and the 10% band retired, `SchlickKrayBRDFConsistencyTest` section 6 promoted from printed-only to a per-draw gate on every multi-emit row), each with an isolated pre-fix A/B; the quadrature column in the furnace suite is UNCHANGED by the fix, which is what refutes its old "quadrature under-integrates the sharp specular peak" note. **DL-176** was NOT a `CookTorranceSPF` defect and NOT seed luck -- a 10-seed sweep on the unfixed harness puts the filed row's true mean at 938.26 (z = +11.02), above critical -- but a harness defect: Part 3's expected counts were never normalised to the observed total, so a legitimately SUB-unit `int Pdf` (that SPF emits nothing on 1.5% of calls at 60 deg) injected ~100 chi2 of pure normalisation artifact. Fixed by rescaling (the degree of freedom it costs was already being subtracted), folding the discarded trailing merge group back in, and evaluating the statistic at 8 EXPLICIT seeds gated on the seed-mean `z`. New rows: **DL-211** (`CookTorranceSPF::Pdf` prices the `kSelFloor`-floored specular lobe at an authored literally-black specular painter, which `Scatter` can never emit: `int Pdf = 1.00001` vs an emission rate of 0.998014, chi2 `z = +3.58`) and **DL-212** (the Ward analogue of DL-178 -- the MODEL's own grazing energy gain, up to 1.99x `Rs` at 89.9 deg, now reaching indirect transport as well as NEE). Full account, derivations, red numbers, tail data, re-derived tolerances, render deltas and cost: [DL177_WARD_DENSITY_AND_KRAY.md](DL177_WARD_DENSITY_AND_KRAY.md). Full derivation, site table, sibling audit and render numbers in [DL96_BSSRDF_OPEN_SHEET_ENTRY.md](DL96_BSSRDF_OPEN_SHEET_ENTRY.md); `docs/DL70_GEOM_NORMAL_ORIENTATION_SITES.md` gained closure notes on both rows. **2026-09-18 (debt-dl170 slice, branched from `master` `d163cc54`):** DL-170
and DL-171 CLOSED (both found by the `debt-dl103` slice's own sibling audit,
no source-doc heading). DL-170 -- `PathTracingIntegrator::IntegrateFromHitHWSS`
broadcast ONE hero-wavelength aggregate MIS partner to every companion
wavelength's emitter-hit/env-escape weight; fixed with a new per-lane
`misBsdfPdfComp[]` array (declared in the same local scope as the existing
`throughputComp[]` bundle, NOT in `RAY_STATE`), recomputed once per bounce at
each active companion's own wavelength via the identical `ISPF::PdfNM` call
PART 2's NEE arm already uses. `tests/PTGuidingMISPartitionTest.cpp`: 98/0
(was 95/3; companion lanes read 6.5/23.0/47.0% off `L_env(nm)` pre-fix,
0.02/0.14/0.29% post-fix; hero lane bit-identical, no RNG stream touched).
Cost: isolated A/B on a realistic mixed scene, n=3, user CPU +3.1% (DL-103's
own sibling fix measured +2.11%). `RayCaster::CastRayHWSS`'s own escape MIS
weight and `EmissionShaderOp`'s HWSS reader were audited and found
structurally unreachable with a real per-vertex partner in the current call
graph (documented, not fixed). DL-171 -- the legacy shader-op chain
(`DistributionTracingShaderOp`, `ReflectionShaderOp`, `RefractionShaderOp`)
never populated `RAY_STATE::bsdfPdf`/`bsdfMisPdf` on any continuation, so
`EmissionShaderOp` took a BSDF-sampled emitter hit at full weight (or zero,
when suppressed) while `DirectLightingShaderOp`'s NEE arm weighted against
the material's real aggregate density -- the pair did not partition to one
in either direction. Fixed by stamping the same aggregate `Pdf()`/`PdfNM()`
(DL-41-guarded) on all three, gated on `scat.isDelta` for the
Reflection/Refraction pair (0 for the historical delta lobe, the aggregate
for a non-delta one, e.g. a misrouted `GGXSPF` rough-reflection lobe).
`FinalGatherShaderOp` is a CONFIRMED REFUTAL, not a fix: every one of its
three continuations sets `considerEmission = false` unconditionally, so
`EmissionShaderOp`'s weight block never fires there regardless of the
partner -- the row's own static grep matched, the dynamic defect cannot
manifest. New `tests/LegacyChainMISPartnerTest.cpp`: 11/0 (was 9/2; measured
pre-fix env-background furnace +17.7% OVER, area-emitter
`bForceCheckEmitters=TRUE` +57.6% OVER, both controls unchanged; post-fix
+0.02%/+0.53%). Sibling audit: no fifth legacy-chain file exists (`grep -rl
"IRayCaster::RAY_STATE rs2" src/Library/Shaders/*.cpp` matches exactly these
four plus `PathTracingIntegrator.cpp`, already correctly handled, and
`BDPTIntegrator.cpp`, a separate, already-audited integrator). **New debt
opened: DL-209** (S, physics-bias) -- the DL-171 fix assumes a matching NEE
strategy exists wherever it stamps a non-delta partner, which
`DistributionTracingShaderOp` cannot verify from within itself; one shipped
scene, `scenes/Tests/Shaders/dt_with_irrcache.RISEscene`, pairs
`distributiontracing_shaderop` with `force_check_emitters TRUE` and NO
`DirectLightingShaderOp` at all, and measures a real, small (~2.5%,
isolated-A/B-confirmed) one-directional UNDER-shift on its illuminated
receiver -- every OTHER shipped scene using this op pairs it with
`DefaultDirectLighting` and is unaffected. Full record:
[DL170_DL171_HWSS_AND_LEGACY_MIS_PARTNERS.md](DL170_DL171_HWSS_AND_LEGACY_MIS_PARTNERS.md).

**2026-09-18 (debt-dl170 slice, P1 review follow-up, same branch, same base
`d163cc54`):** a supervisor-relayed external review of the DL-171 closure
above found ONE P1: the "default (`considerEmission` suppressed)" row's
0.115626 was claimed "genuinely and correctly BELOW the closed form" when
it was actually BIT-IDENTICAL to base with the three touched files reverted
-- proving the UNDER sign the row's own two-named-signs description called
out was never actually addressed by the fix above; `DirectLightingShaderOp`
unconditionally applies `w_nee = PowerHeuristic(p_light, p_bsdf)` even when
its sibling `DistributionTracingShaderOp` has suppressed the competing
BSDF-hit strategy entirely via `considerEmission=false`, so there was no
strategy left for the stamped partner to matter to. RULING (implemented,
not re-litigated): "a strategy's MIS partner is the sibling strategy that
ACTUALLY EXISTS in that shader-op chain; an absent sibling means weight 1,"
both directions. Fix (`8e7bdde7`): two new `RAY_STATE` fields,
`chainHasNEEOp`/`chainHasBsdfContinuationOp`, resolved once at construction
by `StandardShader` (`dynamic_cast` over its flat op list) and per-shade-call
by `AdvancedShader::ResolveChainFlagsForDepth` (needed for real: `blurry_
glass.RISEscene`'s `adv` shader pairs `dt`/`DefaultRefraction` at disjoint
depth windows) -- `DistributionTracingShaderOp` stamps its non-delta
partner only when a `DirectLightingShaderOp` sibling exists (else 0, closing
DL-209 as a side effect -- see that row); `DirectLightingShaderOp` forwards
the converse flag as a new trailing parameter on `LightSampler::
EvaluateDirectLighting{,NM}` (`bBsdfSamplingPartnerExists`, chosen over a
literal null `pMaterial` because `EvaluateDirectLighting` also reads
`pMaterial` for `ScattersFullSphere()`, load-bearing for hair/fabric/weave
full-sphere NEE -- a null would have silently regressed that). The pre-MIS
`considerEmission=false`-whenever-BSDF-present suppression hack in
`DistributionTracingShaderOp` is retired as obsolete now that a real partner
exists; `bForceCheckEmitters` stays wired, now a no-op for that removed
clause (the orthogonal caustic-map suppression it also controls is
untouched). Census of every shader-op chain shape in the 459-scene corpus
(`shaderop` line grep, resolved to chunk type) confirms the auto-derived
answer is correct for every shipped shape, with no scene-language change.
`tests/LegacyChainMISPartnerTest.cpp` rewritten with explicit
`chainHasNEEOp`/`chainHasBsdfContinuationOp` parameters per row: 20/0 (was
11/0); the suppressed row now reads 0.115616 at
`chainHasBsdfContinuationOp=TRUE` (matching the reviewer's own 0.115626 to
five decimal places, kept as an explicit contrast) and 0.203427 (0.14%
error) at `=FALSE` -- the "0.1156 -> ~0.2037" move the ruling specified.
Render-level re-measurement (isolated A/B): `dt_with_irrcache.RISEscene`
(DL-209's own scene) 71.94302 post-fix vs the original pre-any-fix baseline
71.97167 (0.04% apart, MC noise) -- DL-209 confirmed closed at the render
level; `pillow.RISEscene` (the reviewer's own named example) ratio
1.0000123 pre/post -- correctly UNCHANGED, since its only active lights are
five `omni_light` (point/delta) sources and its two
`lambertian_luminaire_material` definitions are never bound to any object,
so no BSDF-sampled continuation in this scene can ever hit an emitter
regardless of the fix (a correction to the reviewer's own framing of that
one example, not a dispute of the ruling or the underlying defect). **DL-209
struck CLOSED in this same commit** (its own `chainHasNEEOp=FALSE` contrast
row: 0.0879028, 56.9% low, matching DL-209's historical shape; fixed row:
0.203133, 0.29% error). Gate (this round, full re-run against `8e7bdde7`):

`EnvLightBalanceTest` 123/0, `FinalGatherSpectralTest` 8/0,
`RefractiveRadianceScalingTest` 41/0, `CstDeriveGoldenTest` 452 MATCH / 0
DRIFT, `SourceHygieneTest` 167/0, `AgentChunkCrudTest` 3816/0,
`BDPTStrategyBalanceTest` 123/0 (`TestPTSpectralHWSSKnownDefect` still 1.619,

**2026-09-18 (debt-dl186 slice, P1 review follow-up, same branch, same base `50dbc4d8`):** a supervisor-relayed external review of the DL-186 landing above found a P1 (row not reopened -- the fix landed in the same slice before merge): `add_pbr_metallic_roughness_material` squares Blender's perceptual `coat_roughness` into a GGX alpha only on the NUMERIC fallback path -- `resolve_material_scalar_slot`'s texture branch bound the channel view UNSQUARED, so the SAME authored value (0.5) read `CoatedBRDF::GetCoatRoughness()` alpha 0.25 through the numeric path and 0.5 (2x) through a texture. Fixed by adding a `square` parameter to `resolve_material_scalar_slot`: true wraps the shared channel view in a `RISE_API_CreateMultiplyScalarPainter(view, view)` self-product, cached under a further-derived `::sq` name (so a texture bound unsquared to `sheen_roughness_texture_painter_name` and squared to `coat_roughness_texture_painter_name` gets two independent, correct results); the coat_roughness call site now passes `square=true`. Sibling audit (per the review's own instruction): `sheen_roughness_texture_painter_name` needs no squaring on either path (Charlie alpha is never perceptual-then-squared); hair's `beta_m`/`beta_n` scalar-texture slots need none either (the Chiang BCSDF consumes them directly); `subsurface_roughness` has no texture-driven variant in this ABI, and its numeric path is ALREADY squared uniformly inside `SubSurfaceScatteringBSDF`/`SubSurfaceScatteringSPF` themselves (`alpha = roughness^2`), so no bridge-side change belongs there. Red-proof: `tests/BlenderBridgeCoatTest.cpp` gained `TestTextureDrivenCoatRoughnessSquares-LikeNumeric` and `TestTextureDrivenCoatRoughnessSquaresPerTexel`, both probing `CoatedBRDF::GetCoatRoughness()` directly; isolated A/B (revert only `rise_blender_bridge.cpp` to this slice's pre-P1 commit, keep the new tests) measured 4/59 failures (numeric alpha 0.25 vs textured 0.5, exactly the reviewer's reported symptom), confirming genuine red. Full suite counts: `tests/BlenderBridgeCoatTest.cpp` 59/0 (was 49/0), `tests/BlenderBridgeSSSTest.cpp` 29/0 (unaffected -- audited, not touched), `tests/BlenderBridgeFabricTest.cpp` 43/0, `tests/BlenderBridgeHairTest.cpp` 133/0, `test_hair_export.py` 77/0, `SourceHygieneTest` 167/0 -- all unaffected by this fix (native/header-only change plus new test rows). Fix commit note: `Co-Authored-By: Claude Sonnet 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>`. This slice's branch HEAD does not itself update the "Authoritative totals on `master`" line below; that recount happens at merge.

ABI v12 -> v13: `rise_blender_material` gained six `coat_*` fields (numeric + the v12 texture-exception pattern for weight/roughness) and five `subsurface_*` fields, plus a new `RISE_BLENDER_MATERIAL_RANDOMWALK_SSS` model value. `add_pbr_metallic_roughness_material` wraps the PBR base in a `coated_material` layer using the identical `<name>::pbrbase` intermediate-name + deferred-emission mechanism DL-18 established for sheen; a new `add_randomwalk_sss_material` builds a `randomwalk_sss_-material` as a SEPARATE model (mutually exclusive with PBR/Coat/Sheen, mirroring the pre-existing PBR-vs-DIELECTRIC exclusivity for heavy Transmission). `exporter.py` reads Blender's Coat/Subsurface sockets and performs the Radius/Scale/Base-Colour -> sigma_a/sigma_s conversion (a documented single-scattering-albedo approximation, not a photon-beam-diffusion fit) since it already holds the raw untextured colour triple that conversion needs. Renders (no Blender/bpy in this sandbox — the "Blender-authored" side is the exact documented arithmetic conversion, run through the same bridge functions the two new test files exercise directly): a coated red plastic sphere vs the same base with no coat, sphere-region mean R channel (sRGB PNG) 110.8 -> 83.6 (coat darkens, matching `coated_material.RISEscene`'s own documented "wet darkening" direction); a Subsurface sphere (Base Color (0.9,0.85,0.7), Radius (1.0,0.4,0.2), Scale 0.05) renders a warm skin-tone bias (mean RGB 63.8/55.9/46.1), consistent with the per-channel sigma_a the conversion assigns. `docs/BLENDER_MATERIAL_TRANSLATION.md` "Coat and Subsurface", `src/Blender/README.md`, `tests/README.md`, and this file's own CLAUDE.md Blender-bridge fact bullet updated. **2026-09-17 (debt-dl25 slice, branched from `master` `be8904dc`):** DL-25
CLOSED (WETNESS_COAT_DESIGN.md §12 item 6b / §4(g) — the expression VM
gained `sample(name)`/`sample_scalar(name)`, and `add_wetness`'s clause 2
now darkens a textured/procedural substrate per-texel instead of refusing
it). Fix commit `98836b7c` (`ExpressionEval.h`, `ExpressionPainter.h`,
`ExpressionMemo.h`, `Job.cpp`, `ChunkParserRegistry.cpp`, `AgentSession.h`/
`.cpp`, `AgentMcpAdapter.cpp`, `AgentChatCodecs.cpp`, `AgentRpc.cpp`),
red-proof + green in the same commit (`tests/TextureExpressionVMTest.cpp`
tests 75-77, 846/0 -> 888/0). The sibling audit found no comparable
painter-reference gap elsewhere in the VM (the existing
`occlusion`/`proximity`/`convexity`/`interior` signal family already has
its own dispatch channel; `ramp`/`mix`/`vec3` are the only other bespoke-
parse builtins and none of them need a chunk-manager lookup).

**REVIEW PASS, same day, same slice (merged `master` `e1772631` first).**
Three P1s from an external review, each red-proved before its fix.
**P1-1** (`128cbd60` + census `f0f9d4f7`): the builtins evaluated their
bound painter at a SYNTHETIC five-field hit record, so a sampled texture
lost its mip filter, a sampled expression lost `curv`/`fw`/the signal
channel, a sampled `vertex_color_painter` its vertex colour and a sampled
`iridescent_painter` its view ray -- 18 red checks. Fixed by forwarding
the CALLER'S record (`ExprEvalContext::pHit`), which grows the L2 memo key
by `ExpressionMemo::PainterSampleHitKey` (35 -> 52 fields, `Tables` 2432 ->
2976 bytes); the alternative, declaring a sampling program un-memoable,
measured 3.35x more user CPU and was rejected. A `SourceHygieneTest`
census over every `ri.<field>` read in `src/Library/Painters` now guards
the key's field enumeration. **P1-2** (`3766b5ca`): `add_wetness` set
`hasReadableColor` on the textured branch while leaving `baseR/G/B` at 0,
so the RPC emitted `baseColor: [0,0,0]` and the message offered to have
darkened "the 0 0 0 that was there"; split into a `hasBaseColor` gate, the
field OMITTED rather than zeroed, and the message now names the substrate
painter. **P1-3** (`86fd16c2`): the row's own recipe asked for a render
regression and there was none -- `tests/WetTextureDetailRenderTest.cpp`
(23/0) is it. Plus the P2/P3 set: `sample_scalar()` now reads the 549 nm
`ScalarPainterRGB::kSingleSampleChannel` rather than lane 0 (611 nm); both
chunk descriptors and WETNESS_COAT_DESIGN.md document that `sample()` is
RGB `GetColor` only and that a metallic-named material gets NO darkening
at all (an earlier draft of §6.3/§12-6b claimed it took the sample()
branch); the guard is RAII; and the self-reference check is now PER
MANAGER, so a colour `expression_painter` may `sample_scalar()` a
`scalar_painter` of the same name (red-proved, rows (e2)/(e3)).

**Two new rows opened by the review: DL-164** (`sample()` references are
invisible to the CST reference graph, so `DocRename` / remove-analysis
silently break them -- loud at the next load, not a wrong render) and
**DL-165** (`sample()` is RGB-only, so a spectral/blackbody substrate
loses its spectrum with no diagnostic). Both ids were RESERVED on
`master` (they sit in the totals block's "minus" list); the recount at
merge must REMOVE them from it. This slice's snapshot: 1 row
closed (DL-25), 2 opened. This slice's branch HEAD does not itself update
the "Authoritative totals on `master`" line below; that recount happens at
merge.

**2026-09-17 (debt-dl18 slice, P1 fix-up round, same branch, same base
`be8904dc`):** a supervisor-relayed external review reproduced a P1 in
the DL-18 landing above (row not reopened -- the fix landed in the
same slice before merge): `add_pbr_metallic_roughness_material` baked
`emission_painter_name` into the PBR base unconditionally, then handed
that base to `AddFabricMaterial` as the sheen substrate;
`FabricMaterial::IsSupportedSubstrate` refuses `GetEmitter() != 0`, so
Emission Strength > 0 + Sheen Weight > 0 on one Principled node failed
`add_material` outright and `rise_blender_scene_to_job` aborts the
whole job on one material's failure. Audit-by-bug-pattern found the
identical latent bug in `GLTFSceneImporter.cpp`'s `KHR_materials_sheen`
handling (which this bridge mirrors) and a second sibling in its
`KHR_materials_clearcoat` handling (`CoatedMaterial::IsSupportedSubstrate`
is the same refusal). Fixed all three: build the PBR base WITHOUT
emission when it is about to be wrapped, wrap it, then re-attach
emission at the OUTER layer via `AddLambertianLuminaireMaterial` (a
fully generic wrapper, physically equivalent to baking emission
straight into the GGX base) -- keeps BOTH the sheen/clearcoat layer
and the emission; RISE already had a way to make a wrapped material
emissive, so the reserved id **DL-153 was NOT used** (no residual
debt -- see the DL-18 row's closure note for why the "warn and drop"
fallback option was not needed). Fix commits `e80f9f23` (Blender
bridge) + `83d0181b` (glTF importer + Python marshalling test + docs).
`tests/BlenderBridgeFabricTest.cpp`: 43/0 (was 40/6 red).
`tests/GLTFSheenImportTest.cpp`: 35/0 (was 22/1 red).
`tests/GLTFClearcoatImportTest.cpp`: 27/0 (was 14/1 red).
`test_hair_export.py`: 65/0 (was 64/0). Re-run clean, unaffected:
`FabricMaterialChunkTest` 170/0, `WeaveMaterialChunkTest` 296/0,
`GLTFLoaderTest` 3321/0, `AgentChunkCrudTest` 3809/0,
`SceneGraphNodeApiTest` 317/0, `SourceHygieneTest` 165/0. Clean
rebuild (library, Blender bridge dylib, all touched tests), zero
warnings. No new rows opened -- the whole-job-abort-on-one-material-
failure behaviour was considered and found to be pre-existing,
uniform, cross-cutting design (every `add_*` loop in
`rise_blender_scene_to_job`, and `GLTFSceneImporter`'s own material
loop, already behave this way), not a defect reachable only through
this bug. This slice's branch HEAD does not itself update the

non-arity-checked `DispatchChunkParameters`) entirely, so they were exactly

painter` center, `gerstnerwave_painter` wind_dir, `polynomial_function2d_

uv_offset params, `target_orientation` on the 4 camera chunks, `orthographic_
camera`'s `viewport_scale`); `rect_light`'s `size` was audited and left
alone (already arity-checked via a pre-existing `HasExactNumericArity` call,
a different and already-safe pattern).  Descriptor-consumer switches over
`ValueKind` updated for the new enumerator: `SchemaGen.cpp` (`TypeFor`/
`ArrayLenFor`), `SuggestionEngine.cpp` (`MakeValuePlaceholder`),
`AgentSession.cpp` (`IsNumericKind`).  Also opened **DL-156** (new, distinct
defect, NOT the same bug pattern — an atomicity gap in the INCREMENTAL
derive engine's rollback design that DL-32 round 2's arity hard-error
exposed, not a parsing-arity bug itself): see its row above.  See the DL-32

debt-dl200 slice note (2026-09-18, branched from `master` `caa0432a`; does NOT sum into the totals below -- recomputed at merge): **DL-201 CLOSED**, **DL-200 CLOSED**, **DL-217 opened and closed in the same slice**, **DL-218 opened**.  Three things in this slice contradict what the rows themselves said, and they are the part worth carrying forward.  (1) **DL-201's own prescribed fix -- a per-pixel weight accumulator in `SplatFilm` -- is mathematically WRONG and was rejected on derivation**: a splat estimator's denominator must count every lane that COULD have deposited at a pixel, not the ones that did, so a weight summed at deposit time turns the estimator into a mean over CONTRIBUTING samples and over-brightens sparse splat regions without bound.  (`SplatPixel::weight` already counts deposits and is deliberately read only as a nonzero flag -- that is why.)  Scaling each BUNDLE's deposits by `N / swl.NumActive()` at the source is exact, is bit-identical whenever nothing terminated, and is strictly FINER-grained than any per-pixel scheme, because termination is a per-bundle property and the spatial correlation the row is about disappears once each bundle is renormalized before pooling.  Balance statistic 0.2661 -> ~1.01 (BDPT), 0.2984 -> ~1.01 and 0.6307 -> ~1.01 (VCM, the second at an ORDINARY eye depth), both flat-slab CONTROL rows green in both builds.  (2) **DL-200's predicted mechanism is worth exactly ZERO.**  The configuration WAS reached, on the first scene variant, and instrumented (`LIGHT-rooted enclosed medium verts conn=4683400/nonconn=2132904` vs `EYE-rooted conn=0/nonconn=2197533`; MEDIUM-endpoint connection attempts `light-side ok=27474287` vs `eye-side ok=0 skipped=32085651`) -- but the row's own prescribed rule (derive connectibility from the enclosure BOUNDARY's material, symmetrically) leaves the image BIT-IDENTICAL to the pre-fix baseline, so the ASYMMETRY costs nothing.  The real defect is that `isConnectible` -- a statement about the vertex's own scattering function, and a phase function is never a delta -- was carrying a VISIBILITY heuristic, and visibility is a property of the PAIR of endpoints: two points inside the SAME enclosure are connectible, which is exactly the light-inside-a-fog-filled-shell case whose NEE was being dropped.  BDPT/PT 0.90338 -> 1.00356 (RGB) and 0.79556 -> 0.98217 (spectral), with BDPT's own sd falling 2.94% -> 0.37%.  (3) **The sibling audit found a bigger bug than the row it was auditing.**  DL-217: the spectral splat deposit never applied `mYNormalization`, so `vcm_spectral_rasterizer` rendered at **45-68% of its own Pel twin** on ordinary scenes, not just caustic ones -- larger than DL-201 by an order of magnitude and at the identical three lines.  A methodological note that cost a round: the "no glass" row of DL-217's test was DRAFTED as a control on the assumption that removing the glass would collapse VCM's splat share the way it does under BDPT; it measured 0.6805 pre-fix, so it is a third money row and the label was corrected rather than the row dropped (a genuine control, BDPT with no glass at 1.0094/1.0122, replaced it).  New tests: `tests/SpectralSplatActiveCountTest.cpp` 30/0 (red 24/6), `tests/SpectralSplatIntegralNormalizationTest.cpp` 12/0 (red 12/3), `tests/MediumEnclosureConnectibilityTest.cpp` 12/0 (red 10/2).  Gate, all green: MISWeightsTest 59/0, ConnectionLegalityTest 319/0, BDPTVertexRIGRebuildTest 68/0, VolumeAbsorptionAttenuationTest 89/0, VolumeEnvFurnaceTest 32/0, BDPTStrategyBalanceTest 123/0, VCMStrategyBalanceTest 74/0, MLTSpectralHWSSNormalizationTest 8/0, EnvLightBalanceTest 123/0, CstDeriveGoldenTest 452 MATCH/0 DRIFT, SourceHygieneTest 167/0.  `EnvLightBalanceTest` needed NO band re-derivation but its SPECTRAL VCM rows moved by up to +4.7% under DL-217 (its RGB rows are unchanged to 4-5 significant figures) -- recorded here because the DL-201 brief asked for exactly that disclosure. **2026-09-18 (debt-dl126 slice, branched from `master` `3299cd78`):** DL-126
CLOSED — `BDPTIntegrator.cpp`'s eye/light subpath generators now continue a
null-BSDF (`biospec_skin_material`/`generic_human_tissue_material`)
non-delta scatter via the delta branch's own `kray/selectProb` formula, and
`MISWeight`'s two ratio walks skip such a vertex (`isDelta || !isConnectible`)
so no phantom connection strategy is reserved MIS mass for it.  A SECOND,
independent instance of the identical pattern was found by testing in
`VCMIntegrator.cpp`'s `ConvertLightSubpath`/`ConvertEyeSubpath` (their
outgoing-update branch selection also needed `|| !v.isConnectible`) —
without it VCM read 1.6% of PT on the red-proof scene even with the
generator fix alone; with it, 98.4%.  That file was outside this slice's
originally-scoped touch set but was fixed anyway (same audited pattern,
and this row's own success bar required it); see the row and
docs/DL126_BDPT_NULL_BSDF_CONTINUATION.md for the full account and the
explicit scope-deviation note.  `BDPTStrategyBalanceTest` 99/0 (new
topology N), `VCMStrategyBalanceTest` 74/0 (new topology J), plus the full
gate list in the row/doc.  **DL-183** opened (S, physics-bias) — a
distinct, unrelated bug found while building the red-proof scene:
`GenericHumanTissueSPF::Scatter`/`ScatterNM` set the scattered ray's
origin to `ri.ray.origin` instead of `ri.ptIntersection` (its sibling
`BioSpecSkinSPF` does this correctly); NOT fixed here (different bug
pattern, and this slice worked around it by using `biospec_skin_material`
for the red-proof instead).  This slice's own snapshot: 1 row closed
(DL-126), 1 opened (DL-183).  This slice's branch HEAD does not update
the "Authoritative totals" line below; that recount happens at merge.

Four things in the row's own text turned out to be wrong and are
corrected there and in the struck row: the 14-site enumeration was both
incomplete AND arithmetically wrong (its own seven numbered items sum to
15 call sites, not 14 — item (6), the BSSRDF gate, is six call sites on
its own; and `Object.cpp`'s override-UV-generator argument is a
sixteenth, not a fifteenth), the "adjacent hazard ... no sign error
today" verdict was wrong (`ManifoldSolver::BuildSeedChain`'s `bEntering`
reads the stored normal's sign, and its IOR-stack workaround cannot fire
for a walk that starts inside the solid), `BDPTVertex::geomNormal` must
NOT be un-flipped the way `ManifoldVertex::geomNormal` now is (its
emitter rows need the ray-facing value), and a full re-grep during this
slice's review fix-pass found **281** non-comment `vGeomNormal` lines
outside `RayIntersectionGeometric.h` (226 reads), not the 200 this row
originally quoted. DL-95 is independent of the DL-70 pattern — an
ordering defect in `Object::IntersectRay` — and was found while
red-proofing the UV site.

**2026-09-14 (debt-guiding2 slice, branched from `ddf05c6c`, the 71/48/23
baseline directly above — verified by an independent recount of the table:
71 non-divider rows before this slice's edits, matching the baseline
exactly).  Figures below are this slice's state AFTER review round 2,
which supersedes the round-1 wording that used to stand here:**
DL-66 CLOSED (test-only fixture repair, red-proved on this exact HEAD:
868/1 before, 884/0 after).  DL-72 CLOSED — round 3 wired the
moment/count pairing correctly, round 4 corrected its red-proof claim
(the row had cited a hand-driven accumulator test that executes no line
of either production file; the real red-proof is the new
`OptimalMISTrainingSitesTest`, red at clampMin/clampMax with the two
files reverted to `ddf05c6c`, 7/0 wired) and fixed P2-5, the volume
moment being divided by the raw phase pdf rather than the density the
direction was drawn from.  DL-74 CLOSED **for every guiding mode**, not
just the default one — round 1's narrower repair (an env-arm-only,
one-sample-only blend on the base alpha) did not close the partition and
had no test that could have seen that; round 2 replaced it with the
two-roles split (`RAY_STATE::bsdfPdf` = true sampling density,
`RAY_STATE::bsdfMisPdf` = one lobe-independent nominal MIS-partner
density evaluated identically on every side) and red-proved it with the
new `PTGuidingMISPartitionTest` (4 of 6 rows red on `8fcce0bf` by −66 %
to +63 %; 22/0 after).  **DL-83** was filed in round 1 for the RIS-mode
residual and is CLOSED in round 2 by that same repair: the nominal
density does not depend on the guiding mode, so the premise DL-83 was
filed on (that the NEE side must reproduce RIS's sampling density) was
itself wrong.  Main rows: 71 -> **72** (DL-83 added, then closed).
OPEN-confirmed: 48 -> **45** (DL-66, DL-72, DL-74 closed; DL-83 opened
and closed within the slice).  CLOSED-by-later-slice (this slice): **4**
(DL-66, DL-72, DL-74, DL-83).  Independently re-verified by direct
recount of the table this pass: 72 non-divider `| DL-` / `| ~~DL-` rows,
45 containing "OPEN-confirmed", 27 starting `| ~~DL-` (struck/closed) —
45 + 27 = 72, consistent.

**Review round 3 (same slice, same day) closed no row; it opened DL-84 (see
below, main rows 72 -> 73).**  Its two other findings were defects IN this slice's own
round-2 work on rows that were already struck, so they were fixed under
those rows rather than filed.  (1) A REGRESSION round 2 introduced — the
`bsdfPdf`/`bsdfMisPdf` split was not carried across the shader-op
boundary, so `PathTracingShaderOp` and `EmissionShaderOp` weighted with
the guided density against an NEE arm that used the raw one: **+44.1 %**
on a global medium + trained volume guiding + an area emitter.  (2) A
residual round 2 had dismissed in writing as "pre-existing and
orthogonal" — the NEE arms evaluating the MIS-partner aggregate pdf
under an `IORStack(1.0)` sentinel while the escape side uses the live
stack: **−14.0 %** on an albedo-1 furnace inside a dielectric, with
guiding OFF as much as ON.  Both are red-proved in
`PTGuidingMISPartitionTest`, now **37/0** (34/3 on the round-2 HEAD
`2ebcaff9`).  Round 3 DID open one NEW row: **DL-84**, the third
volume continuation (`PathTracingIntegrator`'s own in-loop one, reached
only after a surface bounce), whose optimal-MIS training DL-72 never
covered.  Its MIS-PARTNER half was a live bias and is fixed under DL-74
(the stale partner cost **+9.6 %** on VolumeEnvFurnaceTest's
floor-in-fog furnace, 29/0 before the slice -> 26/3 after round 2 ->
29/0 again now); the TRAINING half is left conservative (numerator
cleared to zero rather than carrying the previous vertex's) and filed.
Recount after round 3: main rows 72 -> **73**, OPEN-confirmed 45 ->
**46**, struck 27 — 46 + 27 = 73, consistent.

**Review round 4 (same slice, same day) changed no count either.**  It
found no P1.  Its one live defect was again in this slice's own work on
an already-struck row — rounds 2-3 treated a zero material AGGREGATE pdf
as proof that the BSDF technique cannot reach a direction, which stops
being true the moment a GUIDE is mixed in, so both MIS halves took weight
1 over that region: **+100.4 %** with an area emitter placed entirely
inside a partial-support lobe's blind spot, **+10.6 %** (converging on a
closed-form 14.6 %) on a whole-hemisphere env furnace.  Fixed under
DL-74; `PTGuidingMISPartitionTest` **63/0** (61/2 before).  A second
finding corrected the trained numerator at both halves of the BSSRDF
optimal-MIS pair, fixed under DL-72 (`OptimalMISTrainingSitesTest`
**23/0**, was 21/2 — both pre-fix ratios EXACTLY 1 where the derivation
says 4).  Round 4 opened NO row.  In particular it did **not** file the
`VolumeAbsorptionAttenuationTest` flake it was asked to consider: 40
measured renders of the failing row show it unbiased (mean within 0.6 %
of the closed form) with a 3.13 % run-to-run sd against an 8 % gate —
a 2.6-sigma test failing ~1 % of runs, i.e. a fixture that is
under-averaged for its own tolerance, not an estimator or transport
defect.  Row [R] is now repeat-averaged over 4 independently seeded
renders (sd 2.09 %, worst of 36 loaded runs +4.83 %).  Recount after
round 4: main rows **73**, OPEN-confirmed **46**, struck 27 —
46 + 27 = 73, unchanged.

**2026-09-14 (debt-ggx2 slice, review-findings follow-up, base `8b298537`):**
review of the DL-63/DL-65 closures found four P2s and several P3s (no P1s);
this pass fixes all of them. (1) `GGXHeightCorrelatedEnergyLUTTest.cpp`'s
`TestKullaContyIdentityHoldsForG2` was TAUTOLOGICAL at Schlick F0=1
(`ComputeFms(1, Eavg)` collapses to exactly 1 by pure algebra for any
`Eavg`, so its `total==1` check passed for ANY table content) — replaced
with `TestEssEavgConsistencyG2` (re-derives each `E_avg_TABLE_G2` row from
the checked-in `E_ss_TABLE_G2` values via the generator's own midpoint-rule
discretization; matches to ~2-5e-9) and `TestFurnaceStyleAtF0Point9`
(F0=0.9, where `F_ms` genuinely depends on `Eavg`; asserts the provable
`0 <= total <= F0` bound plus `F_ms < 1`). (2) Added
`TestUniformHemisphereIndependentQuadrature`: a third estimator with its
own re-implementation of GGX `D`/Smith `Lambda`/height-correlated `G2`,
its own RNG, and UNIFORM-hemisphere (not VNDF) sampling, closing the gap
that the existing quadrature and the offline generator share one VNDF
identity; matches `LookupEssG2` at the three reviewer-specified
`(alpha, mu)` points (e.g. alpha=1.0/mu=0.0156: `LookupEssG2=0.93494` vs
`uniformHemisphere=0.93486+/-0.00002`). `GGXHeightCorrelatedEnergyLUTTest:
23 checks, 0 failures` (was `14 checks, 0 failures`). (3) **New debt
opened: DL-77** (isotropic-LUT-vs-anisotropic-render mismatch in
`GGXBRDF`/`GGXSPF`'s DL-63 compensation; see the table row above) —

their Schlick half-vector lobe unconditionally rather than through a

row was opened for DL-64's pattern.

## Verification recipes

Recipes are retained after closure as regression specifications. The main
table and Counts section above define current status; a retained recipe
does not reopen a closed item.

Concrete "how to know it's fixed" for each DL-xx row above.

**~~DL-01 (TranslucentSPF RGB/NM exit weight divergence).~~ CLOSED
2026-09-12 — `1239edf2`, `TranslucentSpectralParityTest: 676 checks, 0 failures`.**
The test committed as `fc371041` failed with 174 failures against the
unfixed library from `6486656e`. Both pipes now split Beer extinction
between exit and backscatter, with primary-layer tau paid on entry only.
It covers 450/550/650 nm, uniform/per-channel N, tau and scattering
endpoints, and chained spectral entry/exit. The absolute weight tolerance
is explicitly `kWeightTolerance = 1e-3`; the old recipe's claim that
`TranslucentIORStackTest` already had a `CROSS_VAL_TOL` was incorrect.
The scene parameter is `tau`, not the old recipe's `transmittance`.
Diffuse exit-direction/Pdf consistency was subsequently closed by DL-02;
full mixture density remains DL-41 and integrator/HWSS amplitudes DL-38. See [closure and audit](DL01_TRANSLUCENT_EXIT_WEIGHT.md).

**~~DL-02 (TranslucentSPF exit-density support and spectral shape).~~ CLOSED
2026-09-12 — `a041e51d`, `TranslucentSpectralParityTest: 1918 checks, 0 failures`.**
Test `adc9a286` failed 324 checks against the unfixed `cb9dd0f4` library.
RGB/NM exit support, stored/evaluated cosine density, fixed-variate inverse
CDF and separate hemisphere integrals now agree at N=1/8 and per-channel
N=5/10/15. Entry geometric-horizon controls still pass. This closes the
conditional diffuse exit only; full mixture/reverse densities and NEE
state are DL-41, BSDF amplitudes are DL-38, and guiding stack propagation
was subsequently closed by DL-03. See [closure and audit](DL02_TRANSLUCENT_EXIT_DENSITY.md).

**~~DL-03 (TranslucentSPF guided-direction IOR-stack leak).~~ CLOSED
2026-09-12 — `8a9bdb18`, `TranslucentIORStackTest: ALL TESTS PASSED`.**
The trained real-PT regression was red on unfixed `00bdcef5` with four
outward-stack failures. Explicit guide-eligible diffuse arrival avoids
ordinary PT translucent specular-arrival suppression; positive actual
outward substitution counts are required in both guiding modes and both
RGB/NM. The later same-object Scatter verifies entry classification and
popped membership, while inward substitutions retain inside state. Added
BDPT eye/light RGB/NM real-entry/exit coverage passes too; eye RIS retains
SPF directions because DL-43 remains open, so it is not claimed as actual
outward-guide coverage. See [closure and audit](DL03_GUIDED_IOR_CONTINUATION.md).

**~~DL-04 (SSS family eta^2 direction).~~ CLOSED 2026-09-12 —
consistency pin, `1b705ce1`.** The original ratio-of-ratios alone cannot
identify a constant multiplying every SSS event, and the proposed planar
fixture exposes a separate missing-support defect. The revised test retains
the matched explicit-volume/diffusion/RW camera matrix, anchors each raw
observer ratio to 1.33 squared, and adds independent helper and absolute
conservative-furnace bounds. Both deliberately added eta-square directions
fail six SSS air-channel checks; a missing observer transform fails nine.
The unchanged implementation passes. This closes the convention question,
not exact material equality within MC noise: fixed pixel Sobol scrambles
are not independent repetitions. Of the DL-48 through DL-53 range this
sentence originally pointed at, only **DL-49** (non-air relative-index)
remains open — DL-48 (normalization), DL-50/51 (survival/support), and
DL-52/53 (probe reach, recursive-MIS) are all CLOSED; see
[the complete decision record](DL04_SSS_RADIANCE_DECISION.md).

**DL-05 (PT two-layer weave gap, debt 27).** Extend
`tests/BDPTStrategyBalanceTest.cpp` with a topology pairing two
`weave_material transmission thin` layers with a light OUTSIDE both (the
doc's own box/plane fixtures at gap 0.1/0.3). Expected today: PT/BDPT ~=
0.65-0.78 (i.e. BDPT/PT 1.28-1.55x). Fixed when a `HasNonBendingDeltaLobe()`
material query lets `CastShadowRayTransmittance` (or an equivalent walk)
see through the far layer's delta gap for PT's NEE, closing the row to the
suite's standard 8% band, with an added-energy suppression against PT's
own BSDF-sampled delta continuation for AREA lights (to avoid a new
double-count).

**DL-06 (VCM env+mesh SA-MIS partition).** Re-run `EnvLightBalanceTest` at
its strict tolerances (`{0.10, 0.30, 1.00}`); currently 78/80. Fixed when
the monolithic SA-MIS migration (both subpath sides, the shared generator's
env-vertex pdf convention, the 6 BDPT rows, OpenPGL) lands and this reaches
80/80 strict without regressing hwss=true below ~0.82 (its pre-existing,
separately-tracked bias floor).

**~~DL-07 (OrenNayarBRDF hemisphericalAlbedo bias)~~ CLOSED 2026-09-14 —
see the table row above.** Original recipe kept for the historical
record: add a `tests/OrenNayarHemisphericalAlbedoTest.cpp` comparing
`OrenNayarBRDF::hemisphericalAlbedo()`'s closed form against a brute-force
hemispherical quadrature at roughness in {0, 0.3, 0.5, 1.0}. Expected then:
0% / ~5% / ~12.6% / ~25.6% over. Fixed when a corrected estimator (or a
small baked correction table) brings all four under a stated tolerance
(e.g. 2%), then re-measure `fabric_material`/`coated_material`'s furnace
configs that depend on it (cloth furnace config using Oren-Nayar substrate;
`WETNESS_COAT_DESIGN.md`'s coated-Oren-Nayar furnace row). Landed as a
64-node baked `A1(sigma)/A2(sigma)` bihemispherical-integral table
instead of a correction factor on `Rd` — residual measured under 0.02%,
well past the 2% bar this recipe asked for.

**DL-123 (GGXBRDF hemisphericalAlbedo grazing over-estimate).** Add a
`tests/GGXHemisphericalAlbedoTest.cpp` comparing
`GGXBRDF::hemisphericalAlbedo{,NM}`'s `mean + diffuse*Transmission(mean,mean)`
estimator against an independent brute-force bihemispherical double
integral of the real `GGXBRDF::value{,NM}`, at alpha in {0.05, 0.2, 0.5,
1.0} and F0 in {0.04, 0.5}, matching `tests/OrenNayarHemisphericalAlbedoTest.cpp`'s
pattern for DL-07. Expected today (from `FabricMaterialChunkTest` gate
5(b)'s "substr%" column, alpha=0.5 F0=0.04): ~7.7% high. Fixed when the
estimator (or a baked correction) brings the measured error under a
stated tolerance (e.g. 2%, matching DL-07's bar), then re-measure
`fabric_material`/`coated_material`'s furnace configs that depend on it
the same way DL-07's fix was re-measured.

**~~DL-08 (PSSMLT stream aliasing)~~ CLOSED 2026-09-17 — `d7ebd453`,
`tests/PSSMLTStreamAliasingTest.cpp` Test F.** Original recipe kept for
the historical record: add a case to `tests/PSSMLTStreamAliasingTest.cpp`
that drives the eye walk to depth >= 32 under `PSSMLTSampler` and asserts
that stream indices used by the eye walk never collide with the MLT
film/lens/aperture lanes (e.g. by instrumenting `PSSMLTSampler::StartStream`
to log/assert on `streamIndex >= kNumStreams` distinctness). Landed as a
direct sampler-arithmetic proof (two identically-seeded `PSSMLTSampler`
instances, one on `StartStream(16+32)`, one on the historical literal
`StartStream(48)`, produced bit-identical draws pre-fix) rather than a
rendered-scene comparison — no render was needed since the collision is
provable at the `(stream, sampleIndex) -> idx` arithmetic layer,
independent of what BDPT/MLTRasterizer do with the returned values.
Fixed by raising `kNumStreams` (49 -> 4096) and moving MLT's reserved
stream off the literal 48 to `BDPTCameraUtilities::kPSSMLTFilmLensApertureStream`
(2048, strictly above the new `kMaxBdptWalkStreamUnderPSSMLT` == 1040)
— see [DL08_PSSMLT_LANE_LAYOUT.md](DL08_PSSMLT_LANE_LAYOUT.md).

**DL-09 (RadianceEtaScale spatially-varying ior — interior gather AND
DielectricSPF exit-hit Snell trace).** `tests/RadianceEtaScaleGradedIndexTest.cpp`
now exists and pins the through-slab net at exactly 1 (no eta factor) —
that file's two rows are pure through-transmission and pass today, by
construction, whether or not any fix has landed; they are a REGRESSION
GUARD on the reverted round-1 bug, not a red-proof of the open residual.
The still-open recipe: extend that graded slab with a diffuse gather
target INSIDE the object (or lower `scattering` so the walk scatters
non-deltaically at an interior vertex, restricting the `ior` painter to
a world-position-only `expression` — a UV-driven form has no defined
value at an interior point, see the row's evidence), and compare against
a piecewise-uniform reference built as N concentric shells of CONSTANT
`ior` stepping `n_A -> n_B`, where every shell boundary is a real
interface and RISE's existing per-crossing factor is correct by
construction; the graded render must converge to the multi-shell one as
N grows. Fixed when (a) an interior-segment factor `(n_prev/n_C)^2` is
applied along the walk AND (b) `RadianceEtaScale`'s exit-crossing read
switches from the stale `before.top()` to the SPF's freshly re-fetched
value — BOTH changes, landed together: either alone fails the new
interior-gather test (in opposite directions — see the row's evidence
for why landing only (a) is a mirror-image regression of the reverted
round-1 bug, not a fix) even though either alone still passes the
existing through-slab pin. Also fixed in the same pass: `DielectricSPF`'s
exit-hit `CalculateRefractedRay` call, whose direction/TIR classification
uses the fresh exit-hit `ior` value against a walk that (pre-fix) still
tracks the entry-hit value — make it consistent with whatever medium the
walk's corrected model tracks at that point instead.

**~~DL-10 (fisheye pixelAR Jacobian)~~ CLOSED 2026-09-14 — `bf74d3ab`/`b083af79`,
`CameraImportanceTest` 968/0.** Kept as the regression spec. Author a
fisheye-camera scene with `pixelAR != 1` and a uniform-radiance sphere
filling the frame (the same fixture
`tests/CameraImportanceTest.cpp::TestFisheyeFilmResponse` uses at
`pixelAR = 1`, where it integrates to 1.000166). Expected: at `pixelAR !=
1` the closed-form integral departs from 1 by more than measurement noise
(red: 966/2, 0.554694 at pixelAR=0.5, 1.511822 at pixelAR=2.0). Fixed when
`Importance`'s per-pixel solid-angle term folds in the full Jacobian of
`normalize ∘ Stretch(pixelAR,1,1)` and the extended test converges to 1 at
several `pixelAR` values (post-fix: 0.999988 / 1.000166 / 0.999972 at
pixelAR ∈ {0.5, 1.0, 2.0}). See the table row above for the fix and
sibling audit.

**~~DL-11 (sheen middle-band residual)~~ CLOSED 2026-09-14 — see the

re-run `tests/SheenDirectionalAlbedoTest.cpp`'s domination-slack check
at the current 64x64 table; then-current middle band (n·v in
[kMinSheenAlpha, 0.0349)) read +0.75% over 1. "Fixed when a root-caused
correction near the roughness floor (α ≈ 0.065) brings that band under
the same ~0.2% bar the other two bands already clear, without another
blind resolution doubling" — landed as a log(mu) interpolation blend
(not a resolution change) bringing the band to +0.45%, with the
remaining residual PROVEN model-inherent (an independent table-free
cross-check reads +0.54% at the same point) rather than a further
table/interpolation defect — so the ~0.2% bar this recipe asked for is
not reachable by any table over this energy-compensation model, and the
row closes on that honestly-measured floor instead.

(not all-zero) on a `BDPTVertex` in a scene using footprint-driven texture
filtering. Fixed when ray differentials are computed and threaded through
the BDPT/VCM/MLT eye-subpath walk (mirroring the S1 `derivatives`/`signals`
widening already done for the same struct) and the PT-vs-BDPT filtered-band
difference shrinks to noise on a control scene.

**~~DL-15 (eccentric ellipsoid proximity bound).~~ CLOSED 2026-09-14 —
`0933d317`, `tests/EllipsoidProximityBoundTest.cpp` 37/0 (was 33/4).**
`EllipsoidGeometry::DistanceToSurface` now solves the classic
Lagrange-multiplier construction for the near-exact nearest ellipsoid
point by safeguarded Newton (`RefineExteriorDistanceToEllipsoid`,
`EllipsoidGeometry.cpp`), then rescales the candidate to land marginally
OUTSIDE the true surface before measuring — which is what makes the
answer a valid upper bound REGARDLESS of how well Newton converged: any
point outside (or on) the ellipsoid is at least as far from the query as
the true nearest surface point, by definition. The old scaled-sphere
bound is kept as a fallback and safety net (`DistanceToSurface` takes the
MINIMUM of the two), so a refinement failure can only ever leave the
pre-fix behaviour in place, never regress past it. Red-proof reproduced
the doc's own cited numbers exactly (a 4:1 ellipsoid at true distance
2.75 reporting 11.0) and closed them to near-exact: `4:1 waist: true
2.75, old-formula bound 11, reported 2.75`; an off-axis point against an
independent coordinate-descent oracle went from a 2.26x over-report
(`old-formula bound 3.81025`) to `new reported 1.68274` against
`independent-oracle true 1.68274`. `SignedDistanceLower` (the LOWER-bound
magnitude `interior(r)` is built on) is untouched — it needs a bound in
the opposite direction and Phase 3 already made its sign exact, so this
row does not extend to it.

red-proof independently reproduces `ChunkParser:: Failed to parse
parameter name \`tangent_rotation_scalar\` (not declared in
\`ggx_material\` descriptor)`).** Added a new `tangent_rotation_scalar`
